\section{Policy}
This ledger tracks arXiv:2207.14599v1 statement by statement.  We distinguish
false printed statements, type/domain errors, local repairs, missing arguments,
and statements actually validated in Lean.  A corrected statement is never
silently substituted for the printed one: the repair is recorded here first.

\section{Detailed issues}

\subsection{The auxiliary node order}
The paper orders shorter nodes first and uses reverse lexicographic order on
ties.  For
\[
S=\{\varnothing,0,10\},
\]
the canonical tree/lexicographic map from $2^{<2}$ sends
$0\mapsto0$ and $1\mapsto10$.  In the source the printed tie-break has
$1\preccurlyeq0$, while in the target the length clause gives
$0\prec10$.  Thus the canonical map cannot preserve the printed order.

\paragraph{Lean.} \ok
\lean{DualTree.OrderAudit.canonical_order_counterexample}.
The local forward-lex repair is checked by
\lean{DualTree.OrderAudit.forward_order_repairs_counterexample}.

\paragraph{Proposed repair.}
Use forward lexicographic order among nodes of equal length.  Exhaustive checks
found zero failures for the canonical-order and interior tests in the tested
binary and ternary ranges.  This does not by itself repair Remark 1.

\subsection{Remark 2}
With the printed order,
\[
S=\{\varnothing,0,1,10,11\}
\]
gives a semi-complete example whose interior
$\{\varnothing,1\}$ violates the one-successor direction convention.
The statement should be reproved after the order convention is corrected.

\paragraph{Lean status.}
The concrete witness is certified by the executable Section 3 checker:
\lean{DualTree.BinarySkewAudit.remark2_witness_is_semicomplete_printed} and
\lean{DualTree.BinarySkewAudit.remark2_interior_not_skew_printed}.  Equivalence
of this executable checker with the final propositional skew-tree definition
is still \todo.

\subsection{Remark 1}
Two independent problems were found.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The condition $m\le n-|s|$ permits concatenations reaching level $n$,
outside $b^{<n}$.  The evident safe bound is $m\le n-|s|-1$, modulo the
authors' intended indexing convention.
\item The claimed extension of every skew $T'\subseteq T$ to a complete skew
subtree of the spliced tree has a finite counterexample even with forward lex.
This needs a new statement, not just an index correction.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Lean status.}
\ok The substantive forward-lex failure is now certified in
\lean{DualTree.Remark1Audit.no_forward_complete_extension}.  The chosen
spliced tree and the prescribed intersection tree are separately certified
skew by \lean{DualTree.Remark1Audit.forwardS_is_skew} and
\lean{DualTree.Remark1Audit.forwardTprime_is_skew}.  The remaining task for
this item is not verification of the printed statement---it is false---but
identifying the intended replacement needed downstream.

\subsection{Span inclusion and Theorem 3 for one-letter alphabets}
The paper defines refinement using
\[
[g]_{\Lambda}\subseteq[f]_{\Lambda}.
\]
If $|\Lambda|=1$, every variable word evaluates to the same constant word.
For $b=2$, $n=2$, $k=m=1$, coloring by whether the unique variable root is
$\varnothing$ gives two colors, while every one-variable word is a refinement
of every candidate in the printed sense.  Hence Theorem 3 is false literally
for $\ell=1$.

\paragraph{Repair and Lean status.}
\ok The algebraic and tree-specific issues are now separated and both are
formalized.  The singleton-alphabet collapse is
\lean{DualTree.SpanAudit.singleton_span_subset}.  The concrete binary-tree
witnesses are \lean{DualTree.Theorem3Audit.rootWord} and
\lean{DualTree.Theorem3Audit.leftWord}; their singleton supports are
1-complete skew trees, their supports differ, and
\lean{DualTree.Theorem3Audit.unary_spans_equal} proves their evaluated spans
are identical.  Finally,
\lean{DualTree.Theorem3Audit.two_colors_below_every_candidate} certifies the
finite witness, while
\lean{DualTree.UnaryMainAudit.no_unary_eventual_bound} strengthens this to
the actual threshold-level failure: for every binary ambient height $n\ge2$,
a fixed two-coloring has two differently colored $1$-complete refinements
below every candidate.  Hence no eventual Ramsey threshold exists for the
literal $\ell=1$, $k=m=1$ statement.

Conversely, if the alphabet contains two distinct letters and every source
variable occurs, then
\lean{DualTree.SpanAudit.span_subset_iff_substitution} proves that evaluated
span inclusion is equivalent to uniform syntactic substitution.  For the
tree-variable-word structure,
\lean{DualTree.VariableWord.support_subset_of_span_subset} further proves
that this refinement preserves variable roots/support.

Thus the span-based formulation is sound for $|\Lambda|\ge2$.  For
$|\Lambda|=1$ the literal Theorem~3 is false; defining refinement
syntactically instead would remove this degeneracy without excluding unary
alphabets.

\subsection{Corollary 22}
The rendered PDF is essential here because the text extractor repeatedly drops
equality bars.  Definition~12 defines
\operatorname{MTHJ} as the least positive $n_0$ for which the mixed-product
conclusion holds for every $n\ge n_0$, Theorem~10 likewise uses
$n\ge n_0$ in the definition of \operatorname{CT}, and Corollary~22 assumes
\[
d_0+d_1+d_2\ge1
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
n\ge
\operatorname{MTHJ}\bigl(d_0,d_1,d_2,b,\ell,
\operatorname{CT}(1,k,b,d_2,r),r\bigr).
\]
Thus the height-one obstruction below is not an artefact of a strict/non-strict
threshold mismatch; it is a genuine counterexample to the displayed
Corollary~22.

Take
\[
d_0=d_1=0,\quad d_2=1,\quad b=\ell=r=2,\quad k=n=1,
\]
and color $(w,\varnothing)$ by $w(\varnothing)$.  The marked point is forced
to be the root, so the coloring is smooth with $\Gamma_1=D_2$.  Every genuine
one-dimensional candidate has both colors in its starred span.  At the same
time the canonical height-one subspace is $c$-good for every coloring, so the
relevant mixed-product threshold is $1$; and
$\operatorname{CT}(1,1,2,1,r)=1$ is immediate because the ambient vector tree
at height one has only the root singleton.  Hence the hypotheses of the
printed corollary really do include this bad case.

\paragraph{Lean status.} \ok
The height-one mechanism is source-facing:
\lean{DualTree.Corollary22Audit.color_is_smooth} proves the specialized
Definition~21 smoothness condition, and
\lean{DualTree.Corollary22Audit.no_monochromatic_complete_candidate} proves
that no complete one-variable candidate has monochromatic starred span.
Moreover,
\lean{DualTree.Corollary22Audit.canonical_good_for_every_coloring}
proves that at ambient height one the canonical one-dimensional subspace is
$c$-good for every coloring, the local fact behind
$\operatorname{MTHJ}(0,0,1,2,2,1,r)=1$.  The equally trivial CT identity has
not yet been wrapped in the general vector-CT API in Lean.

\paragraph{Likely repair.}
The obstruction identifies the missing resource in the usual proof:
$c$-goodness at one marked root can still leave dependence on the letter at
that root.  With two distinct variable roots one can keep the underlying word
fixed while moving the marked point, and combine smoothness with $c$-goodness
to eliminate this last dependence.  A natural corrected intermediate
dimension is therefore
\[
H=\max\{2,\operatorname{CT}(1,k,b,d_2,r)\},
\]
followed by
$\operatorname{MTHJ}(d_0,d_1,d_2,b,\ell,H,r)$.
This is consistent with the exhaustive binary height-two checks from the
pre-Lean audit.  The general corrected proof remains \todo.

There is a second formal boundary issue: Corollary~22 permits $d_2=0$ but
writes $\operatorname{CT}(1,k,b,d_2,r)$, while Theorem~10 assumes positive
vector dimension $d$.  The case $d_2=0$ should be split off or CT should be
extended by convention.

\subsection{The comparison after Theorem 25}
The displayed estimate
\[
\operatorname{PTGR}(l,m,b,r)
\le \operatorname{TGR}(k_l+1,m,b,\ell,r)
\]
fails already for $b=\ell=r=2$, $l=1$, $m=2$.  The right side is $2$, while a
simple coloring at ambient height two defeats the sole height-two candidate.
The qualitative reduction may survive with different parameters.

The notation $\operatorname{PTGR}(l,m,b,r)$ also suppresses the fixed alphabet
size $\ell$, although Lemmas 27 and 28 carry $\ell$ explicitly.  We should use
$\operatorname{PTGR}(l,m,b,\ell,r)$ in the formal development.

\subsection{Definition 24}
$I_S$ was defined for complete skew trees, but Definition 24 applies it to a
semi-complete tree.  The intended canonical transport should be defined
explicitly rather than overloaded.

\subsection{Proposition 15: direction alphabet in the Hales--Jewett lift}
The rendered page~16 contains a genuine type error in the construction after
the Hales--Jewett application.  For each $i\in D_1$, the paper defines
$w_T^i(v_0,\ldots,v_{k-1})$ to be a $k$-dimensional variable word over the
direction alphabet
\[
\{0,\ldots,b-1\}.
\]
However, the subsequent definition of $S_i$ evaluates $w_T^i$ at
$a_0,\ldots,a_{k-1}\in\Lambda$, and the displayed description of $A_i$ repeats
the same substitution.  These expressions are not typed unless
$\Lambda=\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$.

The repair is local: in both displays the parameters used to evaluate
$w_T^i$ must range over the direction alphabet.  For example,
\[
S_i=
\bigl\{
 I_{T_i}\bigl(w_T^i(j_0,\ldots,j_{k-1})\upharpoonright \ell_p^w\bigr)
 :
 j_0,\ldots,j_{k-1}\in\{0,\ldots,b-1\},\ p<k
\bigr\}
\]
together with the corresponding terminal-level points, and the analogous
formula for $A_i$.  The $\Lambda$-valued word $w_\Lambda^i$ continues to be
evaluated with alphabet letters as printed.

This appears to be a notational substitution error rather than a defect in the
Hales--Jewett reduction: after the correction, $w_T^i$ records branch
directions while $w_\Lambda^i$ records letters, exactly as required by the
operator $Q$.

\paragraph{Lean progress.}
The supporting root-trace lemma
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.prefix_values_eq_of_root_values_eq}
shows that for two evaluations of the same tree-variable word, agreement at
the variable roots along a chosen leaf implies agreement along the entire
predecessor chain.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.sameUpTrace_iff_supportTraceRecord_eq_of_mem_span}
transfers this statement to the mixed-product span, retaining the chosen
$D_1$ leaves.  Finally,
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.upCanonical_iff_same_supportTraceRecord}
formalizes factorization of a Lemma~14-canonical coloring through these
variable-root records.

This is the algebraic interface underlying Proposition~15's $Q$.
The identification of the root records with a word of length $M$ over the
alphabet $(\Lambda\times\{0,\ldots,b-1\})^{D_1}$, surjectivity of $Q$,
and the Hales--Jewett construction of the refined skew subtrees remain
\todo.

\subsection{Lemma 16 and Claim 17}
The rendered source confirms that Corollary~9 is available at
$N\ge mq$.  The problem in Lemma~16 is therefore not strictness of that
inequality but the interpretation of the recursively defined $q_p$ as a total
block length.

For Claim~18, a type-correct cumulative recursion is obtained by adjoining
$q_{m+1}=1$ and setting, for $p=m,\ldots,1$,
\[
q_p
  =q_{p+1}
    Q\bigl(\ell,d_2^p,d_2q_{p+1},r\bigr).
\]
Then the stage-$p$ ambient length $d_2q_p$ is exactly
\[
(d_2q_{p+1})
Q\bigl(\ell,d_2^p,d_2q_{p+1},r\bigr),
\]
which is the amount required by Corollary~9 to produce
$d_2q_{p+1}$ blocks.  The displayed recursion in the paper omits the factor
$q_{p+1}$.  The same cumulative factor must be inserted in the starred
majorant recursion defining $q_p^*$.

Claim~17 has the analogous one-step indexing shift.  The clean formulation is
to adjoin
\[
M_{m+1}=1
\]
and, for $p=m,\ldots,1$, define $M_p$ by the paper's displayed MTHJ expression
with target dimension $M_{p+1}$.  Start with the identity subspace of
dimension $M_1$.  After processing coloring $c'_p$, the selected subspace
should have dimension
\[
M_{p+1},
\]
not $M_p$.  Thus immediately before stage $p$ the current subspace is
isomorphic to the corresponding mixed-product space of dimension $M_p$, and
Proposition~15 refines it to dimension $M_{p+1}$.  At $p=m$ this gives the
one-dimensional space asserted in Claim~17.  This simultaneously removes the
undefined $M_0$ appearing in the printed proof.

These repairs are bookkeeping changes to the iteration rather than a change
of its qualitative strategy.  The corrected qualitative simultaneous-
refinement lemma remains \todo in Lean; numerical bounds should be restored
only after that proof is formalized.

\subsection{Theorem 13 gluing}
The double-induction architecture still appears coherent, but the rendered
pages 21--24 confirm four literal source errors in the decomposition/gluing
formulas.

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item In the definition
\[
\mathcal B
 = \{D'_2\subseteq \bigcup_{i\in D_2}J_i:
       |D'_1\cap J_i|\le1\text{ for all }i\in D_2\},
\]
the cardinality condition must contain $D'_2$, not $D'_1$.  As printed it
mentions a set which is not a parameter of the comprehension.  The intended
condition is
\[
|D'_2\cap J_i|\le1\qquad(i\in D_2).
\]

\item After Lemma~16 supplies
$(f'_z)_{z\in[d']}$ and
$(X'_z)_{z\in[d']\setminus D_2^*}$, one subsequent occurrence is printed as
$(f'_z)_{z\in[d]}$.  The ambient mixed-product partition is on $[d']$, so this
index must be $[d']$.

\item On page~24 the numbers $y^i_j$ are chosen in $[b^M]$ and
\[
Y_i=\{y^i_j:j\in[b^k]\}\subseteq[b^M].
\]
Hence the printed
\[
\overline Y_i=[b^k]\setminus Y_i
\]
is ill-typed; it must be
\[
\overline Y_i=[b^M]\setminus Y_i.
\]

\item In clauses (ii)--(iv) defining $f_i$, the source quantifies
$t\in b^{n-M}$.  Since $s_y\in b^M$, concatenating such a $t$ has length
exactly $n$ and therefore lies outside the word domain $b^{<n}$.  The intended
tail domain is
\[
t\in b^{<n-M}.
\]
With this correction, $|s_y^\frown t|<n$ and the gluing covers exactly the
nodes above level $M$.
\end{enumerate}

These corrections are local typing/indexing repairs.  They do not by
themselves certify the final implication that the glued
$((f_i)_{i\in[d]},(X_i)_{i\in D_1})$ is $(k+1)$-dimensional and $c$-good.
That final verification should be carried out after the corrected mixed-product
and Lemma~16 interfaces are formalized in Lean.

\subsection{Definition 26: preservation up to the distinguished node}
Immediately after Definition~26, the paper observes that a refinement
$f'$ retaining every root of $f$ at or before the distinguished node $t_0$
leaves the word unchanged at every ambient position at or before $t_0$
in the auxiliary order.  This depends on a small but important order fact:
if $r\sqsubseteq s\preccurlyeq t_0$, then $r\preccurlyeq t_0$.

\paragraph{Lean validation.} \todo
\lean{DualTree.CutPreservation.paperAux_of_prefix} and
\lean{DualTree.CutPreservation.forwardAux_of_prefix} establish this monotonicity
for both the printed reverse-lex convention and the proposed forward-lex
repair.  The lemma
\lean{DualTree.CutPreservation.underlyingSymbol_eq_of_syntactic_cut}
then shows that, under syntactic refinement, retaining the roots up to $t_0$
preserves every constant or variable symbol at all ambient positions up to
$t_0$.  To compare variable symbols belonging to different support-dependent
types, the formal statement forgets the subtype membership proof and compares
their underlying root nodes.

For an alphabet with at least two letters,
\lean{DualTree.CutPreservation.paperCut_preserved_of_span_refinement}
and its forward-lex analogue recover the same conclusion from the paper's
evaluated-span inclusion.  For a singleton alphabet this inference is invalid,
as the earlier counterexample to Theorem~3 demonstrates.  The stronger
assertion following Definition~26---that the entire set of signatures observable
at $t_0$ is unchanged---still requires a formal signature-transport argument
and is \todo.

\subsection{Definition 26: the signature path boundary}
The signature $(S',g')$ of Definition~26 includes, for certain leaves $t$,
the least ambient prefix of $t$ outside
\[
D=\{s\in b^{<n}:s\preccurlyeq s^*,\ s\ne s^*\},
\qquad s^*=\max_{\preccurlyeq}\operatorname{Int}(S).
\]
This is an operation on the \emph{ambient} predecessor path, which can
contain nodes not in the skew support tree.

\paragraph{Lean validation.} \todo
\lean{DualTree.SignatureBoundary.Boundary} encodes the first prefix
outside $D$, and
\lean{DualTree.SignatureBoundary.existsUnique_boundary_on_path}
proves that this prefix exists uniquely whenever the leaf $t$ itself
lies outside $D$.  The choice
\lean{DualTree.SignatureBoundary.firstBoundary} therefore has an
explicit existence argument, and its specification is checked separately.
This builds on the verified finite frontier decomposition, but does not
silently interpret a potentially undefined minimum.

The remaining geometric obligation is to prove that every leaf selected
in the printed definition---a leaf outside $\operatorname{Int}(S)$ that is
not a descendant of $s^*$---is indeed outside $D$ under the
semi-complete/skew hypotheses.  We must then establish that the resulting
signature boundary nodes interact with the cone frontier of Lemma~27
as claimed.  The entire signature and its transport have not yet been
validated.

\subsection{Definitions 23 and 26: starred objects and signature skeleton}
The class $W^*_{v,l}$ of Definition~23 consists of semi-complete skew
trees $S$ together with a tree-variable word $g$ whose variable roots
are the interior vertices of $S$ (and form a skew support when $l>0$).
The interior has precisely $l$ vertices.

\paragraph{Lean validation.} \todo
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.StarredWord} records these requirements
using the original finite-tree and variable-word types.  The theorems
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.variable_root_in_interior} and
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.variable_at_interior} check both
directions of the support/interior identification.

The conditional constructor
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.signatureTree} reproduces the tree
component of Definition~26: the interior vertices plus, for each
exceptional leaf, the first point on its ambient predecessor path
outside $D$.  It requires an explicit proof of existence of that
point for every leaf; this proof is not built into the definition.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.interior_mem_signatureTree}
checks that no interior vertex is lost.

Finally, \lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.prefixWord} represents the
restriction $g\upharpoonright D$, with variable symbols identified
by their underlying roots; its preservation under syntactic refinement
is proved by
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.prefixWord_eq_of_syntactic_refinement}.

\paragraph{Conditional removal of the boundary hypothesis.} \todo
The lemma \lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.leaf_not_before_of_fullBefore}
proves that, when the branching number is positive and all nodes
preceding a cut branch fully, no leaf can precede that cut.
Using the interior of the starred object,
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.exceptionalLeaves_not_before_of_fullBefore}
therefore proves that every exceptional leaf in Definition~26 lies outside
$D$, \emph{provided} the cut satisfies the exact
$\operatorname{fullBeforeB}$ clause of Section~3.
The constructor
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.signatureTree_of_fullBefore}
then eliminates the per-leaf existence hypotheses while retaining an
explicit full-branching hypothesis at the cut.

\paragraph{Last-interior witness.} \ok
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.fullBefore_at_maxInterior}
uses clause~(iv) of the skew-tree definition: its distinguished branching
node has at least one successor while all later nodes are leaves.
Consequently any maximal interior vertex coincides with this branching
witness and satisfies the full-before clause.  The corollary
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.exceptionalLeaves_not_before_of_maxInterior}
establishes the missing existence condition for every exceptional leaf.
The constructor
\lean{DualTree.StarredSignature.signatureTree_at_maxInterior}
therefore no longer requires a separate full-branching or per-leaf
hypothesis once the maximal interior cut is provided.  These results
were already proved and transitive-axiom-checked in the Lean development.
This verification concerns the boundary-existence obligation only.

The complete signature map is still \todo: we need to formalize the
existence/canonical choice of the maximal interior vertex for every
nonempty starred object, verify the structural properties of the
resulting signature tree, and prove invariance of all observable
signatures under refinement and the signature-cone coding of Lemma~27.

\subsection{Lemma 27}
This is the present structural bottleneck.
\paragraph{Ambient meet preservation under direction-preserving prefix replacement.} \todo
A prefix-poset isomorphism does not by itself preserve longest common
ambient prefixes: the target may introduce unrecorded common prefixes
between successive vertices.  The module
\lean{DualTree.MeetImageTransfer} identifies the exact extra invariant
needed to rule this out.  Suppose a prefix-preserving and reflecting
replacement fixes every vertex of an old meet base and also preserves,
for each old meet vertex, the first ambient direction occupied by
every source node.  If every meet of distinct source nodes is represented
in that fixed base, then
\lean{DualTree.MeetImageTransfer.meet_represented_of_prefix_and_directions}
proves that every ambient meet of target nodes equals the image of
the corresponding source meet.
Consequently
\lean{DualTree.MeetImageTransfer.meetClosed_of_prefix_and_directions}
transports meet-closure to the complete reconstructed image.
The proof is a one-level contradiction: any extra target meet length
would produce an extra shared first direction in the source,
contradicting maximality of its old meet.
It remains to instantiate these hypotheses with the verified
literal skeleton prefix isomorphism and the source-facing
direction-preservation theorem, then deduce uniqueness of immediate
successors in $S_w$.



\paragraph{Concrete $Q$-support: ambient meet closure and successor uniqueness.} \ok
The generic meet-transport criterion above can now be instantiated
with the actual labelled occurrences
$\operatorname{Int}(S')\cup\{t_0\}\,\dot\cup\,R$
of Lemma~27, without an additional faithfulness hypothesis.
The source meet belongs to $\operatorname{Int}(S)$ for any pair of
distinct occurrences; the labelled $Q$ map fixes this old base,
preserves and reflects prefixes, and preserves the first ambient
direction from every vertex of the old base. Finally every node of
$S_w$ is represented by one of these occurrences. Thus
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QMeet.sourceQ_meetClosed} gives actual
ambient meet closure of the reconstructed finite $Q$ node set, and
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QMeet.sourceQ_atMostOneDirection}
shows that two distinct immediate successors of a $Q$ vertex
cannot lie in the same ambient direction.
Both declarations passed the clean Lean build and transitive axiom
audit (PR~\#133, 463 audited endpoints). The preceding
meet-transfer and directional-uniqueness subobligations are discharged.
This remains strictly weaker than $S_w$ being semi-complete skew:
one must still connect occupied cones to \emph{immediate} successors,
verify rootedness and the printed auxiliary-order/skew clauses,
and construct the word component $g_w$. In particular, a
prefix-poset isomorphism alone must not be used to assert
preservation of the auxiliary order.

\paragraph{From occupied cones to actual $Q$-successors.} \ok
Even after ambient meet-closure, existence of a point of $S_w$ in
each ambient cone from an old interior node only gives a descendant,
not literally an immediate support successor.  The finite first-step
lemma \lean{DualTree.Lemma27QImmediate.uniqueBranch_of_directional_descendant}
extracts the first support point on a path to such a descendant.
Meet-closure makes that point unique within the direction,
and duplicate-freeness gives the exact Boolean
\lean{SkewTree.uniqueBranchB} predicate.  Combining this with the
previously established occupancy of every direction gives
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QImmediate.sourceQ_full_immediate_directions}
for every old interior vertex of the \emph{actual} $Q$ support.
Both declarations passed the clean CI and transitive axiom check
(PR~\#134, 465 audited endpoints).
To finish the semi-complete branching property, use
$\operatorname{Int}(S_w)=\operatorname{Int}(S)$ to split old interior
vertices from terminal new markers, and then establish the exact
immediate-successor count $0$ or $b$ at every vertex.  The
remaining skew/rooted/auxiliary-order and word-construction
requirements are independent; do not infer them merely from
these branch facts.

\paragraph{Root and non-leaf branching of the reconstructed $Q$ support.} \ok
The ambient meet-closure theorem also supplies rootedness with no
extra order assumptions. Any finite nonempty meet-closed set has a
member below all others in the ambient prefix order, by iterating
pairwise meets. The general facts
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QRooted.common_support_root_of_meetClosed}
and \lean{DualTree.Lemma27QRooted.rootedB_of_meetClosed_nonempty}
yield \lean{DualTree.Lemma27QRooted.sourceQ_rooted}, since $t_0$
is retained in $S_w$. Moreover, for $b>0$ the previously proved
identity $\operatorname{Int}(S_w)=\operatorname{Int}(S)$
upgrades the full-direction theorem to every actual non-leaf:
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QRooted.sourceQ_full_immediate_at_nonleaf}.
All four declarations passed the clean Lean build and transitive
audit (PR~\#135, 469 audited endpoints).
The remaining numerical branch count follows by proving that the
immediate successors are partitioned by their unique first
directions; this finite cardinality step should be formalized rather
than inferred from the Boolean predicates. Even after this, neither
the printed auxiliary-order clauses nor the word-construction
part of Lemma~27 follows from rooted meet-closed geometry.

\paragraph{Exact numerical $0$-or-$b$ branching of the reconstructed $Q$ support.} \ok
There is a missing combinatorial step between
having one immediate successor in each direction and having exactly
$b$ immediate successors. The new generic lemma
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QBranchCount.immediateSuccs_length_eq_of_full_directions}
constructs an actual equivalence between the $b$ directions and
the immediate-support-successor list (viewed as a finite set).
The proof checks that every support successor occupies a direction,
that the first direction of a fixed node is unique, and that
the unique-branch predicates supply the inverse map.
Using $\operatorname{Int}(S_w)=\operatorname{Int}(S)$ and the
verified all-directions property at every $Q$ non-leaf yields
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QBranchCount.sourceQ_zero_or_b_immediate_successors}
for all vertices of the reconstructed $S_w$, assuming $b>0$.
The source-facing counting theorem passed the full Lean build and transitive
axiom audit (PR~\#136; 472 audited endpoints).
Even after it passes, the full semi-complete skew predicate
needs independent proofs of the printed auxiliary-order and
height/rank clauses; no such order facts are inferred here.

\paragraph{Precise residual skewness for $Q$ semi-completeness.} \ok
Once the exact $0$-or-$b$ count is established, the additional
Boolean branching conjunct in the definition of semi-complete is
automatic. The source-facing
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QBranchClause.sourceQ_boolean_branch_clause}
checks this conjunct on the actual reconstructed support, and
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QBranchClause.sourceQ_semiComplete_iff_skew}
reduces its full semi-completeness, under $b>0$, \emph{exactly} to
the printed skew predicate.
Both declarations passed CI and the transitive axiom audit
(PR~\#137; 474 audited endpoints). They do not establish skewness by definition,
and in particular do not bypass the known counterexample to
preservation of the printed auxiliary order.

\paragraph{Preparing the final branching-witness check for $Q$.} \ok
The failure of canonical preservation of the printed auxiliary
order does not mean that the order itself lacks antisymmetry.
The new module \lean{DualTree.PaperAuxAntisymm} verifies
antisymmetry of both the ambient lexicographic tie comparison
and the printed length-first auxiliary order. The generic
\lean{DualTree.LastBranchWitness.condIVB_of_full_last_witness}
shows that, for $b>0$, the printed skew clause~(iv) follows
from a retained last branching vertex with all $b$ directions,
the full-before condition, and the empty-after condition.
The chosen partial-branch index is the last element of $[b]$.
All four generic declarations passed CI and the transitive axiom
audit (PR~\#138; 478 audited endpoints). Their application to
the actual reconstructed $S_w$ is a separate obligation:
one must show that all nodes before the old maximal interior
cut are interior and all nodes after that cut are leaves.
Neither the skew height-order clauses (ii)--(iii) nor the
global variable word is established by this conditional check.

\paragraph{Skew clause~(iv) at the original maximal interior cut.} \ok
The reconstructed Q support retains the maximal original interior
node $t_0$. Every reconstructed node at or before this cut
is in the original interior: an additional projected marker would
contradict its verified outside-cut frontier property.
Therefore all such nodes have one immediate support successor
in each ambient direction.
Conversely, any non-leaf after $t_0$ would belong to the original
interior, contradicting maximality of $t_0$ by antisymmetry
of the printed auxiliary order.
The source-specific results
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QLastWitness.sourceQ_fullBeforeB},
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QLastWitness.sourceQ_emptyAfterB},
and \lean{DualTree.Lemma27QLastWitness.sourceQ_condIVB}
formalize exactly this argument. These four statements passed Lean CI
and the transitive axiom audit (PR~\#139, 482 audited endpoints).
Once certified they close the final branching-witness clause
(iv) of skewness for $S_w$ under $b>0$. The independent intrinsic
height and ambient-length clauses (ii)--(iii) still require proof;
neither follows merely from the checked prefix isomorphism or
direction-preserving replacement.

\paragraph{Exact residual: skew clauses~(ii)--(iii) and no others.} \ok
For a nonsingleton duplicate-free rooted support already satisfying
the final branching witness clause~(iv), the full skew predicate is
equivalent to the conjunction of its two intrinsic-height versus
ambient-length comparison clauses.  The generic theorem
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QOrderReduction.skewB_iff_order_conditions}
records this factorization.
The actual Q node set is nonsingleton since the retained maximal
interior vertex has an immediate successor in every direction.
Combining the certified branching, rootedness, and clause~(iv)
gives \lean{DualTree.Lemma27QOrderReduction.sourceQ_skew_iff_order_conditions}.
Together with the previously proved exact $0$-or-$b$ successor count,
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27QOrderReduction.sourceQ_semiComplete_iff_order_conditions}
states that the \emph{entire} semi-complete-skew assertion for $S_w$
is equivalent to exactly the Boolean conditions
$\operatorname{condIIB}(S_w)$ and $\operatorname{condIIIB}(S_w)$,
under $b>0$ and the current safe cone domain.
This reduction passed Lean CI and the transitive axiom audit
(PR~\#140, 486 audited endpoints); it is not a proof of either
of those remaining inequalities. Both need an independent check
against the actual ambient lengths and intrinsic ranks, and
may require revising the printed construction.

Before proving the two outstanding skew height/order clauses,
one must check whether the product's independent bullet coordinates
can force unequal ambient depths of newly projected terminal nodes.
The binary candidate uses the 3-complete support

has two immediate branches at its root, and satisfies skew clauses

the lexicographically earlier leaf $00$ has length two, whereas

\lean{DualTree.QLengthObstruction} checks these finite claims

If that instantiation succeeds, the arbitrary independent choice
of $D_2$ bullet points is incompatible with the printed skew
axiom~(ii); a synchronized-depth restriction or a modification

\paragraph{Typed Q-depth selection at the literal frontier coordinates.} \ok
To distinguish the finite shape test from a counterexample to

instantiation in \lean{DualTree.QLengthTypedWitness}.
Here $b=2$, the source starred tree is
$\{\varnothing,0,1\}$, the enclosing support is the

interior cut is $\varnothing$.  The source word has one
variable rooted at $\varnothing$, meeting the stated
root/support conditions.  The distinguished markers $0,1$
are assigned two different bounded tail points, of lengths

The lemmas \lean{DualTree.QLengthTypedWitness.left_bullet_is_chosen}
and \lean{DualTree.QLengthTypedWitness.right_bullet_is_chosen}

These declarations passed the clean Lean build and transitive axiom audit
(PR~\#142, 508 audited endpoints).
This confirms only that independently unequal local tail lengths
are well typed; to certify failure of skew clause~(ii) for the
literal $Q$ construction, still prove the precise projected
ambient node lengths, their first branching directions, their
intrinsic heights in $S_w$, and their simultaneous membership
in the actual reconstructed support. No conclusion about the
full paper is drawn before that final test.

\paragraph{General rank/ambient-length obstruction certificates.} \todo
The new module \lean{DualTree.SkewOrderObstruction} isolates exact
finite certificates for failure of skew axioms (ii) and (iii),
without needing to evaluate a noncomputably reconstructed support.
To disprove clause (ii), it suffices to exhibit two support nodes
$s,t$ of the same intrinsic height such that $s$ precedes $t$
lexicographically but $|s|>|t|$. For clause (iii), a pair
of strictly increasing intrinsic height but non-increasing
ambient length suffices. The module also derives failure of
skewness and semi-completeness from a witnessed clause-(ii)
failure, for nonsingleton supports.
These five generic declarations await CI and transitive axiom
auditing. They are intended for the remaining literal $Q$
projection test and constitute neither a new counterexample
nor a substitute for checking the actual projected nodes.

\paragraph{Canonical projected depths for the actual typed Q source.} \ok
Building on the typed source and mixed-product element, the module
\lean{DualTree.QLengthProjected} checks the *actual* canonical
support projections rather than independent terminal replacements.
In the full binary ambient support $T=2^{<3}$, intrinsic support
height is equal to ambient word length.  Each literal child
marker of the root cut is its own minimal outside-cut support
frontier; this is derived from the frontier minimality theorem,
not by evaluating a noncomputable choice. The established canonical
projection-height formula then gives ambient depths two and one
for the left and right new $D_2$ points, respectively:
\lean{DualTree.QLengthProjected.left_projection_length_two}
and \lean{DualTree.QLengthProjected.right_projection_length_one}.
These source-facing declarations passed the Lean build and transitive
axiom audit (PR~\#144; 516 checks on its stacked branch).
They resolve the output *lengths* but still do not establish that
the two projected points have the same intrinsic height inside
$S_w$, nor the lexicographic comparison required to refute skew
clause~(ii). The exact final Q-support test remains a separate TODO.

\paragraph{Intrinsic heights of the actual new Q terminal markers.} \ok
The ambient lengths of the two projected nodes are not their
intrinsic heights in the reconstructed finite support.
The module \lean{DualTree.QLengthNewIntrinsicHeight}
formalizes the missing rank argument: in a duplicate-free
support whose only interior node is the empty root, every
nonroot support node has precisely that root as its unique
strict support predecessor and therefore has intrinsic height
one. The previously checked equality
$\operatorname{Int}(S_w)=\operatorname{Int}(S)$ specializes to
$\operatorname{Int}(S_w)=\{\varnothing\}$ in the binary typed
test. Using literal marker membership in $S_w$, the theorems
\lean{DualTree.QLengthNewIntrinsicHeight.left_projection_Q_height_one}
and \lean{DualTree.QLengthNewIntrinsicHeight.right_projection_Q_height_one}
show that both genuine projected markers have intrinsic
height one, even though their ambient lengths are two and one.
These seven declarations passed the Lean build and transitive axiom
audit (PR~\#145; 523 audited endpoints on the stacked branch). The remaining lexicographic comparison of the
projected nodes and the consequent Boolean clause-(ii)
failure will be a final, separate source-facing test.

\paragraph{Definition~20 starred-domain admissibility of the Q witness.} \ok
The whole mixed product $W^D$ is larger than the starred subspace
$W^D_*$. Definition~20 requires the $D_2$-coordinate marked
points, \emph{in the prescribed order}, to form a vector
$1$-complete skew subtree. For singleton coordinate supports
this means their ambient lengths are nondecreasing; the lengths
need not be equal. The printed length-first auxiliary order uses
reverse lexicographic tie-breaking, so the literal frontier
enumeration for the binary root cut is $(1,0)$, with corresponding
local points $(\varnothing,0)$ of lengths $(0,1)$.
The Lean modules
\lean{DualTree.QLengthStarredDomain} and
\lean{DualTree.QLengthStarredCoordinateOrder}
verify the actual finite frontier enumeration, the noncomputable
literal marker-to-coordinate map, and the vector $1$-completeness
of the genuinely selected points, rather than assuming that
input list order agrees with the paper. These checks passed the
Lean build and transitive axiom audit (PRs~\#147--148).
Thus the typed height-three counterexample is admitted by the
paper's $W^D_*$ restriction, not merely by $W^D$.
This does not refute the existence conclusion of Lemma~27 or
the final dual Ramsey theorem.

\paragraph{Noncomputable source marker index identities for $W^D_*$.} \ok
Beyond the vector $1$-completeness check, the literal
Q witness must use the actual coordinate map, not just a
hand-selected tuple with a convenient coordinate order.
The module
\lean{DualTree.QLengthStarredCoordinateOrder}
checks that the selected frontier coordinate of the
right marker is $0$ and that of the left marker is $1$
(in zero-based indexing). These identifications use
the exact noncomputable marker-to-frontier choice,
\lean{coordinate_frontier}, and the previously verified
printed-order frontier enumeration $(1,0)$.
The combined source-facing theorem
\lean{actual_starred_input_certificate}
certifies that the genuine mixed-product bullet tuple
has the required vector $1$-complete order. All three
declarations passed the Lean build and transitive axiom
audit (PR~\#148, 546 audited endpoints).
The fully checked example has complete height three;
the self-contained author note gives the elementary
arbitrary-height construction independently.

\paragraph{Repair attempt: global forward order versus frontier-only reindexing.} \ok/\todo
The first counterexample disappears if one merely lists the
two frontier coordinates in forward lexicographic order,
while retaining all other printed conventions.
However, \emph{reindexing the frontiers alone does not repair
Lemma~27}. In the binary support $T=2^{<4}$, take the
printed semi-complete skew source
\[
 S=\{\varnothing,0,1,10,11\},\qquad t_0=1.
\]
Then $R=\{0,10,11\}$ and the frontier set equals $R$.
Choose the one-letter marked tail $0$ at every $D_2$
coordinate. Because all selected tails have length one,
the marked tuple is vector $1$-complete in \emph{either}
enumeration order. The displayed $Q$ produces
\[
 S_w=\{\varnothing,1,00,100,110\}.
\]
Inside $S_w$, the two nodes $00$ and $1$ have the same
intrinsic height one, with $00<_{\rm lex}1$, but
$|00|=2>|1|=1$. This is another exact failure of clause~(ii),
and it persists if only the $D_2$ coordinates are reindexed.
Lean tests the source, equal-tail starred admissibility,
and the failed condition in
\lean{DualTree.QOrderRepairAudit} (pending CI).

A coherent \emph{candidate} is to change the
auxiliary linear order \emph{globally}: in Section~3.1,
replace the equal-length comparison
$t\le_{\rm lex}s$ by $s\le_{\rm lex}t$,
and propagate this convention through all definitions,
signature cuts, canonical embeddings, and frontier
enumerations. Then the printed source $S$ above ceases
to be skew. Its mirror
$\{\varnothing,0,1,00,01\}$ is semi-complete skew in
the global forward order, and the corresponding finite
reconstruction
$\{\varnothing,0,10,000,010\}$ is also semi-complete
skew. These are individual kernel tests, \emph{not}
a general proof of repaired Lemma~27 or a correction
to the separately refuted Remark~1.

Independent exhaustive regressions, added as
\texttt{scripts/check-q-order-repair.py}, test
the literal signature/frontier geometry and all
vector $1$-complete singleton marked tuples for
full binary supports of intrinsic heights three
and four, and all $45$ complete skew supports of
intrinsic height three embedded in $2^{<4}$.
For the consistent forward-order convention the
respective tests find zero failures among $49$,
$2509$, and $2205$ admissible $Q$-tree instances.
For the printed reverse-lex convention, they find
$14$, $1110$, and $518$ distinct failures.
For frontier-only reindexing in the full height-four
test, $176/2509$ cases still fail.
The printed-order height-four sample contains $1038$
clause-(ii) and $88$ clause-(iii) failures, with $16$
overlap. These are \emph{finite computational evidence}
and all recorded binary regression tests passed CI in PR~\#149.

\paragraph{Why global forward lex agrees with the skew rank axioms.} \todo
The candidate global order is not merely a change of
notation: the paper's clause~(ii) requires weakly increasing
ambient lengths with \emph{forward}-lexicographic order
inside each intrinsic support level, and clause~(iii)
requires strictly increasing lengths across levels.
The general theorems
\lean{DualTree.ForwardOrderCompatibility.forwardAux_of_equal_height_lex}
and
\lean{DualTree.ForwardOrderCompatibility.forwardAux_of_lower_height}
derive the corresponding forward auxiliary comparison
directly from clauses~(ii) and (iii).
The companion cone lemma
\lean{DualTree.ForwardOrderCompatibility.finLexLE_of_ordered_cones}
shows that ordered sibling directions in a (possibly
stretched) support enforce ordinary lexicographic
order on their entire ambient cones.
These lemmas are intended as input to a proof that the
canonical embedding of a complete skew support preserves
the repaired forward auxiliary order; they do not alone
establish Lemma~27. Pending Lean CI and transitive axiom checks.

\paragraph{Candidate repair of canonical order preservation.} \ok
The printed page~8 assertion that the canonical map $I_T$
preserves the length-first reverse-lex order is already
refuted by a kernel-checked three-node example.
For the \emph{forward}-lex candidate replacement there
is now a structural proof plan, not merely finite evidence.
First, the canonical support walk preserves ordinary lex
order on all admissible source addresses, since the first
different ambient direction is preserved by the walk
(\lean{DualTree.CanonicalForwardAux.walk_lex_of_admissible}).
The proved admissibility of all bounded addresses yields
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalForwardAux.canonicalEmbedding_lex_mono}.
Next, the skew axioms on intrinsic height and ambient
length make forward auxiliary comparison monotone along
both same-rank and different-rank pairs, yielding
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalForwardAux.canonicalEmbedding_forward_mono_of_complete}
for any nonsingleton complete skew support. All four new declarations passed the Lean build and transitive
axiom audit in PR~\#151, bringing the checked endpoints to 562.
They would establish the desired direction of order
preservation for the \emph{globally} corrected convention.
They do not validate the whole repaired $Q$ map, and
the published source remains frozen.

\paragraph{Full auxiliary order reflection for the repaired canonical map.} \todo
The previously verified theorem
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalForwardAux.canonicalEmbedding_forward_mono_of_complete}
establishes one-way preservation of the repaired
length-first forward-lex auxiliary order by the

The new module \lean{DualTree.CanonicalForwardAuxIso}
proves that this repaired order is reflexive,
total, and antisymmetric, then combines the
canonical map's injectivity with its monotonicity
to obtain \emph{order reflection}. Thus, for

\[
 u\preceq_{\rm fwd}v
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 I_T(u)\preceq_{\rm fwd} I_T(v).
\]
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalForwardAuxIso.canonicalEmbedding_forward_iff}
would supply precisely the missing order-isomorphism

forward-lex convention, with no changes to the

full Lemma~27 proof or the separate Remark~1 gap.

\paragraph{Terminal rank and lex separation at the forward cut.} \todo
A key source-support invariant of the globally repaired
convention can be proved independently of the $Q$ map.
Suppose $S$ satisfies forward-order skew clauses
(ii)--(iv), $t_0$ is its final branching cut,
and a leaf $u$ belongs to $S$.
The full-before clause implies that no terminal
$u\ne t_0$ can occur before $t_0$ in the
forward auxiliary order
(\lean{DualTree.ForwardCutTerminalOrder.cut_forwardAux_before_terminal}).
Using clause~(iii), it follows that
$h_S(u)\ge h_S(t_0)$
(\lean{DualTree.ForwardCutTerminalOrder.terminal_height_ge_cut}).
Using clause~(ii), if their intrinsic heights
are equal then $u$ cannot be lexicographically
earlier than $t_0$
(\lean{DualTree.ForwardCutTerminalOrder.terminal_not_lex_before_cut_at_same_height}).
These are the two source-side comparisons needed
to divide the literal $R$ markers into the
same-height and next-height groups in a repaired
$Q$ reconstruction. The three declarations await
CI and transitive axiom audit. The next open
step is to identify the \emph{ambient complete-support
frontier ranks} of these groups and use ordered
local marked-point lengths to verify $S_w$
satisfies clauses~(ii)--(iii).
The large-parameter theorem and colouring part
remain unverified.

\paragraph{Forward-order frontier rank bands and projected-rank arithmetic.} \todo
For a nonsingleton forward-auxiliary skew support
$T$ with an interior cut $t_0$ of rank $m$,
every minimal support point $f$ outside the
inclusive forward-auxiliary cut satisfies
\[
 m\le h_T(f)\le m+1.
\]
The lower bound follows because a node of rank
less than $m$ is shorter than $t_0$ by skew
clause~(iii), hence lies \emph{inside} the auxiliary
cut. For the upper bound, all proper support
predecessors of a minimal outside-cut frontier
must precede $t_0$ and therefore have rank at
most $m$; the rank map is injective along each
branch. The checked declarations under development
are \lean{DualTree.ForwardFrontierRanks.frontier_rank_band}
and its two constituent inequalities.

More strongly, if frontiers $f_i,f_j$ are
listed in the \emph{forward} auxiliary order,
$i<j$ implies $h_T(f_i)\le h_T(f_j)$.
A vector $1$-complete family of singleton
$D_2$ points has nondecreasing local tail lengths
$|s_i|\le |s_j|$; therefore
\[
 h_T(f_i)+|s_i|
 \le h_T(f_j)+|s_j|.
\]
This gives the precise monotonicity of the
\emph{intrinsic projected ranks in $T$}, provided
the canonical cone-height formula is verified
for the corrected globally forward-auxiliary
support class. The module
\lean{DualTree.ForwardFrontierRanks}
formalizes both results (pending CI).
It does not identify the ranks of these
points in $S_w$, nor show their
\emph{ambient word lengths} are monotone.
Those are the two remaining geometric steps
needed before certifying the corrected
semi-completeness statement, independently
of the still-open word and colouring parts.

\paragraph{Ambient-length monotonicity of forward ordered cone projections.} \todo
The frontier-rank calculation alone is insufficient:
Lemma~27 requires ambient node lengths to be
nondecreasing at a \emph{fixed intrinsic height
of the reconstructed $S_w$}, whereas the canonical
cone maps advance the rank in the enclosing support
$T$ by the length of their local tails.
For the globally repaired forward-lex convention
we can, however, establish the following
general ordering theorem in $T$ itself.

Let $f\ne g$ be incomparable minimal outside-cut
frontiers in forward auxiliary order, and
let $u,v\in T$ extend $f,g$ respectively with
$h_T(u)=h_T(f)+n$, $h_T(v)=h_T(g)+m$,
where $n\le m$ is the marked singleton-vector
condition. Then
\[
 u\preceq_{\rm fwd}v,\qquad |u|\le |v|.
\]
Indeed forward order of frontiers implies
$h_T(f)\le h_T(g)$, and so the projected
ranks are nondecreasing. If the ranks are
equal, both summands are equal, which forces
equal frontier ranks and equal local tail
lengths. Forward frontier order and the
skew same-height axiom imply lex order of
$f,g$; incomparability propagates that lex
comparison to all their extensions.
The skew height/length clauses then imply
the required forward auxiliary order and
the ambient-length inequality.
This is formalized as
\lean{DualTree.ForwardProjectedTerminalOrder.ordered_frontier_projections_forwardAux}
and
\lean{DualTree.ForwardProjectedTerminalOrder.ordered_frontier_projections_length_le}
(pending CI).
The next source-specific step is to identify the
intrinsic $S_w$-rank groups of projected markers
and show that they appear in contiguous blocks
of the correct forward frontier order.
Comparisons with \emph{retained interior vertices}
and the global $g_w$/colouring proof remain open.

no earlier support node can be a leaf.

\paragraph{Corrected inclusive-cut frontier uniqueness for forward supports.} \todo
Lemma~27 uses an \emph{inclusive} auxiliary cut
of the complete support $T$ to define minimal
frontiers, whereas Definition~26 uses a
\emph{strict} auxiliary cut to locate
signature leaf boundaries. Under the
globally repaired forward order, these
must continue to be distinguished.

The corrected forward auxiliary order is
closed downward along ambient prefixes:
if $s\sqsubseteq t\preceq_{\rm fwd}t_0$,
then $s\preceq_{\rm fwd}t_0$. Hence
any extension of a node \emph{outside}
the inclusive cut remains outside.
For a minimal ambient boundary $s$
of this inclusive cut which has a
support descendant, ambient meet
closure of $T$ implies that there
is a unique minimal frontier $f\in T$
outside the inclusive cut with
$s\sqsubseteq f$.
This general result is
\lean{DualTree.ForwardInclusiveFrontier.unique_frontier_of_boundary}.

Moreover, the generic complete-skew
branching argument gives ambient meet
closure for any nonsingleton support
that is $k$-complete under the \emph{forward}
auxiliary convention; it does not assume
that the support is complete under the
old printed-order convention.
Thus
\lean{DualTree.ForwardInclusiveFrontier.unique_frontier_complete_forward}
provides the corrected analogue of the
original source frontier theorem.
These five declarations await CI and the
transitive axiom audit.
The remaining source-specific steps are
to show that all markers in the forward
signature's literal $R$ satisfy the
inclusive boundary condition and have
support descendants, prove injectivity
of their assigned frontiers, and
rebuild the actual Q coordinate maps.

\paragraph{Both corrected literal marker types meet unique frontiers.} \todo
The corrected literal $R$ consists of boundary
markers from exceptional leaves and the $b$
immediate ambient children of the final cut.
The inclusive frontier theorem requires that
each candidate marker be a boundary of the
\emph{inclusive} forward cut and have a
descendant in $T$.

For an exceptional source leaf $t$ outside
the strict forward cut, let $s$ be its first
ambient prefix outside that strict cut.
Since $t$ is not a descendant of the cut,
$s\ne t_0$; therefore $s$ also lies
outside the \emph{inclusive} cut, and
its earlier prefixes lie inside.
Moreover $s\sqsubseteq t\in S\subseteq T$,
so it has a $T$-descendant. Hence it
has a unique minimal outside-cut frontier
in a complete forward skew support:
\lean{DualTree.ForwardMarkerFrontierCoverage.exceptional_boundary_unique_frontier}.

For a child marker $t_0^\frown i$,
all earlier ambient prefixes lie at or before
the inclusive forward cut, and the child
itself lies outside. A complete support
at an early cut has one support successor
in every direction, so this marker also
has a $T$-descendant. The corresponding
unique frontier is supplied by
\lean{DualTree.ForwardMarkerFrontierCoverage.cut_child_unique_frontier}.
These five source-facing declarations await CI.
They establish individual frontier existence
and uniqueness for both marker types, but not
yet collision-freeness \emph{between} distinct
members of the corrected literal $R$.
The next tasks are the corrected marker antichain,
a genuine $R\hookrightarrow F$ injection, and
the global Q support reconstruction.

\paragraph{Forward-cut boundary antichain and marker assignment injectivity.} \ok
Unique-frontier existence for individual repaired markers
does not on its own make the map from the literal marker
set $R$ to frontiers injective. The generic lemma
\lean{DualTree.ForwardMarkerAntichain.eq_of_common_extension}
shows that two inclusive forward-cut first-exit boundaries
below one common ambient node must coincide. This proves
that the set of such boundaries is a prefix antichain.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardMarkerAntichain.boundary_list_frontier_injective}
then verifies injection of \emph{any} chosen extending
frontier map on a typed list of literal markers, provided
each marker is an inclusive first-exit boundary.
All four generic declarations passed Lean CI and the transitive axiom audit (PR~\#160). The source-specific missing argument is to show
that every point of the corrected literal $R$ is an
inclusive boundary. That requires persistence of all
old interior vertices except the final cut in the
corrected signature; otherwise the literal terminal
filter could introduce spurious markers.
After that, the checked unique-frontier choice gives
the collision-free $D_2$ coordinate injection.
The full repaired $Q$ tree, word coding, and colouring
remain open.

cut of Lemma~27, but the general height-one

The twelve declarations passed Lean CI and transitive axiom audit; PR~\#162 is merged. The remaining
canonical-map obligations are surjectivity,
inverse addresses and full order reflection,

domains and the reconstructed $Q$ word.
No use of printed-order completeness is

the \emph{actual} corrected $S_x$
(\lean{markedProjection_terminal}), that
\[
 \operatorname{Int}(S_x)\subseteq
 \operatorname{Int}(S),
\]
and that all $S_x$ nodes lie in the
genuinely forward-complete ambient support
$T$. These results use the sorted
frontiers and safe canonical projections,
not the false printed-order map.
Four declarations await stacked CI and
transitive axiom auditing.
The reverse interior inclusion, now
within reach using the already checked
literal-marker-to-projected-point
coverage, will give exact intrinsic
old levels and allow a direct check
of the remaining skew rank inequalities.
We do not yet claim full semi-completeness,
a variable word, or a valid colouring
pullback.

\paragraph{Exact Q interior under the globally corrected forward order.} \ok
For positive branching $b>0$, the genuine corrected
source-facing Q tree has precisely its original interior:
\[
   s\in\operatorname{Int}(S_x)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   s\in\operatorname{Int}(S).
\]
The forward-specific proof is in
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQInteriorExact}.
It does \emph{not} import the old printed-order
interior-persistence lemma as a black box.
Every old interior vertex $s\ne t_0$ remains
interior in the corrected signature; choose
a strict signature successor $u$. Either
$u$ is an original interior vertex retained
in Q, or it is a first boundary of an exceptional
source leaf and thus belongs to the literal
corrected marker set $R$. In the latter case
the verified sorted-marker-to-frontier
coordinate map provides a genuine strict
Q descendant. The maximal cut itself
has a strict Q descendant through any
one of its $b$ ambient child markers.
For the converse, all new D2 projections
are terminal by
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQSourceTerminals.Q_interior_subset_original}.
Four new declarations await stacked Lean CI
and transitive axiom audit.

This closes the \emph{interior equality}, not
the remaining semi-complete skew conditions.
The next steps are exact intrinsic Q ranks,
same-rank ambient-length monotonicity
including old/new pairs, and the genuine
rooted, full-branch, last-witness axioms.
The global variable word and colouring
part of Lemma~27 also remain open.

\paragraph{Exact intrinsic Q rank as a cardinality of old-interior prefixes.} \ok
For every actual node $t$ in the globally
forward-corrected $Q$ support, the new projected
$D_2$ nodes are terminal and therefore cannot
appear as strict $Q$ predecessors. Consequently,
\[
 h_{S_x}(t)=
 \left|\{s\in\operatorname{Int}(S):s\sqsubset t\}\right|.
\]
This applies equally to retained old interior
vertices and to newly projected terminal leaves;
it requires only the previously verified
old-base equality and source-facing marked
terminality. The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQIntrinsicRanks.Q_height_eq_original_interior_prefix_card}
makes the formula exact with \texttt{Finset.card}
and does not assume the retained base list has
no duplicates. The intermediate lemmas
\lean{Q_strict_predecessor_is_oldBase} and
\lean{Q_preds_iff_oldBase} characterize all
predecessors. Four source-facing declarations
await stacked Lean CI and transitive axiom
audit.

This reduces the remaining skew conditions
(ii)--(iii) to comparisons between the
prefix masks of the projected Q leaves
and the ambient lengths supplied by the
genuine forward-canonical cone maps.
It is \emph{not} yet a proof of these
inequalities, of full branching or
semi-complete skewness, or of the
variable-word and colouring parts.

\paragraph{Projected marker Q ranks depend only on old-interior prefix masks.} \ok
The signature marker $r\in R$ is an inclusive
forward-cut first-exit boundary. Every original
interior node $s$ belongs to the inclusive cut;
the selected projected Q point $P(r)$ extends $r$.
The kernel-level geometric equivalence
\[
 s\sqsubset P(r)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 s\sqsubset r,\qquad s\in\operatorname{Int}(S),
\]
follows because if $r\sqsubseteq s$ then the
forward-cut initial segment would be violated.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQMarkerRanks.oldBase_strictPrefix_projection_iff_marker}
makes this precise. Combining it with the
exact intrinsic Q rank count gives
\[
 h_{S_x}(P(r))=
   \left|\{s\in\operatorname{Int}(S):s\sqsubset r\}\right|.
\]
This is the projected-marker rank independently
of its chosen local $D_2$ tail length, and
\lean{equal_Q_height_of_equal_marker_masks}
identifies the corresponding same-height
classes. Three source-facing declarations
await stacked Lean CI and transitive axiom
audit. This supplies the main intrinsic-rank
calculation required for the repaired Q skew
conditions. The remaining difficulty is to
relate these marker masks to the source's
forward-cut and lexicographic order so that
all old/new and marked/marked length
inequalities hold.

\paragraph{Retained original interior vertices preserve their intrinsic ranks.} \ok
For every $s\in\operatorname{Int}(S)$
retained by the corrected forward $Q$ construction,
the intrinsic height is unchanged:
\[
      h_{S_x}(s)=h_S(s).
\]
Every strict predecessor of an interior node
in the original source $S$ is itself an interior
node, since it has a strict support descendant.
Combining this with the exact Q predecessor
formula gives
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQRetainedRanks.original_interior_Q_height_eq_source}.
The proof uses the source's verified
semi-completeness and duplicate-free support;
it does not assume all source subtrees have
the same ambient lengths.
Two new source-facing declarations await
stacked Lean CI and transitive axiom audit.
With the projected-leaf prefix-mask
rank formula, this identifies intrinsic
ranks for \emph{all} nodes of the
corrected $Q$ support. The separate
skew clauses (ii) and (iii), final
branching completeness, and the word
and colouring arguments still require proof.

\paragraph{Corrected Q has no branching after the maximal forward cut.} \ok
The printed skew clause~(iv) requires all
nodes strictly later than the designated
final branching cut to be terminal.
This part can be discharged for the
\emph{actual} corrected Q construction
without first proving meet-closure or
full branching. By
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQSourceTerminals.Q_interior_subset_original},
every Q-interior point is an original
interior point of $S$, hence at or
before the maximal source cut $t_0$
in the forward auxiliary order.
Forward-order antisymmetry makes
a strictly later Q vertex impossible
to be interior. The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQFinalCutOrder.Q_emptyAfterB}
verifies the exact Boolean
$\operatorname{emptyAfterB}$ part
of clause~(iv). Its companion lemma
\lean{Q_interior_before_cut} records
the stronger source-facing interior
upper bound.
Two declarations await CI and
transitive axiom audit.
The remaining $\operatorname{fullBeforeB}$
and $\operatorname{partialAtB}$
requirements need the exact
directional branching theorem.
The intrinsic level/ambient length
conditions (ii)--(iii), $g_x$ and
colouring are still open.

\paragraph{Corrected Q rootedness is inherited from the original source root.} \todo
The complete ambient support $T$ and the
starred source $S$ can have different roots;
a proof that $S_x\subseteq T$ therefore
does \emph{not} establish that the actual
$Q$ support is rooted.
The module \lean{DualTree.ForwardQRooted}
constructs the source's genuine prefix
root $r_S$ from semi-completeness and
the existence of the final interior cut.
It proves $r_S\in\operatorname{Int}(S)$,
so $r_S$ is retained in $S_x$.
For each literal corrected signature
marker $r\in R$, its defining boundary
lies on the path to an exceptional
source leaf, or is an immediate child
of the cut. Thus it extends $r_S$.
The actual sorted D2 coordinate map
is bijective with $R$ and its cone
projection extends the marker; hence
every new projected $Q$ node extends
$r_S$ as well.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQRooted.Q_rootedB}
verifies the literal Boolean rootedness
predicate on the source-facing $S_x$.
Eight declarations await stacked CI
and transitive axiom auditing.
This discharges skew clause~(i);
full directional branching, the
rank/ambient-length clauses (ii)--(iii)
and the last branching witness still
need proof. The word $g_x$ and
colouring pullback remain untouched.

\paragraph{Ambient meet closure of the repaired literal source skeleton.} \todo
The proposed global forward-order repair requires
careful distinction of the \emph{source}
signature skeleton and its reconstructed
$Q$-image: a prefix-poset isomorphism does not
automatically preserve ambient longest-prefix
meets. The source-side assertions are collected
in \lean{DualTree.ForwardQLiteralSkeletonMeets}.

Using the generic semi-complete skew branching
theorems, a genuinely forward-semi-complete
starred source $S$ is ambient meet-closed.
Every literal marker $r\in R$ has an
actual descendant in $S$, not only in
the enclosing complete support $T$:
exceptional-leaf boundaries have their
defining leaf, and immediate cut-children
have a support descendant from full
semi-complete branching at the cut.
The inclusive forward-cut first-boundary
property makes the markers $R$ a prefix
antichain and prohibits a marker from
prefixing any old interior vertex.
The general marker/interior meet theorem
therefore gives, for any two distinct
labels $u,v\in\operatorname{Int}(S)\cup R$,
\[
 u\wedge v\in\operatorname{Int}(S).
\]
In particular the \emph{actual} corrected
literal source skeleton is ambient meet-closed
(\lean{sourceSkeleton_meetClosed}).
All seven source-facing declarations await
the Lean build and transitive axiom audit.

The image-side step is still separate:
one must show that the actual sorted
D2 replacement fixes all source-interior
meets and preserves the first ambient
direction at each old interior node.
After that, the generic
\lean{DualTree.MeetImageTransfer}
can prove ambient Q meet-closure; neither
rootedness nor prefix equivalence alone
is enough. Full branching, skew order
clauses, word construction and colouring
remain open.

\paragraph{Ambient meet closure and directional uniqueness of the corrected Q support.} \ok
The old literal source skeleton is now known to be meet-closed.
For the genuine corrected $Q$ tree we must not equate ambient
meets merely because the replacement map preserves the abstract
prefix poset. The new generic lemma
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQMeetClosed.meetClosed_of_terminal_extensions}
checks a stronger, sufficient geometric condition: a prefix
antichain of old terminal first-exit markers can be replaced by
arbitrary extensions inside their own cones without changing
its ambient meets with other source vertices. The proof works
directly with original-source support descendants and meets,
including the old-base/marker and marker/marker cases.

In the source-facing corrected reconstruction, the exact
bijective sorted $R\to D_2$ coordinate map lets us recover the
unique literal marker from any bullet coordinate, and
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQMeetClosed.recoveredMarker_prefix_projection}
shows that its true projected $Q$ point extends the recovered
marker. The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQMeetClosed.Q_meetClosed} then establishes
ambient meet closure of the *actual* corrected finite $Q$ support.
The converse
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQMeetClosed.Q_atMostOneDirection}
rules out two immediate support successors in the same
ambient direction. All six declarations passed Lean CI and the transitive axiom
audit (PR~\#185, merged into main).

This is independent of ambient-length and rank monotonicity,
and still does not establish the exact number of immediate
successors, the remaining skew clauses, or the $Q$ word
and colouring pullback. Published source unchanged.

\paragraph{Every old interior vertex retains every ambient branching direction in Q.} \ok
Meet closure of the new Q support rules out multiple immediate
successors in one direction, but alone does not ensure all
$b$ ambient cones at an old interior node remain occupied.
The source-specific lemma
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQAllDirections.Q_all_directions}
takes any old source interior vertex $s$ and direction
$i$ and finds an \emph{actual} corrected Q node extending
$s^\frown i$. For $s=t_0$ it uses the literal cut-child
marker. For an earlier source interior vertex it takes
the unique immediate source successor in direction $i$.
If that node remains interior it survives unchanged in Q;
otherwise it is an exceptional leaf, and the first boundary
outside the strict forward cut must still lie \emph{in the
same direction} from $s$. The new first-boundary lemma
verifies that directional preservation from the downward
closure of the strict forward auxiliary cut, not from an
invalid abstract prefix-isomorphism assumption.
The actual marker-to-Q projection then supplies the
required descendant.

Combining this occupancy with Q meet closure and the
generic immediate-successor extraction lemma proves
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQAllDirections.Q_full_immediate_directions}:
each original interior node of corrected Q has exactly
one immediate successor in every ambient direction.
The four declarations passed CI and the transitive axiom audit
in PR~\#186; the clean main-branch integration remains to be checked. Together with exact interior
persistence they should imply the precise $0$-or-$b$
branch count required by semi-completeness.
The skew rank/ambient-length clauses and the Q word
and colouring pullback are independent remaining tasks.

\paragraph{Exact zero-or-$b$ immediate successor counts in corrected Q.} \ok
The source-facing all-directions lemma and the independent
Q meet-closure theorem together imply that every actual
nonleaf of the repaired forward-order Q support has
\emph{exactly one immediate successor in every ambient
direction}. The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQBranchCount.Q_full_immediate_at_nonleaf}
uses the checked equality $\operatorname{Int}(Q)=
\operatorname{Int}(S)$ to transfer that fact to
all new Q interior vertices.
The exact numerical statement
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQBranchCount.Q_zero_or_b}
then shows each vertex has either no immediate
support successor or precisely $b$, using
an explicit direction-to-immediate-successor
bijection, rather than identifying cone occupancy
with successor count by fiat.
The source-facing Boolean theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQBranchCount.Q_boolean_branch_clause}
discharges the branching conjunct in Definition~23.
Consequently
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQBranchCount.Q_semiComplete_iff_skew}
reduces full Q semi-completeness exactly to the
remaining skew predicates of Definition~3.1.1
under $b>0$.
The four declarations passed the full Lean build and transitive
axiom audit (PR~\#187, followed by a clean main rebase). This remains separate
from the ambient-length/intrinsic-level order
conditions and the word/colouring component of Q.

\paragraph{Skew clause~(iv) and exact residual order axioms for corrected Q.} \ok
The corrected Q node set is already known to be rooted at
the original source root, to have the same interior as the
source, and to satisfy the no-branching-after-cut clause.
The new theorem
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQSkewIV.Q_before_cut_old_interior}
verifies that any actual Q node at or before the final
forward auxiliary cut is an old source interior: the newly
projected markers all lie outside the inclusive cut.
Consequently every Q node strictly before the cut has
unique immediate successors in all $b$ directions, as
certified by
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQSkewIV.Q_fullBeforeB}.
At the cut itself we already know all directions occur
exactly once. The generic last-branch witness theorem
with $i_*=b-1$ then establishes
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQSkewIV.Q_condIVB}, the literal
skew clause~(iv). The source-facing Q support is also
shown to be nonsingleton under $b>0$.

Combining this with exact numerical $0$-or-$b$ branching,
rootedness and duplicate-freeness proves
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQSkewIV.Q_semiComplete_iff_order_conditions}:
the \emph{entire} corrected Q semi-completeness assertion
is equivalent to exactly the two pending conditions
$\operatorname{condIIB}(S_x)$ and
$\operatorname{condIIIB}(S_x)$ comparing intrinsic Q heights
and ambient lengths. All six theorems passed Lean CI and transitive axiom auditing
on PR~\#188 before clean integration. This is a precise reduction,
not a proof of those remaining inequalities. The Q
word, observable signature and colouring pullback also
remain unverified.

\paragraph{Old-interior rank/ambient-length comparisons in corrected Q.} \ok
The globally corrected $Q$ construction retains the exact
old source interior, and every such vertex keeps its
original intrinsic rank. Its old/old comparisons in the
two independent skew numerical axioms are therefore
automatic: the theorems
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQRetainedOrder.Q_old_old_condII}
and
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQRetainedOrder.Q_old_old_condIII}
pull them directly from the original forward-skew source
via the checked rank-preservation equation.

Furthermore each retained old interior vertex is at
or before the maximal source cut in forward auxiliary
order, whereas every new projected marker is strictly
outside that inclusive cut. Totality and transitivity
give \lean{DualTree.ForwardQRetainedOrder.Q_old_before_new}
and its ambient-length consequence
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQRetainedOrder.Q_old_new_length_le}.
These six declarations passed Lean CI and the transitive axiom audit
(PR~\#189; 775 expected checks on the clean main branch). They solve the entire old/old part of skew
clauses~(ii)--(iii), and the weak old-to-new length
comparison, without imposing any additional local
point conditions on the mixed-product input.

The remaining Q numerical obligations concern
new/new comparisons at equal and unequal intrinsic
ranks, strict old/new rank separation, and proving
the necessary absence of lexicographically reversed
equal-rank mixed pairs. In particular, the weak
ambient-length comparison proved here cannot
replace the strict inequality required by clause~(iii).

\paragraph{A verified obstruction in the literal $Q$-tree reconstruction.} \bad
The source-facing Lean counterexample
\lean{DualTree.QLengthActualSkewFailure}
uses an actual starred binary source, a $3$-complete skew ambient
support, the literal marker-to-$D_2$ coordinate map, and the
canonical projections on their certified intrinsic domains.
The mixed-product element assigns the two marker points local
tails of lengths one and zero.  The resulting projected nodes
have ambient lengths two and one, respectively, but both have
intrinsic height one \emph{inside the reconstructed} $S_w$.
The left projected node extends $0$ and the right extends $1$,
so the longer node is lexicographically earlier. Thus clause~(ii)
of the printed skew definition fails:
\[
  \operatorname{condIIB}(S_w)=\mathrm{false},\qquad
  \operatorname{semiCompleteB}(S_w)=\mathrm{false}.
\]
The checked theorem
\lean{DualTree.QLengthActualSkewFailure.no_starred_Q_output}
also shows that no choice of a word component can make this exact
$S_w$ the support of a starred object.
All seven source-specific declarations passed the Lean build and
transitive axiom audit on the stacked branch (PR~\#146, whose
clean integration was merged), bringing the cumulative audit to
535 endpoints.

This is a concrete obstruction to the claim in the proof on
pp.~28--29 that the displayed $Q$ is automatically a
well-defined map into starred objects for arbitrary independent
$D_2$ point coordinates. It is \emph{not}, on its own, a
counterexample to the conclusion of Lemma~27 subject to the
printed large-parameter bound, nor to the dual-tree Ramsey
theorem: the fully checked example has complete height $3$,
and the complete observable-signature parameter package for
the theorem statement has not yet been formalised in this example.

\emph{Suggested local repair to investigate.}
The mixed-product definition permits marked coordinates from
$b^{<n'}$ without a common depth. It is therefore insufficient
to rely solely on preservation of prefix order and branching
directions under marker replacement. A candidate is to force
all new projected terminal points to occupy a common intrinsic
level in the ambient complete skew support $T$, with
coordinate-dependent local tail depths when the frontier roots
have unequal heights. The resulting restriction would have to
be made compatible with Corollary~22, the colouring pullback,
and the recursive dimension bound. Neither sufficiency nor
Ramsey compatibility is claimed. An alternative correction
may be needed if this fails.

\paragraph{The literal signature skeleton is ambient meet-closed.} \todo
The generic boundary-marker meet lemma is now instantiated with
the \emph{literal} set $R$ of Lemma~27.  In the semi-complete
source support $S$, every non-leaf—including the distinguished
maximal interior cut $t_0$—has one support successor in each
ambient direction.  Hence every marker of $R$, including each
child $t_0^\frown i$, has an extension inside $S$:
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27LiteralMeet.literalMarker_has_original_support_descendant}.
The already checked inclusive-cut boundary properties give
pairwise incomparability of markers and show that no marker
is a prefix of an original interior node.
It follows from the generic theorem that the ambient meet
of any two distinct nodes of
$\operatorname{Int}(S)\cup R$ belongs to $\operatorname{Int}(S)$,
by
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27LiteralMeet.distinct_literal_skeleton_meet_in_original_interior}.
In particular,
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27LiteralMeet.oldSkeleton_meetClosed}
certifies meet-closure of
$\operatorname{Int}(S')\cup\{t_0\}\cup R$.
This proves the source-side meet-closure obligation directly from
the starred semi-complete tree, with no new axioms.
The remaining step is to show that the canonical projected
replacement preserves the \emph{actual ambient meets}, not only
the prefix-poset structure.  The verified first-direction
preservation from each retained original interior node is
expected to supply the missing geometric invariant.



\paragraph{Generic meet-closure transfer through terminal markers.} \todo
The module \lean{DualTree.InteriorMarkerMeet} proves a reusable
finite-tree transport lemma.  Suppose $S$ is ambient meet-closed,
