The module verifies the root identity $P_t(\varnothing)=t$,
prefix preservation, cone containment, the rank equation
$h_T(P_t(z))=h_T(t)+|z|$, and exact inverse coordinates
$I_T^{-1}(P_t(z))=I_T^{-1}(t)^\frown z$ on that domain.
The last assertion is
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalConeCoordinates.inverse_project_tail}.
These results are stronger than the preceding abstract projection
algebra, but \emph{only} for tails with intrinsic length budget
$|I_T^{-1}(t)|+|z|<k$.  The original Lemma~27 uses an ambient
parameter $N$ and longer projected cone words. Its full domains,
the resulting mixed-product dimension, and the coding map $Q$
remain to be repaired and validated separately.


\paragraph{Padding an antichain of frontier addresses.} \todo
A route to the still outstanding $d\le b^{m'+1}$ estimate is to
transfer the antichain of minimal outside-cut frontiers to their
canonical inverse addresses and then pad each address to level
$m'+1$.  The module \lean{DualTree.FrontierAntichainPadding}
verifies the coding principle: padding by a fixed direction gives
a word at the requested length whenever the source length is
bounded, and two original addresses with equal padded words
must be prefix-comparable.  Consequently
\lean{DualTree.FrontierAntichainPadding.pad_injective_on_antichain}
gives an injection on any finite prefix antichain.  This uses
no assumptions on the support beyond the antichain property.
To finish the numerical estimate we still need to transport the
actual inclusive-cut frontier list through the canonical inverse,
instantiate the intrinsic-height bound, select a padding direction
(or treat the nullary case separately), and count the
$b^{m'+1}$ full-level words.


\paragraph{Specialization of padding to actual frontiers.} \todo
The module \lean{DualTree.FrontierSourcePadding} connects the abstract
padding argument with the actual minimal outside-cut frontiers of
a complete skew support.  The result
\lean{DualTree.FrontierSourcePadding.inverse_frontier_rank_bound}
derives $|I_T^{-1}(f)|\le m'+1$ from the verified frontier-height
bound.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.FrontierSourcePadding.inverse_frontier_antichain}
transports the frontier antichain through the canonical isomorphism.
Consequently all padded inverse addresses have length $m'+1$
and are collision-free, by
\lean{DualTree.FrontierSourcePadding.padded_frontier_length}
and \lean{DualTree.FrontierSourcePadding.padded_frontier_injective}.
The final numerical inequality $d\le b^{m'+1}$ still requires
collecting these maps over the exact duplicate-free frontier list,
showing $b>0$ (or handling $b=0$), and counting full-level words.
No cardinality inequality is claimed merely from the padding lemmas.


\paragraph{Numerical cardinality of the complete-skew frontier.} \todo
The theorem \lean{DualTree.FrontierCardinality.frontiers_length_le_pow}
now establishes the exact inequality
\[
  d=|\operatorname{Frontier}_T(t_0)|\le b^{m'+1}
\]
for the duplicate-free list of minimal support frontiers beyond
the inclusive auxiliary cut, whenever $T$ is $k$-complete,
$h_T(t_0)=m'$, $m'+1<k$, and $b>0$.
Each frontier is encoded by its inverse canonical address,
padded to length $m'+1$. The previous source-frontier antichain
theorem proves the code injective, and the standard finite-list
cardinality theorem bounds the set of full-level words.
The auxiliary lemmas
\lean{DualTree.FrontierCardinality.paddedSource_injective_frontiers}
and \lean{DualTree.FrontierCardinality.paddedSource_frontier_length}
certify the two source-facing ingredients separately.

This discharges the numerical $d$-bound under the usual positive
branching hypothesis. The literal $R$-marker injection additionally
gives $|R|\le d$, but neither numerical fact yet constructs the
mixed-product colouring map $Q$, repairs the printed larger
cone-tail domain, or proves the monochromatic reconstruction.


\paragraph{Explicit coordinates for literal signature markers.} \todo
The cardinal inequality $|R|\le d$ alone does not determine which
of the $d$ mixed-product coordinates correspond to the elements of
the paper's literal marker set $R$.  The module
\lean{DualTree.LiteralMarkerCoordinates} fixes the numbering: a marker
$s\in R$ is sent to the index in the duplicate-free frontier list
of the unique minimal outside-cut support node extending $s$.
The construction uses the canonical equivalence between positions of
a duplicate-free list and its members.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.LiteralMarkerCoordinates.coordinate_frontier}
recovers the frontier from its assigned index, while
\lean{DualTree.LiteralMarkerCoordinates.coordinate_injective}
shows that distinct markers receive distinct coordinates.
These facts establish the collision-free choice of the $D_2$
coordinates required at the beginning of Lemma~27, but not the
coordinatewise coding of the mixed-product word $Q$ or its smoothness
compatibility.

\paragraph{Concrete canonical cones in the root-aligned word decoder.} \todo
The module \lean{DualTree.CanonicalConeWordTransport} instantiates
the previously abstract projection map in the cone-word lift with the
verified canonical successor walk of a complete skew support.
Its total extension
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalConeWordTransport.totalProjection}
sends the empty address to the actual frontier, preserves prefixes,
and remains in the ambient prefix cone.  On intrinsically safe tails,
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalConeWordTransport.totalProjection_eq_project}
identifies it with the certified support-valued cone projection.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalConeWordTransport.lifted_root_prefix}
now proves the needed prefix alignment of every variable decoded from
the enlarged alphabet without separately postulating geometric
hypotheses for the canonical map.  The corresponding substitution
alignment appears in
\lean{DualTree.CanonicalConeWordTransport.source_root_alignment},
conditional only on the explicitly stated identification of local
addresses with the old source variable roots and the actual cone code.
These results are a bridge from the verified $I_T$ to the
root-aligned reconstruction, not a validation of overlong cone tails
or a construction of the global smooth-colouring-compatible map $Q$.

\paragraph{The induced $D_0/D_2$ partition of frontier coordinates.} \todo
The indexing map from literal signature markers into frontier coordinates
determines a concrete mixed-product coordinate kind:
\lean{DualTree.LiteralMarkerProductKind.kind} assigns kind $D_2$
(\texttt{bullet}) exactly to indices in the image of the injection,
and kind $D_0$ (\texttt{plain}) elsewhere; no $D_1$
(\texttt{up}) coordinates are introduced at this stage.
The generic
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.bulletKindOfRange_iff} proves the
membership characterization, and
\lean{DualTree.LiteralMarkerProductKind.literalMarker_bullet_bijective}
checks that the literal markers biject with the resulting bullet indices.
This finishes the proposed coordinate selection and its inverse
on the marked coordinates. It does not yet define the elements of the
mixed product, the induced colouring, or the coding map $Q$.

\paragraph{Assembling typed mixed-product elements from marker data.} \todo
The chosen $D_2$ indices can be inverted without ambiguity, since
the literal markers are bijective with the bullet-coordinate subtype.
The module \lean{DualTree.MixedElementFromMarkers} uses this inversion
to construct a mixed-product element from (i) a word in every frontier
coordinate and (ii) a bounded marked point for each literal marker.
The kind function introduces no $D_1$ coordinates at this stage.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.LiteralMarkerMixedElement.assemble_marker_point}
shows that the constructed element carries exactly the specified
marker point at its designated $D_2$ coordinate.
This removes the indexing and dependent-type obstacle in the
definition of the map $Q$, but the words themselves must still be
constructed from the starred input and the compatibility of the
induced colouring with smoothness must be established.

\paragraph{Actual support-valued projections for one shortened common cone height.} \todo
The repaired bound on the intrinsic height of every minimal outside-cut
frontier now yields a single well-typed local address domain:
$b^{<\,k-(m'+1)}$ when the ambient complete support has intrinsic
height $k$ and the cut height is $m'$.
The constructor \lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.commonTail}
maps every such local address into the exact source-tail domain
at any frontier without adding an independent length assumption.
The resulting
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectCommon} has values in $T$,
preserves initial segments, stays within its frontier cone, advances
the intrinsic height by the local address length, and has the exact
inverse concatenation property. These assertions are checked by
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectCommon_prefix},
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectCommon_cone},
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectCommon_height}, and
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectCommon_inverse}.
Thus the corrected common cone domain is now certified for the
*actual* canonical support map. Whether shortening the local
mixed-product dimension this way still leaves enough levels for
the final Ramsey refinement is an independent, unresolved
numerical problem in Lemma~27.


\paragraph{Retaining the printed tail height with one more complete level.} \todo
There is a second possible domain repair which avoids shortening the
local Hales--Jewett words.  If the complete support has intrinsic
height $n+1$ rather than $n$, then the frontier-height estimate
$h_T(t_i)\le m'+1$ makes every original tail
$z\in b^{<n-m'}$ fit inside the canonical source domain:
$h_T(t_i)+|z|<n+1$.
The constructor \lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.extraLevelTail}
certifies this with the actual canonical inverse addresses, while
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectExtraLevel_prefix} and
\lean{DualTree.UniformConeDomains.projectExtraLevel_inverse}
verify the corresponding projection's prefix and inverse-coordinate
properties.  This is a precise alternative to the one-level
shortening, but a complete Lemma~27 repair must still check how
increasing the complete-support parameter affects the recursive
height bound, the ambient word length, and the final refinement.

\paragraph{Selecting the root of a complete skew support.} \todo
The theorem \lean{DualTree.CompleteSupportRoot.exists_root}
constructs a common support root from the complete-skew axioms,
treating the source's singleton convention separately.
The map \lean{DualTree.CompleteSupportRoot.rootOf} makes an explicit
choice of this root; its support membership and prefix property
are certified by \lean{DualTree.CompleteSupportRoot.rootOf_mem}
and \lean{DualTree.CompleteSupportRoot.rootOf_prefix}.
The resulting rooted map
\lean{DualTree.CompleteSupportRoot.canonicalWalk}
sends the empty address to that root, preserves initial segments,
and sends every address with a valid support-successor trace
into $T$.  The corresponding cone projections preserve prefixes,
by \lean{DualTree.CompleteSupportRoot.canonicalProjection_prefix}.
The remaining substantial tasks are to discharge trace admissibility
for \emph{all} addresses in the full $b^{<k}$, prove the
intrinsic-height equation $h_T(I_T(u))=|u|$, and verify
injectivity, surjectivity onto $T$, and the inverse addresses used
by Lemma~27.


\paragraph{Counting literal frontier coordinates.} \todo
The literal signature marker set $R$ injects into the distinct
minimal support frontiers beyond the inclusive cut, by
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierIndex.frontierFor_injective}.
The module \lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierCount} constructs the
duplicate-free frontier list and certifies its precise membership
predicate.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierCount.literalR_length_le_frontiers}
proves $|R|\le d$, and
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierCount.frontier_partition_count}
justifies $d_2=|R|$, $d_0=d-d_2$ and $d_0+d_2=d$.
This does not prove the separately required bound
$d\le b^{m'+1}$, the actual coordinate enumeration, or the
coloring map $Q$.

\paragraph{Safe domains for the cone projections.} \todo
The common tail height $n'=n-m'$ printed in Lemma~27
does not always fit the canonical frontier address:
a frontier may have intrinsic height $m'+1$.
\lean{DualTree.ConeTailDomains.common_cone_tail_safe}
checks the uniformly shortened tail height $n-(m'+1)$,
provided that the canonical address of the frontier has
length equal to its intrinsic height.
\lean{DualTree.ConeTailDomains.variable_frontier_tail_safe}
also checks the individual tail height $n-h_T(t_i)$
in each cone.  These results establish domain inequalities,
not the existence of $I_T$ or the numerical feasibility of
the full mixed-product construction with the altered
common tail height.


\paragraph{Injection of the literal markers into frontier coordinates.} \todo
The opening observation of Lemma~27 requires not only that every
$s\in R$ belongs to a unique frontier cone, but that no frontier cone
contains more than one such marker.  The chosen frontier is now a
source-facing map
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierIndex.frontierFor}
on the subtype of nodes in the \emph{literal} $R$.
The theorems
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierIndex.frontierFor_spec} and
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierIndex.frontierFor_eq_of_frontier}
identify it with the unique minimal support node beyond the
inclusive auxiliary cut.  Moreover,
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierIndex.frontierFor_injective}
proves this map injective under the same maximal-interior, support
inclusion, and early-level hypotheses.  This is the geometric
basis for choosing the $D_2$-coordinates in the mixed product
without conflating distinct signature markers.  The explicit
numbering of those coordinates and the repaired coding $Q$
are still \todo.

\paragraph{Early cuts, singleton supports and ambient bounds.} \ok
The hypotheses of the literal marker/frontier correspondence can be
simplified further.  If $h_T(t_0)+1<k$ in a $k$-complete support,
the cut cannot lie in a singleton support:
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierCleanup.nonsingleton_of_early_level}.
Consequently
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierCleanup.literalR_unique_frontier}
needs no separate nonsingleton assumption.

There is also a minor ambient-domain obligation in the printed definition
of $R$: $\operatorname{ImSucc}_{b^{<N}}(t_0)$ refers only to children inside
the ambient tree, whereas the intermediate Lean candidate list uses
all $b$ directions.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.LiteralFrontierCleanup.all_children_bounded_of_early_level}
proves that all children $t_0^\frown i$ do lie in $b^{<N}$
whenever $T\subseteq b^{<N}$ and $t_0$ is below the final intrinsic
level.  It extracts for each direction a bounded support descendant.
Hence this part of the candidate-list construction does not introduce
extraneous ambient nodes.

\paragraph{Persistence of signature interiors at the maximal cut.} \ok
The condition \lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.InteriorPersists}
has now been derived directly from Definition~26 at a maximal interior
cut.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.SignatureInteriorPersistence.interiorPersists_of_maxInterior}
takes an old interior node $s\ne t_0$ and an immediate successor $u$
in the starred tree.  If $u$ remains interior, it is retained in the
signature tree.  Otherwise $u$ must be an exceptional leaf, because
a leaf below $t_0$ could not be an immediate successor of an earlier
interior node.  Its first signature boundary is then a strict
descendant of $s$ in $S'$, proving that $s$ remains interior.
The fully source-facing version
\lean{DualTree.SignatureInteriorPersistence.interiorPersists_at_maxInterior}
uses the previously verified maximal-interior leaf-boundary lemma,
without introducing an extra existence assumption.

Consequently
\lean{DualTree.SignatureInteriorPersistence.literalR_unique_frontier_of_maxInterior}
establishes existence and uniqueness of the inclusive-cut support frontier
for \emph{every node in the paper's literal $R$}, under the remaining
complete-support inclusion and intrinsic-height hypotheses.
The variable coding, smooth-coloring verification, and final
Hales--Jewett lift in Lemma~27 are still \todo.

\paragraph{Literal marker set $R$ and uniqueness in each cone.} \todo
Page~28 of the source defines
\[
R=\bigl(S'\setminus(\operatorname{Int}(S')\cup\{t_0\})\bigr)
  \cup\operatorname{ImSucc}(t_0).
\]
This is \emph{not definitionally} the earlier list of exceptional-leaf
boundaries plus the immediate children of $t_0$.
\lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.literalR} records the literal formula,
while \lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.mem_signatureTree_iff}
unpacks the signature skeleton as old interior points plus leaf boundaries.

The conditional lemma
\lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.literalR_subset_coneMarkers}
shows that all literal $R$ markers are candidates, provided every old
interior node other than $t_0$ remains interior to $S'$.
This persistence property is made explicit as
\lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.InteriorPersists} and is now proved
at maximal interior cuts, as recorded above.
It is unnecessary to establish equality of the two marker lists.

The theorems
\lean{DualTree.MarkerAntichain.eq_of_prefix_boundaries} and
\lean{DualTree.MarkerAntichain.unique_candidate_marker_in_cone}
show that first-exit nodes from the inclusive cut form a prefix
antichain; hence \emph{at most one} candidate marker can lie below
any given frontier node.  The same uniqueness and support-frontier
existence for the \emph{literal} $R$ are reduced to interior persistence
by \lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.literalR_unique_marker_in_cone}
and \lean{DualTree.LiteralSignatureR.literalR_unique_inclusive_frontier}.
The remainder of Lemma~27 is not validated by these reductions.

\paragraph{Full branching below the last intrinsic level.} \ok
The earlier signature-marker argument required locating a later
full-branching witness $w$ and proving $h_T(t_0)<h_T(w)$.  That
numerical witness-height detour is no longer needed.
\lean{DualTree.CompleteInteriorBranching.nonleaf_of_height_lt}
proves directly that a node $s$ with $h_T(s)+1<k$ in a $k$-complete
skew tree is not a leaf.  Clause~(iv) and completeness then force
one support successor in each direction:
\lean{DualTree.CompleteInteriorBranching.fullDirections_of_height_lt_paper}.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.CompleteInteriorBranching.coneMarker_descendant_of_height_lt}
establishes support descendants for every candidate cone marker, and
\lean{DualTree.CompleteInteriorBranching.coneMarker_unique_frontier_of_complete_height}
gives the unique inclusive-cut frontier directly, assuming only the
natural inclusion $S\subseteq T$, membership $t_0\in T$, nonsingleton
support, and $h_T(t_0)+1<k$.
The identification of candidate markers with the paper's exact $R$,
and the variable-word coding in Lemma~27, remain \todo.

\paragraph{The source's inclusive cut and marker/frontier correspondence.} \ok
The prefix-minimal frontier points $t_i$ in the paper are taken beyond
the \emph{inclusive} initial segment $\{t\in T:t\preccurlyeq t_0\}$,
not beyond the strict cut used for the signature boundary in
Definition~26.  Those two frontiers are distinct notions.  In
particular, simply transferring the earlier strict-cut uniqueness
statement to Lemma~27 would not be justified.

The source-matching boundary predicate is
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveFrontier.PaperCutBoundary}.
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveFrontier.unique_frontier_of_boundary}
shows that such a boundary has a unique support frontier beyond the
inclusive cut whenever it has a support descendant and the support
is ambient meet-closed.  For complete skew supports, the meet-closure
assumption is discharged by
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveFrontier.unique_frontier_complete_paper}.

Both kinds of candidate nodes in $R$ are proven to be these inclusive-cut
boundaries:
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveSignatureMarkers.child_paperCutBoundary}
for the immediate successors of $t_0$, and
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveSignatureMarkers.boundaryMarker_paperCutBoundary}
for exceptional signature-leaf boundaries.  Consequently,
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveSignatureMarkers.boundaryMarker_unique_frontier}
gives unconditional existence and uniqueness for exceptional-leaf markers
under the natural support inclusion $S\subseteq T$.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.InclusiveSignatureMarkers.coneMarker_unique_frontier_of_height_lt}
handles \emph{all} candidate markers, including immediate children,
provided the cut has smaller intrinsic height than a later full-branching
witness.  The witness-height formula and the identification of this list
with the paper's literal set $R$ remain \todo, as does the variable coding.



\paragraph{Unconditional meet closure for complete skew supports.} \ok
\lean{DualTree.finLexLE_total} proves totality of finite lexicographic
comparison.  Its specializations
\lean{DualTree.SkewMeetInstantiation.paperAuxB_total} and
\lean{DualTree.SkewMeetInstantiation.forwardAuxB_total}
discharge the sole auxiliary-order assumption of the complete-skew
branching theorem.  The resulting theorems
\lean{DualTree.SkewMeetInstantiation.meetClosed_complete_paper} and
\lean{DualTree.SkewMeetInstantiation.meetClosed_complete_forward}
establish ambient meet closure for \emph{all} complete skew supports,
including singleton supports, in both order conventions.
\lean{DualTree.SkewMeetInstantiation.unique_frontier_complete_paper}
and its forward-lex counterpart eliminate the cone-meet hypothesis in
the corresponding conditional signature-boundary frontier lemma.
These facts do not repair the separate canonical-order-preservation
counterexample, nor do they prove the entirety of Lemma~27:
coverage of the paper's exact marker set $R$, the necessary support
descendants, and the actual variable coding remain to be verified.

\paragraph{Complete-skew directional uniqueness.} \todo
The new theorem
\lean{DualTree.CompleteSkewMeet.atMostOneDirection_of_complete_total}
derives directional uniqueness directly from the executable
\lean{SkewTree.completeB} axioms, assuming only comparability of distinct
support nodes in the chosen auxiliary order.  It uses the full-before and
empty-after parts of clause~(iv), and proves that the partial-branching
parameter at the final witness exhausts all $b$ directions in a complete
support.  Combined with finite ascent,
\lean{DualTree.CompleteSkewMeet.meetClosed_of_complete_total}
establishes ambient meet closure of every nonsingleton complete skew support
under this order comparability hypothesis.  Both order conventions now
satisfy the requisite comparability, by the unconditional theorems below.

\paragraph{Meet closure from directional uniqueness.} \todo
The finite-ascent theorem
\lean{DualTree.MeetClosedFromBranching.meetClosed_of_rooted_uniqueDirections}
derives ambient meet closure of any rooted finite support from uniqueness
of the immediate support successor in each ambient direction.
The supporting lemmas show that whenever a support node properly prefixes
the ambient meet of two points, a longer common support prefix exists.
This directional uniqueness is now derived from complete-skew
condition~(iv), without introducing it as an axiom.

\paragraph{Immediate support successors and direction uniqueness.} \todo
The support path construction
\lean{DualTree.SkewBranchGeometry.exists_first_immediate_on_path}
shows that every proper support descendant has an actual immediate
support successor on its path, without assuming prefix-closure.
The Boolean predicates for strict prefix, immediate successor, and
directional witness are compared with their propositional meanings.
The fact
\lean{DualTree.SkewBranchGeometry.eq_of_mem_uniqueBranch}
extracts uniqueness of two successors from the corresponding
\lean{SkewTree.uniqueBranchB} hypothesis.  These facts are now used in
the complete-skew meet-closure proof above.



\paragraph{Ambient meets and cone uniqueness.} \todo
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.commonPrefix} is the actual longest common
initial segment of two ambient nodes.  The source-facing lemmas
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.commonPrefix_prefix_left},
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.commonPrefix_prefix_right}, and
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.prefix_commonPrefix} establish its defining
universal property.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.coneMeetClosed_of_meetClosed} proves that
ordinary meet-closure of $T$ implies the cone-meet condition used
to derive a unique outside frontier root in each signature cone.
The converse
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.meetClosed_of_coneMeetClosed}
proves that the cone-meet condition is actually equivalent to ambient
meet closure, not a weaker substitute.  The equivalence is
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.coneMeetClosed_iff_meetClosed}.
The source-facing consequence
\lean{DualTree.MeetGeometry.unique_frontier_of_boundary_of_meetClosed}
packages Lemma~27 frontier uniqueness directly under meet closure.

This reduces the remaining structural part to proving that a complete
skew support is closed under ambient meets.  No such consequence is
being assumed without proof.

\paragraph{Uniqueness from the support meet property.} \todo
There is a further simplification of the uniqueness part of the
signature/frontier correspondence.  Say that a finite support $T$ is
\emph{cone-meet-closed} if any two support nodes extending an ambient
node $s$ have a common support predecessor extending $s$.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ConeFrontier.unique_frontier_in_outside_cone}
shows that two distinct minimal outside-support roots cannot both extend
the same node outside the cut: their common support predecessor would
contradict the minimality of the roots.
The stronger
\lean{DualTree.ConeFrontier.unique_frontier_of_signature_boundary}
combines this with the previously verified boundary/frontier path lemma,
and proves that a signature boundary lies below exactly one outside
frontier \emph{provided} it has some support descendant and $T$ is
cone-meet-closed.

This reduces the printed assertion to two independently checkable
requirements: complete skew supports must satisfy the cone-meet property,
and the particular nodes $s\in R$ must have support descendants.
Neither requirement is inferred merely from the existence of an ambient
path boundary.  Both remain \todo.

\paragraph{Existence of support descendants for boundary markers.} \todo
The paper's set $R$ includes boundary markers from exceptional leaves
of the starred tree and the immediate ambient successors $t_0^\frown j$
of the distinguished node.
\lean{DualTree.SignatureMarker.boundaryMarker_has_support_descendant}
proves that every exceptional-leaf marker has a support descendant whenever
the original starred tree $S$ is contained in $T=\operatorname{ws}(f)$:
the leaf used to define its boundary is itself such a descendant.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.SignatureMarker.coneMarker_has_support_descendant}
extends this to the immediate-successor markers, assuming $T$ has a
support descendant in every direction from $t_0$.

The theorem
\lean{DualTree.DirectionalSupport.directional_descendants_of_fullBefore}
derives that directional branching condition whenever $t_0$ precedes
a later support node satisfying the \lean{SkewTree.fullBeforeB}
clause of the skew-tree definition.  It checks every branch
direction and extracts an actual support descendant from the
corresponding immediate-successor witness.
Consequently
\lean{DualTree.DirectionalSupport.coneMarker_has_descendant_of_fullBefore}
eliminates the abstract branch-existence hypothesis from the combined
signature-marker lemma under this precise geometric condition.

The intrinsic-level-to-order part of the remaining argument is now
formalized:
\lean{DualTree.SkewLevelOrder.length_lt_of_height_lt}
proves that two nodes of a nonsingleton skew support at different
intrinsic heights have strictly ordered ambient lengths, and
\lean{DualTree.SkewLevelOrder.paperAux_of_height_lt}
gives their order under the printed auxiliary convention.  The
forward-lex analogue is also proved.

The theorem
\lean{DualTree.SkewLevelOrder.coneMarker_has_descendant_of_height_lt}
now combines intrinsic-height ordering with the full-before property:
whenever the chosen cut has smaller intrinsic height than a later
fully branching witness, every associated signature cone marker has a
support descendant.  No separate auxiliary-order or distinctness
hypothesis is required in this version.  This is still conditional
on identifying the correct witness in the complete skew support.

For the parameters $m'<m-1$ of Lemma~27, it remains to identify the
final branching witness $w$ of the complete skew support and show
$h_T(t_0)<h_T(w)$.  This requires checking that $w$ is at
intrinsic height $n-2$ when $T$ is $n$-complete.  That numerical
height-of-witness statement is still \todo.
The marker list is also not yet proved to coincide without
repetitions with the literal set $R$ from Lemma~27.

\paragraph{Conditional signature-frontier correspondence.} \todo
The lemma
\lean{DualTree.SignatureFrontier.boundary_prefix_frontier_of_shared_descendant}
shows that if a signature boundary $s$ is the first point outside the cut,
and an outside support node $t$ extends $s$, then $s$ precedes the
frontier root of $t$; otherwise that root would contradict the minimality
of the boundary.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.SignatureFrontier.boundary_prefix_unique_frontier_of_support_extension}
gives the unique such frontier root \emph{on the path to a fixed support
descendant}.

These conditional statements do not yet imply the stronger assertion in
the printed Lemma~27 that every $s\in R$ lies below exactly one frontier
node $t_i$.  This requires proving the existence of suitable outside
support descendants for all signature boundaries and identifying their
frontier roots independently of the descendant chosen.  The missing
hypotheses are kept explicit.

\paragraph{Minimal frontier geometry.} \todo
Let $T=\operatorname{ws}(f)$ and cut at $t_0$ in the auxiliary order.
The first geometric step in the proof takes the prefix-minimal elements
of $T\setminus\{t\in T:t\preccurlyeq t_0\}$.  The formal theorem
\lean{DualTree.CutFrontier.paperCut_exists_unique_frontier}
proves, in fact for any finite node set $T$, that every node of this
complement lies above exactly one such minimal point.  The lemma
\lean{DualTree.CutFrontier.frontier_antichain} makes the pairwise
incomparability of these frontier roots explicit.  The same assertions
hold under the forward-lex repair.

This does \emph{not} establish the next claim in the printed proof:
every node of its auxiliary set $R$ lies below exactly one of these
frontier points.  Nodes of $R$ need not themselves belong to $T$, so
that additional signature-specific geometry remains \todo.

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item With $n'=n-m'$, the map
$P_i(z)=I_T(I_T^{-1}(t_i)^\frown z)$ can leave the domain of $I_T$ for
frontier nodes at the larger intrinsic height.  A common safe tail loses one
level, or the cones need different tail heights.
\item The requested surjection from $\{0,\ldots,m'\}$ to all interior
variables can be cardinally impossible and can assign incomparable variables
to a cone.  Only ancestor variables visible from the given frontier cone can
be legitimate targets.
\item The enlarged alphabet
$\Lambda'=\Lambda\sqcup\{0,\ldots,m'\}$ is introduced but is not consistently
used in the mixed-product domain.
\item The induced coloring is defined on the starred part, while Corollary 22
takes a coloring of the full mixed-product space.  A compatible extension is
not supplied.
\item The reconstruction tests the occurrence position $P_i(z)$ rather than
the root $P_i(y)$ of the variable $v_y$, so different occurrences of one
variable fibre can be frozen differently.
\item The default clause~(vi) tests $f'(s)=v_{P_i(z)}$ while defining $f'$.
This is circular and appears to mean $f(s)=v_{P_i(z)}$; the default must be
formulated using only previously fixed data.
\item The formula $R_i(w_i(z))$ is not well typed as written: $R_i$ accepts
auxiliary alphabet symbols $\{0,\ldots,m'\}$, whereas the condition
$w_i(z)\notin\Lambda$ also includes genuine variable symbols $v_y$.
A corrected construction needs separate cases for original letters,
auxiliary letters (decoded by $R_i$), and variables (whose roots are
transported into the corresponding cone).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Lean checks.}
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Audit.printed_projection_address_overflows}
certifies the smallest address overflow with a frontier node of intrinsic
depth $2$ in a height-$3$ tree, and
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Audit.safe_cone_tail} proves a sufficient one-level
tail shortening. The finite cardinal obstruction to an unrestricted onto map
is \lean{DualTree.Lemma27Audit.two_symbols_cannot_cover_three_variables};
the precise realizable ancestor target still needs formal identification.
The constructive alternative is made explicit in
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.visibleAncestors_length_le_height}:
for a frontier node $t_i$, any chosen list of visible ancestor variables
is a subset of $\operatorname{Pred}_T(t_i)$ and has cardinality at most the
intrinsic height $h_T(t_i)$.  If this height is at most $m'+1$ and the
visible set is nonempty,
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.visibleAncestors_auxiliary_coverage}
produces a surjection from the $m'+1$ auxiliary symbol indices onto its
enumerated positions.  This repairs the cardinality issue conditionally;
the actual matching of auxiliary symbols to the legal variables in every
cone still requires verification.
Finally, \lean{DualTree.Lemma27Audit.occurrence_based_freezing_not_substitution}
exhibits a repeated-variable fibre whose occurrences are split by a
position-based freezing rule; no uniform substitution can produce it.

These are checks of the \emph{construction}, not counterexamples to the
intended statement of Lemma~27.

\paragraph{Strict intrinsic ranks along support branches.} \todo
The eventual decoder-height bound requires the intrinsic heights of
successive support predecessors to be strictly increasing.
\lean{DualTree.SupportHeightRanks.heightAt_lt_of_strictPrefix}
proves this without skewness or completeness assumptions, by
comparing the proper-prefix filters.  The theorem
\lean{DualTree.SupportHeightRanks.heightAt_injective_on_common_path}
shows that two support nodes on one ambient branch with the same
intrinsic height must coincide.  This is the rank input for proving
that any minimal support point beyond a cut of intrinsic height $m'$
has height at most $m'+1$.  This cardinality argument is now completed
by \lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.frontier_height_le_cut_succ}.

\paragraph{Cone-local decoder for auxiliary letters.} \todo
The unrestricted map $R_i:\{0,\ldots,m'\}\to
\{v_t:t\in\operatorname{Int}(S_w)\}$ cannot be used in the
printed construction: its codomain can be too large and contain
incomparable variables.  The repaired decoder instead targets
\emph{only} the interior roots properly preceding the frontier $t_i$.
\lean{DualTree.ConeLocalDecoder.visibleAncestor_spec}
certifies both the membership and proper-prefix requirements for
those roots.  The noncomputable
\lean{DualTree.ConeLocalDecoder.decode} is a total map from the
$m'+1$ auxiliary labels to either a fixed fallback letter or a
\emph{visible ancestor index}.  If the target list is nonempty and
$h_T(t_i)\le m'+1$, then
\lean{DualTree.ConeLocalDecoder.decode_onto_visible}
proves that every such index occurs; if it is empty,
\lean{DualTree.ConeLocalDecoder.decode_empty} verifies that all
auxiliary labels decode to the fallback letter.  Finally,
\lean{DualTree.ConeLocalDecoder.decodeRoot_prefix} shows that
any emitted variable root is a strict prefix of the frontier.
The bound $h_T(t_i)\le m'+1$ is now supplied by
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.frontier_height_le_m_succ};
integration with the full coloring $Q$ remains \todo.

\paragraph{Intrinsic height of minimal outside-cut frontiers.} \ok
The source-facing theorem
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.frontier_height_le_cut_succ}
proves that every minimal support point $t_i$ beyond the inclusive
auxiliary cut at $t_0$ satisfies
\[
h_T(t_i)\le h_T(t_0)+1.
\]
Indeed every proper support predecessor $r$ of $t_i$ lies at or before
$t_0$; clause~(iii) of skewness implies
$h_T(r)\le h_T(t_0)$, while distinct predecessors have distinct
intrinsic heights.  The cardinality lemma then bounds the total number
of predecessors.  This does not require choosing a canonical
enumeration of the frontier.

For a complete skew support,
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.frontier_height_le_complete}
removes the separate nonsingleton assumption.  In particular, if
$h_T(t_0)=m'$, then the required $h_T(t_i)\le m'+1$ follows from
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.frontier_height_le_m_succ}.
The constructor
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.decodeAtFrontier}
can therefore use exactly $m'+1$ auxiliary symbols, with no additional
frontier-height assumption.  Its
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.decodeAtFrontier_onto_visible}
surjectivity conclusion now follows unconditionally from the
complete-skew frontier geometry when the target list is nonempty.
The actual reconstruction of the $Q$-coloring and of the final
complete support remains \todo.

\paragraph{Bounded intrinsic-rank counting.} \todo
The elementary counting principle
\lean{DualTree.RankCardinality.nodup_length_le_of_rank_bound}
states that a duplicate-free finite list carrying distinct ranks
in $\{0,\ldots,m\}$ has length at most $m+1$.  In Lemma~27 this is
intended for the list of proper support predecessors of a frontier
node, with the intrinsic height as its rank.  Establishing the
necessary rank bound for every such predecessor from the inclusive
cut and skew level separation is proved in
\lean{DualTree.FrontierHeightBound.frontier_height_le_cut_succ}.

\paragraph{Uniform ancestor-root substitution.} \todo
The root-masking construction only freezes discarded variables to
letters; the reconstruction of $f'$ in Lemma~27 can also need to
identify several old variable fibres with one surviving root.
The more general constructor
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.substituteRoots}
does this by a single substitution on \emph{source variable roots}.
It requires that selected roots stay fixed and that every surviving
target variable be rooted at a prefix of the corresponding old root.
These hypotheses suffice to produce a legal tree-variable word.
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.substituteRoots_span_subset}
proves syntactic refinement and evaluated span containment for
every alphabet, and
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.substituteRoots_support}
proves exact preservation of the chosen support.  Different
occurrences of one source variable are treated identically, so the
printed occurrence-based freezing error cannot arise.
Constructing the particular substitution from the cone words $f_i$
and verifying its alignment and selected complete support remain
\todo.

\paragraph{Complete-support reconstruction from a root substitution.} \todo
The source-facing constructor
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.reconstructComplete} packages a
uniform root-aligned substitution as a genuine $k$-variable word
provided the chosen roots form a $k$-complete skew support.
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.reconstructComplete_supportNodes}
verifies that the selected roots are exactly its support, and
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.reconstructComplete_syntacticRefines}
shows that it is a syntactic refinement of the original word,
including for unary alphabets.  Thus
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.reconstructComplete_refines}
gives the required inclusion of evaluated spans.
Furthermore, if all original roots at or before the auxiliary cut
are selected, then
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.reconstructComplete_prefixWord_eq}
proves that the word component of Definition~26's signature is
preserved exactly.  This closes the algebraic \emph{interface} for
the final $f'$ in Lemma~27, but not the construction of the
particular selected complete support or the cone-wise substitution.

\paragraph{Typed decoding of the three kinds of cone symbols.} \todo
The source's formula for $g_w$ tests whether a symbol is outside
$\Lambda$ before applying $R_i$.  This conflates auxiliary letters
with genuine variables. In the repaired encoding these are three
distinct constructors: original alphabet letters, elements of the
disjoint auxiliary alphabet, and variables of the cone word.
The maps \lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.liftSymbol} and
\lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.liftWord}
give a total decoding by exhaustive cases.
The corresponding constructor lemmas verify their behavior;
\lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.liftWord_variable_fibre} shows that
repeated occurrences of a cone variable receive identical symbols.
The evaluation identity
\lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.eval_liftWord} makes the enlarged
alphabet and transport maps explicit.

At a frontier, auxiliary letters are decoded using the visible
ancestor list, independently of transported genuine cone variables.
The theorem
\lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.liftAtCone_aux_prefix}
proves that a decoded auxiliary variable is a proper prefix of
the frontier, while
\lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.liftAtCone_variable}
preserves the specified root of each genuine cone variable.
By the established frontier-height bound,
\lean{DualTree.ConeWordLift.liftAtFrontier}
requires no independent height assumption.
The assembly of these local words into the global map $Q$ and
the proof of its smooth-coloring compatibility remain \todo.

\paragraph{Algebra of the canonical cone projections.} \todo
The printed map $P_i(z)=I_T(I_T^{-1}(t_i)^\frown z)$
must preserve initial segments, send the empty tail to $t_i$,
and have all images in the cone above $t_i$.
The module \lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlgebra} verifies these
facts abstractly for any prefix-preserving map $I$ and a
source address $s$ satisfying $I(s)=t_i$.
In particular,
\lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlgebra.projection_prefix}
proves prefix preservation,
\lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlgebra.projection_rooted_at_frontier}
proves the cone condition, and
\lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlgebra.projection_leftInverse_root}
certifies the empty-tail identity for a left inverse.
This supplies the geometric hypotheses required by
\lean{DualTree.ConeRootAlignment.lifted_variable_root_prefix}
once an actual canonical skew-support embedding is available.

The construction and verification of $I_T$ for arbitrary complete
skew supports remain \todo.  In particular these abstract lemmas
must not be treated as proof that the printed $P_i$ is defined
on its full stated domain.

\paragraph{Cone projection root alignment.} \todo
The module \lean{DualTree.ConeRootAlignment} verifies the two
well-typed root-prefix properties of the decoded cone words under
explicit prefix preservation, cone containment, and source-address
identity hypotheses.  In particular the occurrence of an auxiliary
symbol cannot introduce a variable outside the visible ancestral
cone, and genuine local variables inherit the below-root condition
through the cone map.  The combined module
\lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlignment} discharges both geometric
hypotheses directly from the algebraic projection
$P(z)=I(s^\frown z)$ and $I(s)=t_i$.
The two endpoints
\lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlignment.lifted_root_prefix} and
\lean{DualTree.ConeProjectionAlignment.source_root_alignment}
respectively certify the local and source-root versions.
Still missing: construction of $I_T$ on a corrected finite domain,
the identification of old variable-root addresses with their
images under $P_i$, the selection of a complete root set, and the
global coloring-compatible map $Q$.  No form of Lemma~27 is marked
as completely verified.

\paragraph{Partial repair in Lean.}
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.restrictSupport} takes a tree-variable word
and a duplicate-free subset of its variable roots and freezes all occurrences
of the discarded roots uniformly.  The theorems
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.restrictSupport_span_subset} and
\lean{DualTree.Lemma27Repair.restrictSupport_support} show that this is a
legal tree-variable word with exactly the selected roots and with its evaluated
span contained in the old one, for every alphabet.  This repairs the algebra
of occurrence-based freezing, but it does not yet verify the geometric
completeness of the selected skew support or the signature-transport map.

\paragraph{Partial smooth-coloring extension.}
The general classwise extension principle is isolated in
\lean{DualTree.PartialColoring.exists_equivalence_respecting_extension},
with necessity proved by
\lean{DualTree.PartialColoring.partial_coloring_compatible_of_extension}.
Thus a partial coloring on the starred domain extends to a total coloring
constant on smoothness equivalence classes exactly when it is already
constant on each class it meets.  This isolates, but does \emph{not} establish,
the missing proof obligation that the particular induced coloring in
Lemma~27 satisfies the required classwise compatibility.

\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.SmoothRelated} now expresses the actual
coordinatewise hypotheses of Definition~21, taking the inclusive successor
cone from Section~3.1.  The lemmas
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.smoothRelated_refl},
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.smoothRelated_symm}, and
\lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.smoothRelated_trans} prove that these conditions
form an equivalence relation for each partition $D_2=\Gamma_1\sqcup\Gamma_2$.
The theorem \lean{DualTree.MixedProduct.exists_smooth_extension}
instantiates the general extension principle for this relation.

What is still missing is a proof that the particular induced coloring
$c'=c\circ Q$ of Lemma~27 is constant on every smoothness class intersecting
its starred domain.  That compatibility, and the construction of $Q$ itself,
must be verified before the printed application of Corollary~22 is justified.

The final inclusion of every continuation of the fixed signature in the
monochromatic image remains unproved.  The local coding lemma must still be
reconstructed with typed domains.

\paragraph{Intrinsic rank of all forward-corrected $Q$ projected leaves.} \todo
The modules \lean{ForwardQExceptionalRanks},
\lean{ForwardQCutChildRank}, and \lean{ForwardQMarkerRankBand}
separate intrinsic $Q$ rank from ambient word length for the two
genuine literal marker families. For any exceptional source leaf $t$,
its first outside-cut boundary has the same old-interior strict
predecessors as $t$. The actual projected point has rank
\[
  h_Q(P(t))=h_S(t)\in\{h_S(t_0),h_S(t_0)+1\}.
\]
For each literal child marker $t_0^\frown i$, its strict old-interior
predecessors are precisely the strict predecessors of $t_0$ and
$t_0$ itself, giving
\[
  h_Q(P(t_0^\frown i))=h_S(t_0)+1.
\]
The genuine sorted marker-to-$D_2$ bijection therefore yields
\[
  h_Q(P_j(s_j))\in\{h_S(t_0),h_S(t_0)+1\}
\]
for \emph{every} actual bullet index $j$, independent of the local
bullet-tail length. The first exceptional-boundary proof reuses the
already checked generic strict-prefix extension lemma; another
arithmetic proof explicitly normalizes the first-boundary alias
to avoid an \texttt{omega} syntactic mismatch found by CI.

These statements concern the explicitly repaired \emph{forward}
auxiliary order only; they do not repair the printed reverse-order
construction. In particular, equal-rank new--new comparisons,
strict mixed-rank old--new inequalities, and the word/colouring
compatibility of $Q$ remain open. Seven new axioms are added to the
transitive audit. This entry remains \textbf{PENDING} until the
integrated Lean build and no-\texttt{sorryAx} CI check pass.

\paragraph{Strict old--new skew comparisons for cut-child projections.} \todo
The reconstructed Q has two distinct families of projected leaves.
For the genuinely literal child markers $t_0^\frown i$, the strict
old--new part of skew clause~(iii) can be discharged uniformly:
each retained old interior node $s$ satisfies
\[
h_Q(s)=h_S(s)\le h_S(t_0),\qquad |s|\le |t_0|,
\]
by maximality of $t_0$ in the corrected forward auxiliary order
and the rank monotonicity of a forward skew support. Conversely
the projected marker point $P(t_0^\frown i)$ extends the ambient
word $t_0^\frown i$, hence
\[
|s|<|P(t_0^\frown i)|,\qquad
h_Q(s)<h_Q(P(t_0^\frown i))=h_S(t_0)+1.
\]
The Lean module \lean{ForwardQOldCutChildOrder} isolates four
source-facing facts implementing these calculations, with no
assumption on local marked-point tail lengths.
This does \emph{not} resolve the exceptional-source-leaf
old--new comparisons, nor either of the new--new skew
conditions or the mixed-product word/colouring pullback.
The proof is pending CI and the expanded transitive axiom audit.


\paragraph{No new projected Q leaf has lower rank than an old interior.} \todo
The bound on old interior source heights, together with exact
rank preservation under Q and the two-level new-leaf rank band,
implies
\[
 h_Q(s)\le h_Q(P_i(s_i))
 \quad
 (s\in\operatorname{Int}(S),\ i\in D_2).
\]
This is formalized in
\lean{ForwardQOldCutChildOrder.old\_Q\_rank\_le\_every\_marked}.
Thus pairs oriented new-to-old cannot trigger skew clause~(iii).
It remains possible that a new leaf and an old interior vertex
have equal intrinsic rank; the corresponding same-rank lexicographic
and ambient-length comparisons still need verification.
This source-facing theorem remains pending CI.


\paragraph{Strict mixed comparisons below the source's maximal cut rank.} \todo
An old interior vertex $s$ with $h_S(s)<h_S(t_0)$ satisfies
$|s|<|t_0|$ by the original source's skew clause~(iii).
The general forward-order comparison also gives
$|t_0|\le |P_j(s_j)|$ for every actual new projected
leaf. The combined inequalities yield
\[
 h_Q(s)<h_Q(P_j(s_j))
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 |s|<|P_j(s_j)|
 \qquad(h_S(s)<h_S(t_0)).
\]
The Lean module \lean{ForwardQOldCutChildOrder} contains the two
lemmas \lean{old\_below\_cut\_length\_lt\_every\_marked}
and \lean{old\_below\_cut\_Q\_rank\_lt\_every\_marked}.
This handles both the exceptional-terminal and cut-child marker
families whenever the old vertex lies below the cut rank.
It leaves only the top-cut-rank / exceptional-terminal mixed
strict comparison, plus new--new comparisons. Pending Lean CI.


\paragraph{Ordinary lex orientation of equal-rank exceptional Q projections.} \todo
For a source terminal $t$ exceptional relative to the final cut
$t_0$, assume $h_S(t)=h_S(t_0)$. Because $t$ occurs after the
inclusive forward cut and the original source satisfies skew
clause~(ii), the ordinary forward lex comparison gives
$t_0\le_{\rm lex}t$.
Let $r$ be the first outside-cut prefix of $t$.
Since $r$ and $t_0$ are prefix-incomparable and
$r\sqsubseteq t$, ordinary lex order reflects to
$t_0\le_{\rm lex}r$. The actual projected replacement
$P(r)$ extends $r$, so it also satisfies
$t_0\le_{\rm lex}P(r)$.

The general finite-word reflection lemma and the two
source-facing statements are in
\lean{ForwardQExceptionalLex}. In particular, any retained
old interior vertex $s$ at the cut's intrinsic rank obeys
$s\le_{\rm lex}P(r)$, since source skew clause~(ii) likewise
gives $s\le_{\rm lex}t_0$.
Together with the already certified old--new weak ambient
length inequality, this determines the orientation relevant
to skew clause~(ii) for old-top versus equal-rank exceptional
replacement pairs. It does not settle the strict length
condition when the exceptional replacement lies one intrinsic
rank above the cut, nor any new--new comparison.
Three new Lean transitive-axiom checks have been added;
the proof remains pending CI.


\paragraph{Complete mixed old/new skew clause~(ii).} \todo
The Q rank formulas and the preceding lexicographic orientation
combine into a source-facing universal statement: whenever a
retained old interior vertex $s$ and an actual D$_2$ projection
$P_i(s_i)$ have the same intrinsic rank in reconstructed $Q$,
\[
 s\le_{\mathrm{lex}} P_i(s_i).
\]
Indeed the child-marker family always has rank $h_S(t_0)+1$,
strictly above the entire old interior, so only an exceptional
source leaf of rank $h_S(t_0)$ contributes. That exceptional
leaf's old-to-projected lex comparison was established above.

Thus the old-to-new ordered pair has the required weak ambient
length inequality, already proved from corrected forward auxiliary
order. In the reverse lex orientation the two nodes must coincide
by finite-word lexicographic antisymmetry, so the weak inequality
is automatic. This discharges both \emph{mixed} orientations of
condition~(ii), independently of local D$_2$ point depths.
The formalized declarations are
\lean{ForwardQMixedCondII.old\_lex\_marked\_of\_equal\_Q\_rank},
\lean{ForwardQMixedCondII.old\_to\_marked\_condII}, and
\lean{ForwardQMixedCondII.marked\_to\_old\_condII}.
The entire file is pending Lean CI (three added transitive-axiom
audits), and does not settle the new/new comparisons or the
strict unequal-rank mixed condition~(iii).

\paragraph{Rank-by-rank predecessors on finite rooted support paths.} \todo
The generic finite-support lemma
\lean{ForwardSourceRankAncestors.ancestor\_at\_each\_rank}
proves that whenever $t$ belongs to a duplicate-free
rooted support $T$, every integer $0\le j\le h_T(t)$
occurs as the intrinsic rank of a genuine support
ancestor of $t$. Starting from the common root, the
argument repeatedly takes the first immediate support
successor along the root-to-$t$ path; each such move
increases intrinsic height by exactly one. This uses
neither completeness nor any skew ambient-length axiom.

The source-facing consequences
\lean{source\_ancestor\_at\_rank} and
\lean{source\_strict\_ancestor\_at\_cut\_rank}
show that an exceptional terminal at source rank
$h_S(t_0)+1$ has a proper source-support predecessor
of rank $h_S(t_0)$. This is the missing ingredient for
proving its first outside-cut boundary has
\emph{strictly larger ambient word length} than $t_0$.
Three transitive Lean axiom endpoints are added to
CI; pending kernel build and audit confirmation.


\paragraph{Strict ambient length of exceptional $Q$ replacements above cut rank.} \todo
Let $t$ be an exceptional old source terminal with
$h_S(t)=h_S(t_0)+1$. The preceding rooted support rank
lemma produces an actual strict source ancestor $u\sqsubset t$
of rank $h_S(u)=h_S(t_0)$. Since $u$ is interior,
maximality of the final cut and source skew clause~(ii)
imply $u\le_{\rm lex}t_0$. The exceptional condition
$t_0\not\sqsubseteq t$ excludes $t_0\sqsubseteq u$,
and strict rank monotonicity along support chains excludes
$u\sqsubseteq t_0$. Thus their prefix cones are incomparable.

Write $r$ for the first outside-inclusive-cut prefix of $t$.
If $|r|\le|t_0|$, its boundary property forces equality
of those ambient lengths. But the old interior ancestor
$u$ prefixes $r$ and lies in a lexicographically earlier
incomparable cone than $t_0$, so
$r\le_{\rm lex}t_0$; equality of lengths would place
$r$ inside the inclusive cut, contradiction. Therefore
\[
  |t_0|<|r|\le |P(r)|.
\]
This argument depends on neither the selected D$_2$
tail length nor the complete support's ambient rank.
It yields strict old/new inequalities simultaneously in
ambient length and intrinsic $Q$ rank for every retained
old interior vertex versus such an exceptional replacement.

The source-facing Lean module
\lean{ForwardQExceptionalLength} isolates the
strict boundary length, strict projected length, and
combined old/new comparison as three audited theorems.
They remain \textbf{PENDING} until GitHub Actions verifies
compilation and transitive absence of nonstandard axioms.
The remaining family at equal cut rank is covered by the
separate equal-rank lexical argument; all new/new pairs
and the word/colouring portion of Lemma~27 remain open.

\paragraph{Universal strict top-rank projected length and mixed condition~(iii).} \todo
The genuine corrected marker-to-D$_2$ bijection permits a
uniform statement without distinguishing marker types in later
applications. For every actual marked projection $P_i(s_i)$ with
intrinsic new-support height exactly one above the old final cut,
\[
 h_Q(P_i(s_i))=h_S(t_0)+1
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 |P_i(s_i)|>|t_0|.
\]
For a child marker this follows from
$t_0^\frown j\sqsubseteq P_i(s_i)$.
For an exceptional terminal marker, the exact Q rank formula
identifies the source terminal rank with $h_S(t_0)+1$,
and the verified cone-order argument above gives a first
boundary $r$ satisfying $|t_0|<|r|\le|P_i(s_i)|$.

Thus every strictly rank-increasing old-to-new pair also has
strictly increasing ambient word length. If the original old
interior vertex has source rank below the cut, apply the
old source's skew clause~(iii) and the weak cut-before-projection
length bound. Otherwise the old vertex has rank $h_S(t_0)$

\subsection{Appendix Remark 3(ii)}
The unrestricted union assertion is false for disjoint $L_1,L_2$.  It becomes
true when $L_1\cap L_2\ne\varnothing$, which is sufficient for the actual
application $L_j=\{a_j,a_{j+1}\}$.

\subsection{Lemma 31}
The rendered PDF removes two OCR false alarms: Lemma~31 assumes $k\ge2$, and
the proof of Theorem~8 later assumes $k\ge3$.  Two genuine one-step index
errors remain in Lemma~31 itself.

First, at construction stage $i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}$ the new wildcard occupies
the coordinate interval $[q_{i-1},q_i)$ inserted after the first $i-1$
variables.  It is therefore the wildcard of $v_{i-1}$, not $v_i$.  The error
is forced already at the final stage: since
\[
d_m=m+n_0-q_m=m,
\]
the word $w_m$ has precisely the variables
$v_0,\ldots,v_{m-1}$, so a wildcard $v_m$ cannot occur.

Second, with
\[
d_{i-1}=(i-1)+n_0-q_{i-1}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
p_i=q_i-q_{i-1},
\]
the set
\[
J=\{0,\ldots,d_{i-1}-1\}
   \setminus [i-1,i-1+p_i)
\]
has cardinality
\[
\begin{aligned}
|J|
 &=d_{i-1}-p_i \\
 &=(i-1)+n_0-q_i \\
 &=(i-1)+f_1(k,\kappa,m-i,m,r),
\end{aligned}
\]
using equation~(8).  Thus the printed $m-i-1$ must be $m-i$.  With this
correction the exponent in (10) matches equation~(9), so the pigeonhole bound
has the intended value $p_i$.

Both corrections are local to the indexing of the Shelah-style construction;
nothing else in the mechanism needs to change at this point.  A source-faithful
Lean formalization of Lemma~31 remains \todo.

\paragraph{Simultaneous evaluation after occurrence-level Hall matching.} \ok
For the corrected, forward-sorted D$_0$/D$_2$ complete-frontier
decomposition, let $V$ be the genuine old source-variable root set and let
$E$ comprise the \emph{actually occurring}, independently tagged cone
variables. Each $e\in E$ has a finite permitted root set $V_e\subseteq V$
consisting of old source roots strictly preceding its own frontier. The
matching premise is the entire Hall condition
\[
  |F|\leq\left|\bigcup_{e\in F}V_e\right|
  \qquad(F\subseteq E),
\]
not merely separate capacity inequalities at each frontier. In particular,
\[
  |E|\leq |V|
\]
is necessary; if $|E|>|V|$ the Hall premise is impossible. The Lean
statements are
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQHallEvaluations.occurringHall_total_capacity}
and
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQHallEvaluations.not_occurringHall_of_excess}.

Under Hall, a \emph{single} legal three-constructor decoded $Q$-word
$d$ on the actual corrected starred $Q$-support works simultaneously
for \emph{all} independently prescribed local-variable assignments:
\[
 \exists d\;\forall \sigma:E\to\Lambda\;
 \exists\tau:V\to\Lambda\;\forall(i,t,z),\quad
  \bigl(t\text{ in frontier cone }i,\;
    g_i(t)=z\bigr)
 \ \Longrightarrow\
   \operatorname{ev}_{\tau}(d(t))=\sigma(i,z).
\]
The Lean implementation extends each assignment along a root map
injective on \emph{occurring} names, and proves the actual pointwise
decoded-symbol identity rather than only an abstract equality of
sets of evaluations:
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQHallEvaluations.extend_occurring_assignment}
and
\lean{DualTree.ForwardQHallEvaluations.hall_Q_realizes_all_local_variable_assignments}.
The same $d$ is chosen before $\sigma$.

\paragraph{Source-faithful $Q$-symbol map on entire $f$-fibres.} \ok
Once the strengthened ambient frame is supplied, the symbol map
$g_w$ is now defined at \emph{every} ambient position by inspecting
the original ambient variable-word symbol $f(s)$ first.
If $f(s)=a\in\Lambda$, the result is $a$ even if $s$
happens to belong geometrically to a later frontier cone.
If $f(s)=v_t$ with $t$ at or before the chosen cut,
the result is $g'(s)=g'(t)$, where the equality uses the
proved uniform-substitution refinement. If the root $t$
lies after the cut, we determine its unique minimal
outside-cut frontier, compute its intrinsic safe inverse
support address $z$ and decode $w_i(z)$ as an original
letter or through the certified $R_i$ decoder. Otherwise
we use the original fallback letter.

In particular the new source-facing fibre identity is
\[
  f(s)=v_{P_i(z)}
  \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
  g_w(s)=
  \begin{cases}
    a,& w_i(z)=a\in\Lambda,\\
    R_i(j),& w_i(z)=j\in\{0,\ldots,m'\}.
  \end{cases}
\]
Crucially, this is verified for \emph{every} $s$ in the
entire original $f$-variable fibre, not just the projected
root $s=P_i(z)$. Thus the earlier inaccurate replacement
of $f$-fibres by geometric cones of ambient \emph{positions}

\paragraph{Finished root alignment of the corrected fibrewise $Q$

Under the corrected forward-order \texttt{FibreFrame}

       \prec f_i\preceq t\preceq s.
\]
Here $f_i$ is the unique corrected outside-cut frontier
containing the \emph{original $f$-variable root} $t$.
The final prefix is supplied by the original ambient
variable-word fibre axiom. Thus geometric membership of
$s$ in the frontier cone is \emph{not} required.

support and, together with the independently verified

\section{Computational evidence retained}
Before Lean formalization, exhaustive finite programs established or tested:
\begin{itemize}
\item failures of the printed auxiliary order and Remark 2, with zero failures
of the corresponding local tests after the forward-lex change;
\item a separate forward-lex counterexample to Remark 1's extension claim;
\item collapse of refinement for $|\Lambda|=1$ and no nonsyntactic inclusion
in the tested two-letter instances;
\item the height-one Corollary 22 counterexample;
\item the small counterexample to the displayed PTGR/TGR inequality;
\item 1244 finite overlap tests supporting the repaired Remark 3(ii);
\item onto behavior of the Theorem 25 signature map and simplicity preservation
in the tested tree-only cases.
\item The executable regression \texttt{scripts/check-skew-meet.py}
exhaustively enumerates all skew subsets (under both the printed
reverse-lex order and the candidate forward-lex order) in the binary
height-$3$ and height-$4$ trees and the ternary height-$2$ tree.
It found, respectively, $25$, $201$, and $7$ skew trees in each order
convention, and all were closed under ambient meets; of these,
$17$, $114$, and $5$ were complete.  These tests corroborate the
proposed meet-closure lemma for complete skew supports but are
\emph{not} a formal proof for arbitrary height or branching number.
\end{itemize}

\section{Formalization order}
\begin{enumerate}
\item finite homogeneous trees, prefix and the two candidate auxiliary orders;
\item skew, complete skew and semi-complete trees, followed by Remarks 1--2;
\item variable words, evaluation, syntactic substitution and span inclusion;
\item the $|\Lambda|\ge2$ substitution/refinement lemma and the unary
counterexample;
\item starred insensitivity and the Appendix argument;
\item mixed products and Theorem 13;
\item signatures and a repaired Lemma 27;
\item Lemma 28 and Theorem 25;
\item Theorem 3 and finally the product theorem.
\end{enumerate}

Qualitative statements take priority over the large recursive bounds until the
underlying constructions are type-correct.

\paragraph{Sorted bullet coordinates and exact ambient $T$-rank transport.} \ok
In the repaired construction the $D_2$ coordinates are a subset
of the genuinely \emph{forward-sorted} minimal outside-cut
frontiers. It is important not to silently assume that an
arbitrary mixed-product input satisfies Definition~20's starred
vector-completeness condition.

The source-facing predicate
\lean{ForwardQIndexedTRanks.BulletLengthsOrdered}
isolates the necessary pairwise statement
\[
 i,j\in D_2,\quad i<j\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 |s_i|\le |s_j|.
\]
The new module \lean{ForwardQIndexedTRanks} proves that this
implies both
\[
 h_T(P_i(s_i))\le h_T(P_j(s_j))
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 |P_i(s_i)|\le |P_j(s_j)|.
\]
The quantitative identity behind the argument is
\[
 h_T(P_i(s_i))=h_T(f_i)+|s_i|,
\]
where $f_i$ is the actual sorted minimal $T$-frontier of
coordinate $i$. If $|s_i|<|s_j|$ then the first inequality is
strict. Moreover, equality of projected $T$-ranks under the
sorted-coordinate and length hypotheses forces
\[
 h_T(f_i)=h_T(f_j),\qquad |s_i|=|s_j|.
\]

These are \emph{conditional} proofs and do not yet establish
that every actual starred point satisfies
\lean{BulletLengthsOrdered}. The separate existing
\lean{VectorOneMarkedLengths} lemmas verify the length
consequence of a vector $1$-complete family indexed by a

Monotonicity of $h_T$ alone was not sufficient.

\paragraph{An explicit starred $D_2$ vector-completeness certificate.} \ok
The actual repaired $Q$ tree uses selected $D_2$ coordinates,

It must not be silently applied to all mixed-product coordinates

The Lean structure

packages an order-reflecting surjective enumeration of the

\[
 \begin{gathered}
 i,j\in D_2,\quad i<j\implies |s_i|\le |s_j|,\\
 h_T(P_i(s_i))\le h_T(P_j(s_j)),\\
 |P_i(s_i)|\le|P_j(s_j)|.

The three certificate-based implications passed Lean and the

\paragraph{Conditional four-way closure of the new/new $Q$ skew inequalities.} \ok
The frontier rank-band interpretation gives the following
short conditional proof, now verified after the two
source-facing marker-family proofs and the explicit
starred $D_2$ certificate interface were formalized.
Write $M=h_T(t_0)$ and $m=h_S(t_0)$. The proposed
matching for every actual marked projection is
\[
 h_Q(P_i)-m=h_T(f_i)-M\in\{0,1\}.
\]
For \emph{equal} $Q$ ranks this forces equal frontier
$T$ ranks. If $P_i\le_{\rm lex}P_j$, the independent
frontier lex/index theorem gives $i\le j$.
The order-correct vector-$1$ certificate then gives
$|s_i|\le|s_j|$, hence the checked sorted-projection
length lemma gives $|P_i|\le|P_j|$.

For \emph{strictly} increasing $Q$ ranks, the
matching gives $h_T(f_i)=M$ and $h_T(f_j)=M+1$.
Sorted frontier rank monotonicity excludes $j\le i$,
so $i<j$, and the starred-domain certificate gives
$|s_i|\le|s_j|$. The additive canonical projection
rank formula yields
\[
 h_T(P_i)=M+|s_i|
  <M+1+|s_j|=h_T(P_j),
\]
so enclosing $T$ skew clause~(iii) supplies
$|P_i|<|P_j|$. The two corresponding old/new
clauses have already been merged in Lean, and the
conditional closure theorem
\lean{ForwardQOrderClosure.Q\_semiComplete\_of\_pairwise\_order}
combines all four pair types.

The conditional proof plan above was implemented and verified
as \lean{ForwardQCertifiedSemiComplete} in merged PR~\#213,
including both new/new skew conditions and the actual
semi-complete reconstructed support. The conclusion assumes
the selected $D_2$ length order, or the explicit vector-$1$
certificate, and does not yet establish all conditions in
the paper's full $W_*$ domain. The $g_w$ word, its colouring
pullback, Lemma~28, and the final theorem remain open.

\paragraph{Exact ambient $T$-frontier rank of literal cut-child markers.} \ok
The corrected literal $R$ contains each ambient child
$r=t_0^\frown i$ of the original last source interior
vertex $t_0$. Its selected minimal outside-cut frontier
$f(r)$ in the enclosing complete support $T$ extends $r$.
Consequently $t_0\sqsubset f(r)$ strictly, and strict
intrinsic height monotonicity gives
$h_T(f(r))>h_T(t_0)$. The already validated forward
frontier bound gives
$h_T(f(r))\le h_T(t_0)+1$. Thus
\[
 h_T(f(t_0^\frown i))=h_T(t_0)+1.
\]
The canonical projection rank increment immediately yields
\[
 h_T(P(t_0^\frown i))=
 h_T(t_0)+1+|s_i|,
\]
with $s_i$ the chosen local $D_2$ tail. Both formulas
are independent of the ambient edge lengths and the
other selected bullet points.

The source-facing Lean module
\lean{ForwardQCutChildFrontierRank} provides two
transitively audited declarations for these identities.
It settles the cut-child case of the frontier rank
matching subsequently completed for exceptional terminals
in merged PR~\#211.

Both cut-child rank statements passed the kernel build and
transitive axiom audit in merged PR~\#211. The other
frontier families were verified in the same integration;
this passage is now a certified component of the unified
two-band theorem in PR~\#212.

\paragraph{Exceptional terminals: support-frontier path and higher-rank alignment.} \ok
The ambient $T$-frontier selected by a literal first-boundary
marker $r(t)$ of an exceptional source terminal $t$ is not merely
an arbitrary descendant of $r(t)$: it actually prefixes $t$.
To verify this, take the ambient meet of the chosen frontier
$f(t)$ and the source terminal $t$, both belonging to $T$.
They share the boundary prefix $r(t)$, so their meet remains
outside the inclusive forward cut. Meet-closure of the
complete skew support makes this meet a $T$-node; minimality
of $f(t)$ forces the meet to equal $f(t)$, hence
$f(t)\sqsubseteq t$.

Now suppose $h_S(t)=h_S(t_0)+1$. The previously proved
rank-by-rank ancestor theorem supplies an interior source
ancestor $u\sqsubset t$ of rank $h_S(t_0)$.
It is incomparable with $t_0$, and the old source's
same-height skew condition implies $u\le_{\rm lex}t_0$.
Since $u$ prefixes $f(t)$, ordinary lex order gives
$f(t)\le_{\rm lex}t_0$.
If $h_T(f(t))=h_T(t_0)$, skew clause~(ii) in $T$
would imply $f(t)\preceq_{\rm fwd}t_0$,
contradicting the selected frontier's being outside the cut.
The verified two-level frontier bound then gives
\[
 h_T(f(t))=h_T(t_0)+1.
\]

The two exceptional high-rank frontier statements passed
Lean compilation and the transitive axiom audit in merged
PR~\#211. The lower-rank exceptional family was verified
in the same integration, completing the literal case split.

\paragraph{Exceptional terminals: lower-rank ambient frontier alignment.} \ok
The remaining exceptional family consists of terminals $t$
with $h_S(t)=h_S(t_0)$. As established for the actual source,
$t$ is after the final cut in corrected forward auxiliary order,
and the old source skew clause~(ii) implies
$t_0\le_{\rm lex}t$.
Since $t$ belongs to the complete support $T$, and
$h_T(t_0)\le h_T(t)$, the generic rank-by-rank support
ancestor theorem produces $a\sqsubseteq t$ with
$h_T(a)=h_T(t_0)$. The exceptional condition
$t_0\not\sqsubseteq t$ and strict rank growth on common
support paths make $a,t_0$ prefix-incomparable.
The lex order reflects from $t$ to $a$:
$t_0\le_{\rm lex}a$.
Then skew clause~(ii) of $T$ places $a$ outside the
inclusive forward cut.

The selected minimal frontier $f(t)$ already lies on
the terminal path (proved in the companion module).
It must prefix $a$: otherwise $a$ would be an
outside-cut $T$-node strictly before $f(t)$.
Consequently
\[
 h_T(f(t))\le h_T(a)=h_T(t_0).
\]
The general frontier bound supplies the converse inequality,
so the exact lower-rank matching is
\[
 h_S(t)=h_S(t_0)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 h_T(f(t))=h_T(t_0).
\]

The ambient ancestor and lower-exceptional-frontier statements
passed Lean compilation and the transitive axiom audit
in merged PR~\#211. Their combination with the higher
cases was verified in the all-markers theorem PR~\#212.

\paragraph{Equal-frontier-rank lex orientation recovers sorted $D_2$ indices.} \ok
Two selected frontier roots $f_i,f_j$ of equal
intrinsic $T$-rank occur in the corrected forward
auxiliary order exactly when their incomparable cones
occur in ordinary forward lex order. Since the
projected points $P_i(s_i),P_j(s_j)$ extend these
incomparable frontier roots, their lex order is
independent of the local tail words. Thus
\[
 h_T(f_i)=h_T(f_j),\qquad
 P_i(s_i)\le_{\rm lex}P_j(s_j)
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad i\le j.
\]
Indeed $j<i$ would imply $f_j\preceq_{\rm fwd}f_i$,
then source support skew clause~(ii) would give
$f_j\le_{\rm lex}f_i$, and incomparability transports
this to $P_j(s_j)\le_{\rm lex}P_i(s_i)$.
Lex antisymmetry and the verified injectivity of
actual marked projections contradict $j<i$.
The converse source-facing frontier-rank monotonicity
\[
 i<j\ \Longrightarrow\ h_T(f_i)\le h_T(f_j)
\]
is also formalized.

Both lex-to-index declarations passed Lean and the transitive
axiom audit in merged PR~\#211. Their use in the conditional
new/new skew proof is verified in PR~\#213, without
assuming arbitrary mixed-product inputs are starred.

\paragraph{Adversarial two-band frontier regression.} \ok
The strengthened reproducible test
\texttt{scripts/check-q-arbitrary-source.py} now checks

h_Q(P_i)-h_S(t_0)=h_T(f_i)-h_T(t_0)\in\{0,1\}.
\]
It also checks nondecreasing Q ranks by sorted bullet

The strengthened finite regression passed CI in merged

\paragraph{Uniform source-facing $Q$/$T$ frontier rank-band matching.} \ok
The previously isolated source marker cases are combined in
\lean{ForwardQFrontierBandMatch.marked\_Q\_T\_frontier\_rank\_band}.
For any actual selected $D_2$ bullet index $i$, write $f_i$
for its true sorted minimal complete-support frontier and
$P_i$ for its reconstructed $Q$ projection. Then the theorem
has exactly two alternatives,
\[
\begin{split}
 &(h_Q(P_i),h_T(f_i))=(h_S(t_0),h_T(t_0)),\\
 \text{or}\quad
 &(h_Q(P_i),h_T(f_i))=(h_S(t_0)+1,h_T(t_0)+1).
\end{split}
\]
The exceptional-source-terminal cases use the certified
individual low/high frontier theorems; direct child markers
use their independently certified exact source and ambient
frontier ranks. Every selected bullet index is covered by the
genuine sorted $R\leftrightarrow D_2$ bijection.

Three algebraic corollaries are also formalized:
\[
\begin{aligned}
h_Q(P_i)<h_Q(P_j)&\iff h_T(f_i)<h_T(f_j),\\
h_Q(P_i)=h_Q(P_j)&\iff h_T(f_i)=h_T(f_j),\\
i<j&\Longrightarrow h_Q(P_i)\le h_Q(P_j).
\end{aligned}
\]
These are independent of the local bullet tail lengths
and make precise the geometric content of the
computer-checked two-band invariant.

The four all-marker rank matching and comparison corollaries
passed Lean compilation and transitive axiom audit in merged
PR~\#212. This certifies the full two-band matching;
the conditional new/new skew order is separately proved
by PR~\#213, while the word and colouring remain unproved.

\paragraph{Complete semi-completeness of the literal corrected $Q$ under starred $D_2$ order.} \ok
Once the uniform two-band matching has been established,
the remaining new/new skew conditions have short proofs.
For equal $Q$ ranks of projected leaves $P_i,P_j$, the
matching forces equal ambient $T$-frontier heights.
If $P_i\le_{\rm lex}P_j$, the independently checked
equal-frontier lex/index lemma forces $i\le j$.
For $i<j$, the selected $D_2$ point-length order gives
$|s_i|\le |s_j|$, and the sorted projection theorem
gives $|P_i|\le|P_j|$. For $i=j$ the inequality is
trivial. This is source-facing skew clause~(ii).

For strictly increasing $Q$ ranks,
the matching forces $h_T(f_i)<h_T(f_j)$.
Monotonicity of frontier $T$ ranks by the
actual sorted indices forces $i<j$, and the starred
point-length order gives $|s_i|\le|s_j|$.
The canonical projection rank identity yields
\[
 h_T(P_i)=h_T(f_i)+|s_i|
   <h_T(f_j)+|s_j|=h_T(P_j).
\]
The enclosing complete support's skew clause~(iii)
then gives $|P_i|<|P_j|$, the desired strict
new/new skew condition.

The Lean module \lean{ForwardQCertifiedSemiComplete}
proves both source-facing new/new clauses and concludes
\[
 \operatorname{SemiComplete}_{\rm fwd}(S_w)
\]
for the \emph{actual reconstructed support}
$S_w=(\operatorname{nodes}(c,x)).\mathrm{toList}$,
provided the selected $D_2$ point lengths satisfy
\lean{ForwardQIndexedTRanks.BulletLengthsOrdered}.
It also states the same conclusion from a concrete
\lean{ForwardQStarredD2Certificate.Certificate}.
The old/old and mixed old/new pair cases are not
reproved or assumed: the earlier verified closure API
supplies them. No assumption of an arbitrary unstarred
mixed-product input being valid is made.

The four statements in \lean{ForwardQCertifiedSemiComplete}
passed Lean compilation, finite regressions, and the
transitive axiom audit in merged PR~\#213, giving the
actual corrected Q support semi-complete skew when the
selected $D_2$ length order (or certificate) is provided.
This is a conditional geometric result, \emph{not} the
full printed Lemma~27: construction of the certificate from
the original $W_*$ domain, safe recursive height bounds,
specific mixed-cone word $g_w$, and smooth colouring
pullback remain open.
