\paragraph{Constructor-wise prefix alignment for the three-way cone-word decoder.} \todo
The intended Lemma~27 word $g_w$ contains three
distinct kinds of local symbols: old alphabet constants,
auxiliary symbols which must decode either to a constant
or to an old visible ancestor variable, and genuine local
cone variables transported to the target global support.
The existing typed
\lean{ConeWordLift.liftWord} implements this
three-way distinction.

The new module \lean{ThreeWayQDecoderRootAlignment}
isolates an exact necessary-to-use decomposition
of the local variable-occurrence prefix check.
For a source-root map $\operatorname{root}:\nu\to b^{<n}$,
the decoded symbol at a homogeneous-tree position
$u$ can equal a target variable $v$ only if it
came from (i) an auxiliary symbol $a$ decoded to
$v$ or (ii) a genuine local variable $z$ whose
transported target is $v$. Thus the two separate
hypotheses
\[
\begin{split}
 w(u)=\operatorname{aux}(a),\
 \operatorname{decodeAux}(a)=v
 &\Longrightarrow \operatorname{root}(v)\sqsubseteq u,\\
 w(u)=\operatorname{var}(z)
 &\Longrightarrow
 \operatorname{root}(\operatorname{variableRoot}(z))
      \sqsubseteq u
\end{split}
\]
imply the required global decoded-variable prefix
condition. Original alphabet constants need no
such hypothesis. Both the single-cone and arbitrary
indexed-family versions are formalized as source-facing
Lean theorems, with two transitive axiom endpoints
pending CI.

This is a precise \emph{interface} for verifying the
specific word $g_w$ from the actual mixed-product
cone maps; it does not assert that either hypothesis
is already established for those maps. Once they are
proved, the independent disjoint-cone patching and
decoded-word assembly modules can produce the valid
global variable word. Smooth colouring and the
numerical recursive bounds remain separate obligations.

\paragraph{Disjoint selected $D_2$ cones and preservation of source variable roots.} \todo
The mixed-cone word $g_w$ must eventually patch locally decoded
symbols inside the inclusive cones of the \emph{actual}
corrected projected points $P_i(s_i)$, indexed by the
genuine selected $D_2$ subtype. Two geometric properties
ensure such patches cannot overwrite old variable roots
or conflict with one another.

Every old source variable root $v\in\operatorname{supp}(g)$
is an interior source node at/before the final forward cut,
while each projected point lies outside that inclusive cut.
Thus $P_i\not\sqsubseteq v$ for every selected $i$.
If two projected cones shared any ambient node $u$, then
both selected minimal complete-support frontiers $f_i,f_j$
would prefix $u$. Frontier antichain uniqueness would
give $f_i=f_j$, and the genuine injective sorted-coordinate
indexing would force $i=j$. Consequently for $i\neq j$
\[
 \nexists u\quad
 P_i\sqsubseteq u\ \land\ P_j\sqsubseteq u.
\]
In particular the projected points themselves are
prefix-incomparable, and all their inclusive successor
cones are pairwise disjoint in the \emph{whole ambient
tree}, not merely as subsets of $T$.

The three source-facing Lean lemmas are
\lean{ForwardQDisjointWordCones.projected\_cone\_avoids\_source\_root},
\lean{projected\_cones\_no\_common\_extension}, and
\lean{projected\_nodes\_incomparable}. They use no
additional starred point-order or colouring hypotheses.
They supply a clean partition interface for the
three-way cone-word decoding and root-aligned
substitution used to construct $g_w$, but they do not
\emph{yet} construct that word or show smoothness of
the induced colouring. Three transitive axiom endpoints
have been added; pending Lean CI.

\paragraph{Root-preserving cone-local word stitching on the actual projected $D_2$ cones.} \todo
The first two geometric components of the mixed-cone word
construction are checked separately: distinct actual projected
cones are disjoint in the whole homogeneous ambient tree, and no
old variable root is contained in a projected cone.
The module \lean{ForwardQLocalWordPatching} now formalizes
the corresponding \emph{single-valued, noncomputable}
cone selector on the actual bounded homogeneous tree:
a position $u$ above a marked point $P_i(s_i)$ is assigned
to precisely that selected bullet index $i$, whereas
positions outside all selected cones are assigned \texttt{none}.

Given a family of locally translated total symbol maps
\[
 L_i:b^{<n}\longrightarrow
     \Lambda\sqcup\operatorname{Vars}(g),
 \qquad i\in D_2,
\]
the globally patched symbol is
\[
 G(u)=
 \begin{cases}
   L_i(u)&P_i(s_i)\sqsubseteq u,\\
   g(u)&\text{outside every marked cone}.
 \end{cases}
\]
The disjointness theorem makes this formula unambiguous,
and the absence of old source roots from the marked
cones ensures $G(v)=v$ for each old variable root $v$.
If each \emph{locally translated} variable occurrence
$L_i(u)=v$ is aligned with its original root,
$v\sqsubseteq u$, then every variable occurrence of
$G$ also lies above its root. Hence $G$ becomes a
well-typed tree-variable word with support literally equal
to the old source variable-root list.

The new module establishes the cone selector's
exact correctness, the patched word inside/outside
equalities, root preservation, the resulting
\lean{VariableWord} and its exact support and word
component. Eight source-facing transitive axiom
checks are added to the Lean audit; pending CI.
The local root-alignment premise must still be shown
for the \emph{particular} three-way decoded cone
word of Lemma~27, and its smooth colouring pullback
must still be established. This is not a proof
of the complete lemma.


\paragraph{Three-way decoded local cone words assemble into a genuine starred $Q$ word.} \todo
The source-facing word construction can be stated with
a precise \emph{local decoding certificate}, without
yet assuming the paper's chosen cone words satisfy it.
At each actual selected $D_2$ index $i$ we take a total
raw local symbol map
\[
 w_i:b^{<n}\longrightarrow
       (\Lambda\sqcup\mathrm{Aux})\sqcup\kappa,
\]
an auxiliary decoder $\mathrm{Aux}\to
\Lambda\sqcup\operatorname{Vars}(g)$ and a transport
$\kappa\to\operatorname{Vars}(g)$ of genuine cone
variables. Their root-alignment conditions require that
(i) any auxiliary symbol decoded to an old variable
occurs above that variable's source root, and
(ii) any genuine transported cone variable also
occurs above its designated old root.

The module
\lean{ForwardQThreeWayConeWordAssembly}
uses the verified
\lean{ThreeWayQDecoderRootAlignment}
three-way constructor analysis to obtain an aligned
symbol map at each selected cone. It then applies
\lean{ForwardQLocalWordPatching} to construct
an unambiguous global patched variable word:
inside the actual inclusive cone of $P_i(s_i)$
the symbol is \emph{literally} the corresponding
three-way decoded $w_i$, whereas outside all
marked cones the symbol is the unchanged old
source word. Original source variable roots are
automatically preserved because the selected
cones avoid them.

Finally, for an actual member of the corrected
typed \lean{ForwardQActualStarredSpace.StarredInput}
and an explicitly $n$-bounded enclosing complete
support $T$, the decoded-word interface
\lean{ForwardQWordSymbolAssembly.DecodedWord}
packages this variable word into a genuine
\lean{StarredSignature.StarredWord} whose tree
is \emph{exactly} the actual corrected $Q$ support.
The inside/outside symbol identities and full
tree support equality are formalized as separate
statements. Seven transitive no-\texttt{sorryAx}
audit endpoints are added; pending Lean CI.

This closes an important \emph{conditional algebraic
assembly step}. It does not identify the raw maps,
auxiliary decoder or cone-variable root transport with
the \emph{particular} ones prescribed by Lemma~27;
their root-alignment certificate is therefore still
a genuine missing argument. Moreover, the original
printed $W_*$ to corrected starred-domain transport,
smooth colouring pullback, recursive local-height
bound and final Ramsey induction remain open.

\paragraph{Starred $D_2$ membership is not invariant under naive reverse-to-forward frontier reindexing.} \ok
The forward-order geometric repair of Lemma~27 changes
the tie-breaking order of \emph{equal-length} complete-support
frontiers: the printed convention is length-first
reverse lexicographic, whereas the repaired convention
is length-first forward lexicographic. Merely retaining
the same marker-to-local-point assignment and
re-enumerating the $D_2$ coordinates is not guaranteed
to preserve Definition~20's vector $1$-completeness.

The typed binary height-$3$ witness already developed
in \lean{QLengthStarredDomain} provides an exact
source-facing test. Its two selected frontier markers
are the right and left children of the empty cut.
The printed order is $(\mathrm{right},\mathrm{left})$,
with actual local point lengths $(0,1)$ and hence
vector-$1$-complete. The corrected forward
frontier order is $(\mathrm{left},\mathrm{right})$.
Retaining the actual local point assigned to each
marker gives lengths $(1,0)$, which is \emph{not}
vector-$1$-complete:
\[
 \begin{split}
 &\operatorname{VectorComplete}_1
       ((),(0))=\mathrm{true},\\
 &\operatorname{VectorComplete}_1
       ((0),())=\mathrm{false}.
 \end{split}
\]
The module
\lean{ForwardQStarredOrderReindexObstruction}
checks the actual typed selected marked points
under both enumerations, rather than substituting
unrelated abstract singleton supports.
Six source-facing transitive axiom endpoints
passed the Lean build and transitive no-sorry axiom audit in merged PR~\#227.

This is a \emph{counterexample to naive coordinate
reindexing}, not a counterexample to the conditional
forward $Q$-geometry theorem or to the final Ramsey
conclusion. It establishes that deriving the repaired
$W_*$ domain from the printed one must either
transport/reassign the local cone words and marked
points \emph{along with} the frontier permutation,
or explicitly change/restrict the starred domain.
That compatibility, including smooth colouring and
the recursive mixed-product Ramsey argument, remains
a separate genuine proof obligation.


\paragraph{Actual corrected starred inputs are stable under smooth inside-cone word patches.} \ok
The source-facing module
\lean{ForwardQStarredSmoothConePatches}
defines, for an actual mixed-product input $x$ in the
\emph{forward-corrected} $D_0/D_2$ presentation, a word
replacement which changes only the $\Gamma_2=D_2$
coordinate letters \emph{inside} the inclusive cone
of the corresponding marked local point, keeping
the marked-point array and all other coordinate
words fixed.

Two structural identities are important:
\[
 \operatorname{IsStarred}(c,\operatorname{patch}(x))
 \iff \operatorname{IsStarred}(c,x),\qquad
 Q(c,\operatorname{patch}(x))=Q(c,x).
\]
These hold because the corrected starred vector-$1$
condition and the reconstructed support both depend
only on the selected marked points, not on the
letters of the coordinate words.

More precisely, the old and patched mixed-product
elements are related by the literal
Definition~21 $\Gamma_2$ smoothness relation:
their nonbullet-coordinate words and marked points
coincide, and each bullet-coordinate word is unchanged
outside its own marked-point cone. Thus every colouring
genuinely constant on smoothness classes of the
corrected starred input subtype is invariant under
arbitrary such inside-cone patches. The colouring
need not be assumed smooth on all unstarred inputs.

All six source-facing patch-invariance declarations passed Lean
compilation and transitive axiom audit in merged PR~\#229. This conditional smooth-colour
invariance is a \emph{reduction}, not a proof that
the particular induced colouring $c\circ Q$ in
the paper satisfies smoothness. That requires
identifying the actual prescribed local word
translation with an allowed patched-coordinate
comparison, and proving the missing compatibility
of its colours and the recursive Ramsey subspace.

\paragraph{Pointwise and evaluated semantics of the actual decoded $Q$ word.} \ok
The module \lean{ForwardQThreeWayEvaluation} exposes the
pointwise content of the previously verified three-way
local-cone assembly. At a position $t$ belonging to the actual
selected projected cone indexed by $i$, the three raw
constructors have separate, exhaustive translations:
\[
\begin{split}
 \operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inl}a)
     &\longmapsto \operatorname{inl}a,\\
 \operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inr}u)
     &\longmapsto \operatorname{decodeAux}_i(u),\\
 \operatorname{inr}z
     &\longmapsto \operatorname{inr}(\operatorname{coneRoot}_i(z)).
\end{split}
\]
Thus genuine cone variables can never be accidentally
confused with auxiliary letters. Two occurrences of the
same cone variable in a given marked cone translate to
the same original source variable even at different
homogeneous-tree positions. For each valuation $\sigma$
of source variable names the resulting \emph{evaluated}
starred $Q$ word satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{eval}_{\sigma}(g_Q)(t)=
 \begin{cases}
 a,&\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inl}a),\\
 \operatorname{eval}_{\sigma}(\operatorname{decodeAux}_i u),
    &\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inr}u),\\
 \sigma(\operatorname{coneRoot}_i z),
    &\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}z.
 \end{cases}
\]
Outside \emph{all} selected marked cones its evaluation agrees
exactly with the original source word under the same valuation.
These are source-facing statements about the actual word on Q,
rather than an abstract decoder map.
All six source-facing evaluation declarations passed the Lean build
and transitive axiom audit in merged PR~\#228.

This is not yet the printed Lemma~27 word $g_w$: the raw local
cone words and the source's auxiliary/root decoding prescriptions
must still be identified with \lean{ConeData} and shown to satisfy
its alignment assumptions. Nor does pointwise evaluation alone
prove smoothness, invariance of the colouring, simplicity of the
pulled-back colouring or the required recursive parameter bounds.

\paragraph{Exact classwise criterion for smooth extension of corrected $W_*$ colourings.} \ok
The source-facing theorem
\lean{ForwardQStarredSmoothExtension.total\_smooth\_extension\_iff\_starred\_smooth}
specializes the previously verified partial-colouring
extension lemma to the \emph{actual corrected} forward
$D_0/D_2$ starred-input subtype. For a colouring
$d:W_*\to\Gamma$, the following conditions are
equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item There exists a total colouring
$\widehat d:W^D\to\Gamma$ agreeing with $d$ on
every corrected starred point and constant on all
$\Gamma_2=D_2$ smoothness classes.
\item Whenever two actual starred inputs $x,y\in W_*$
are related by Definition~21 smoothness,
$d(x)=d(y)$.
\end{enumerate}
The implication $(i)\Rightarrow(ii)$ follows by
restriction. Conversely, smoothness is an equivalence
relation, and the classwise-compatible partial
colouring extends by the existing general theorem,
with an arbitrary fallback colour on classes
disjoint from $W_*$. Thus the true obstacle is
\emph{classwise compatibility of the induced
colouring on the starred domain}, not the extension
mechanism itself.

All three source-facing extension criterion statements passed
Lean compilation and transitive no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit
in merged PR~\#230. This
theorem should not be interpreted as proof that
the \emph{particular} Lemma~27 colouring
$c\circ Q$ satisfies condition~(ii).
Its compatibility must be derived from the
actual constructed local cone words and the
source colouring, while respecting the
forward-vs-reverse reindexing obstruction and
the quantitative induction hypotheses.


\paragraph{Converse patch theorem: exact normal form for Definition~21 smoothness.} \ok
The earlier cone-patch theorem gave only one direction:
replacing letters inside the inclusive marked $D_2$ cones
preserves smooth-relatedness.  The converse is important in
checking the colouring pullback in Lemma~27, and it has
no extra combinatorial hypothesis.
For two elements $x,y$ of the actual corrected starred
$W_*$ subtype (with fixed selected $D_0/D_2$ coordinates),
let $\operatorname{patch}(x,y)$ retain all marked points and
nonbullet words of $x$ and use the words of $y$ inside
the inclusive marked cones of $x$. Then
\[
 x\sim_{\Gamma_2}y
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \operatorname{patch}(x,y)=y.
\]
Indeed, Definition~21 fixes all nonbullet words,
all marked points, and each bullet word outside its
marked cone; the remaining symbols are precisely
those overwritten by the patch. Hence an arbitrary
starred colouring $d$ is $\Gamma_2$-smooth \emph{if
and only if} it is invariant under \emph{every}
marked-cone patch:
\[
 \begin{split}
 d \text{ is smooth on } W_*
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 &d(x)=d(\operatorname{patch}(x,r))\\
 &\text{for all starred }x
   \text{ and all replacement word arrays }r.
 \end{split}
\]
The source-facing theorem names are
\lean{ForwardQStarredPatchCriterion.reconstruct\_mixed\_input\_from\_smooth},
\lean{smoothRelated\_iff\_patchedStarred\_eq}, and
\lean{smoothStarred\_iff\_all\_patches}.

There is also a useful universal-colouring diagnostic.
If $Q:W_*\to Y$ is an output map, then \emph{every}
endocolouring $h:Y\to Y$ makes $h\circ Q$ smooth
exactly when $Q$ itself is invariant under all marked-cone
patches.  Necessity follows by taking $h=\mathrm{id}_Y$;
sufficiency follows by composition.  The Lean interface is
\lean{ForwardQStarredPatchCriterion.all\_endocolors\_smooth\_iff\_Q\_patch\_invariant}.
This must \emph{not} be confused with smoothness of a
\emph{fixed} induced colouring $c\circ Q$: the latter
requires only equality of its resulting colours,
not equality of the full output words $Q(x)$ and $Q(y)$.

\paragraph{Proposed proof repair.}
To justify the application of the smooth mixed-product
lemma in Lemma~27 it is now enough, and also necessary,
to verify the patch invariance of the \emph{particular}
induced colouring $c\circ Q$ on the actual corrected
starred domain.  Invariance of the support tree alone
is insufficient, since the patched $Q$ output word
may change inside a cone and $c$ may distinguish it.
The local word decoder, source colouring and marked-point
transport must be used together in this check.  Moreover
the forward/reverse coordinate-order obstruction means
the corrected starred subtype cannot simply be identified
with the printed $W_*$ by reindexing.
These are targeted repairs to the Lemma~27 proof,
not a claim that its complete statement has been proved.
\paragraph{Lean status.} \ok{} The four source-facing
patch-converse and colouring-criterion declarations passed
Lean compilation, finite regressions, and the transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit (merged PR~\#232; 916 declarations).


\paragraph{A canonical representative of each corrected smoothness class.} \ok
The exact patch normal form suggests a further algebraic
construction independent of the paper's unverified
specific $Q$ decoder.  Fix any letter $a\in\Lambda$.
For each corrected starred mixed-product input $x$ define
$N_a(x)$ by replacing all letters in the inclusive marked
cones of the $D_2$ coordinate words with $a$, leaving
the marked points and all outside-cone symbols unchanged.
This is an actual element of the \emph{same} corrected
starred subtype, and its reconstructed $Q$ support
is unchanged.  By the override property of patches,
\[
 \operatorname{patch}(\operatorname{patch}(x,r),s)
 =\operatorname{patch}(x,s).
\]
Consequently,
\[
 N_a(N_a(x))=N_a(x),\qquad
 x\sim_{\Gamma_2}y
   \Longleftrightarrow N_a(x)=N_a(y).
\]
The converse uses smooth-relatedness of $x$ to $N_a(x)$,
its symmetry, and transitivity.  In particular
\emph{every} colouring of the form
$x\mapsto c(Q(N_a(x)))$ is smooth, for arbitrary
$Q$ and $c$, because the input has first been replaced
by a canonical smoothness-class representative.
The new Lean source-facing declarations are
\lean{ForwardQStarredNormalization.patchedInput\_override},
\lean{normalizeStarred\_eq\_of\_smooth},
\lean{smoothRelated\_iff\_normalizeStarred\_eq},
\lean{normalizeStarred\_idempotent}, and
\lean{normalized\_pullback\_is\_smooth}.

\paragraph{Caution / suggested correction.}
Although this proves that a colouring may be made
smooth \emph{as a set-theoretic map}, it is \emph{not}
by itself a repair of Lemma~27.  Replacing the original
input by $N_a(x)$ discards the letters inside the marked
cones.  The requisite image of the local mixed-word span,
the Hales--Jewett variables, and the paper's particular
decoded $g_w$ may depend on exactly these letters.
Before adopting normalization in the proof one must
establish that the induced map after normalization
still has the required image/refinement and colouring
factorization properties.  The forward/reverse
starred-domain reindexing gap also persists.
Thus normalization provides a diagnostic and a
concrete possible intermediate definition,
not a proved completion of the Ramsey argument.
\paragraph{Lean status.} \ok{} All five normalization
theorems compiled and passed the finite regressions and 921-declaration
transitive no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#233.
The \emph{algebraic normal form} is verified; the proposed
application to the particular Lemma~27 pullback and its
Ramsey span remains \todo.


\paragraph{Normalization provably loses starred-word information.} \todo
The canonical smoothness normal form $N_a$ established above
forces the letter $a$ at each marked point of a selected
$D_2$ coordinate, because the inclusive cone contains
its own root.  This restriction persists even though
the normalization leaves the entire corrected $Q$
support \emph{unchanged}.
Let $x$ be a genuine member of the corrected starred
mixed product, fix an actual index $i\in D_2$,
and suppose the alphabet contains two distinct letters
$a$ and $a'\ne a$.  The element
\[
 y=\operatorname{patch}\bigl(x,(\text{constant }a')_j\bigr)
\]
is also starred and has the same marked points and
$Q$ support as $x$. But
\[
 y_i(s_i)=a',
 \qquad
 [N_a(z)]_i(s_i)=a
 \quad\text{for all corrected starred }z
 \text{ with }N_a(z)=y,
\]
where the latter equality uses the fact that
normalization fixes the marked-point array.
Thus $y$ cannot be in the image of $N_a$:
\[
 D_2\ne\varnothing,\quad W_*\ne\varnothing,\quad
 |\Lambda|\ge 2
 \ \Longrightarrow\quad
 N_a:W_*\to W_* \text{ is not surjective}.
\]
This assertion is \emph{conditional on the existence}
of a genuine corrected-starred input and a selected
bullet coordinate; it makes no claim that all arbitrary
marked-point arrays belong to $W_*$.

The intended Lean certificate consists of
\lean{ForwardQNormalizationObstruction.markedLetter\_patched\_constant},
\lean{markedLetter\_normalized},
\lean{normalized\_Q\_nodes\_eq},
\lean{patched\_other\_letter\_not\_in\_normalization\_range},
and the two \lean{normalizeStarred\_not\_surjective}
statements. Six source-facing transitive axiom checks
are pending on the development branch.

\paragraph{Correction consequence.}
A proof of Lemma~27 cannot simply replace its entire
starred mixed-product domain by the image of $N_a$
\emph{and simultaneously claim to retain the original
full image of the marked-cone word coordinates}.
The normalization deliberately erases at least one
independent letter whenever a nontrivial marked cone
is present. This does \emph{not} refute a possible
Ramsey repair, since the selected subspace might need
only carefully controlled restricted words. Such a
repair must explicitly verify the required span and
refinement properties \emph{after} normalization,
rather than infer them from its smoothness.


\paragraph{Definition~21 requires a split of $D_2$, not necessarily $\Gamma_2=D_2$.} \todo
A further source-facing review of the rendered pp.~24 and~29
revealed that the preceding Gamma$_2$-patch criterion and
constant-letter normalization use only the
\emph{special case} $\Gamma_1=\varnothing$,
$\Gamma_2=D_2$.  Definition~21 instead says that
a colouring is smooth if there \emph{exists} a
partition
\[
 D_2=\Gamma_1\mathbin{\dot\cup}\Gamma_2
\]
such that (a) the \emph{entire word} is fixed at
coordinates of $D_0\cup D_1\cup\Gamma_1$,
(b) the $D_1$ terminal points are fixed, and
(c) at coordinates of $\Gamma_2$ the marked point
and the word \emph{outside} its inclusive cone
are fixed. In contrast, a $\Gamma_1$ marked point
may move even when its whole word is unchanged.
The two types of coordinates therefore encode
different information, and the two relations are
not interchangeable.

In particular, colours depending only on the entire
array of coordinate \emph{words}, and not on marked
point locations, are smooth with $\Gamma_1=D_2$.
There is an explicit binary one-bullet witness:
colour a mixed input by the Boolean value at the
fixed ambient root of its word. It is smooth for
$\Gamma_1=D_2$, but two words that differ inside
the common marked-root cone prove it is not smooth
for $\Gamma_1=\varnothing$. Hence the all-Gamma$_2$
classwise criterion from the previous audit is
\emph{sufficient but not necessary} for the
published existential notion of smoothness.

\paragraph{Lean API correction.}
The new
\lean{ForwardQStarredSmoothPartitions.SmoothStarredFor}
exposes an arbitrary membership predicate
$\gamma_1:D_2\to\{\mathrm{false},\mathrm{true}\}$.
The source-facing theorem
\lean{smooth\_starred\_extension\_iff}
establishes, for each fixed predicate, that a colouring
of the \emph{actual corrected starred subtype}
extends to a total $\gamma_1$-smooth colouring
if and only if it is already constant on those
smooth-related pairs \emph{within} the starred
subtype. The theorem
\lean{exists\_partition\_total\_extension\_iff}
then quantifies existentially over the predicate,
exactly as in Definition~21. The
\lean{StrictnessWitness.smooth\_for\_some\_partition\_but\_not\_gamma2\_all}
proof distinguishes this statement from the
previous all-Gamma$_2$ version.

\paragraph{Required connection to Definition~24.}
Definition~24 gives a \emph{possibly different}
partition $A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$ associated with
simplicity of the original starred colouring.
The proof of Lemma~27 must explicitly map this
witness onto a choice of $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$
on the *selected frontier indices*, verifying
both implications:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item if a selected index belongs to $\Gamma_1$,
the full word equality should suffice to control
all colour-sensitive symbols inside that cone,
\emph{even as the marked point moves};
\item if it belongs to $\Gamma_2$, fixed marked points
and equality outside their local cones must
transport to the $B_2$-insensitivity condition

\end{enumerate}
The corrected forward-sorted coordinate order,
the literal $B_1/B_2$ marker correspondence,
and the typed three-way decoder must all be

proved. The earlier Gamma$_2=D_2$ normalization

for their exact restricted statements, but
\emph{cannot} be demanded as prerequisites

\paragraph{Exact transport of a literal marker split to the sorted $D_2$ coordinates.} \ok
The corrected source-facing reconstruction uses a finite set $R$
of literal signature markers (exceptional first-exit boundaries
and immediate children of the final source cut). The preceding
geometric Lean development has already proved a bijection
\[
 \iota:R\longrightarrow D_2
\]
onto \emph{exactly} the bullet indices of the genuine
forward-sorted complete-support frontier list.
The inverse is the verified noncomputable
\lean{ForwardQMeetClosed.markerOfBullet}, with the
two triangle identities.  Given any subset
$B_2\subseteq R$, put
\[
 \Gamma_2=\{\iota(s):s\in B_2\},\qquad
 \Gamma_1=D_2\setminus\Gamma_2.
\]
The concrete constructor
\lean{ForwardQMarkerSmoothPartition.gamma1OfMarkerB2}
encodes this partition on actual bullet indices
($\mathrm{true}$ for $\Gamma_1$). Theorems
\lean{markerOfBullet\_bulletForMarker},
\lean{gamma1\_at\_marker},
\lean{gamma1\_iff\_not\_B2}, and
\lean{gamma2\_iff\_B2} verify the bijection,
so no arbitrary reordering of the corrected
frontier coordinates is silently introduced.

For $x,y$ smooth-related under this particular split,
the following are then true \emph{at the actual indexed
mixed-product coordinates}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item for $s\notin B_2$, the complete local coordinate
word at $\iota(s)$ agrees between $x$ and $y$;
the local marked points are not assumed fixed;
\item for $s\in B_2$, the local marked point at
$\iota(s)$ agrees, and both local words agree
outside the inclusive cone of that point;
\item at the same $B_2$ marker the actual projected
$Q$ leaf also agrees, by the corrected canonical
cone projection formula.
\end{enumerate}
The source-facing lemmas are
\lean{gamma1\_marker\_words\_equal},
\lean{gamma2\_marker\_points\_equal},
\lean{gamma2\_marker\_words\_outside\_equal}, and
\lean{gamma2\_Q\_projected\_marker\_equal}.
These eight statements passed Lean compilation, the finite regressions,
and the 943-declaration transitive axiom audit in merged PR~\#237.

\paragraph{Remaining correction, not yet discharged.}
The partition $B_1\dot\cup B_2$ from the
\emph{printed} simplicity Definition~24 is a partition
of its canonical set $A$ of signature terminals.
The present transport works for a \emph{given}
partition of the corrected literal marker set $R$.
It does not construct the canonical rooted-tree/address
identification between the abstract canonical terminal set $A$
from Definition~24 and the literal ambient marker set $R$;
literal equality is neither required nor generally meaningful.
Nor does it prove that a colouring simple on $A$
transports its insensitivity condition to this $R$.
Both assertions are needed before concluding smoothness
of the actual induced $c\circ Q$.

There is a second, independent word-construction
distinction that must not be hidden. The source's
displayed $g_w$ decodes words throughout
\emph{each selected complete-support frontier cone}
$P_i(b^{<\cdots})$, including coordinates $i\in D_0$.
In contrast, the verified generic
\lean{ForwardQThreeWayConeWordAssembly}
patches the original word only in
\emph{projected marked-point cones} at $D_2$,
using explicitly supplied root-aligned local data.
This is a genuine difference in the \emph{domain of
symbol replacement}. The conditional word assembly
cannot be identified with the printed Lemma~27 word
without first extending the patching interface to
the correct frontier cones and checking the
prescribed decoder at all their positions.
The Q-support and smoothness-setoid lemmas remain
valid under their precise statements. The
construction, colouring-pullback, quantitative
recursion, Lemma~28 and Theorem~25 remain \todo.


\paragraph{Full-frontier word assembly, including all $D_0$ and $D_2$ cones.} \ok
The previous paragraph identifies a substantive mismatch
between the printed and conditional decoded-word
constructions. We now isolate the precise corrected
word-patching domain as a separate source-facing Lean
interface. Let
\[
 F=(f_i:i\in[d])
\]
be the \emph{complete} list of minimal outside-cut
frontiers of the forward-complete support $T$,
sorted by the globally corrected length-first
forward-lex order. For every ambient homogeneous-tree
position $t\in b^{<n}$, choose the unique $i$ such
that $f_i\sqsubseteq t$ (if such an $i$ exists).
Both its existence and uniqueness are genuinely
based on $F$, not the chosen marked points $s_j$
at the indices $j\in D_2$. The frontier cones
are pairwise disjoint even outside $T$, by the
frontier-antichain theorem; furthermore, none
of the original source variable roots lies in
an outside-cut frontier cone.

Supply for \emph{each} $i\in[d]$ a total
root-aligned decoded local symbol map
\[
 L_i:b^{<n}\longrightarrow
 \Lambda\sqcup \operatorname{Vars}(g),
 \qquad
 L_i(t)=v\Longrightarrow v\sqsubseteq t.
\]
The full-frontier output word is
\[
 G_F(t)=
 \begin{cases}
   L_i(t),&f_i\sqsubseteq t,\\
   g(t),&\text{outside all frontier cones}.
 \end{cases}
\]
All symbol replacements now take place in
\emph{frontier} cones at both ordinary and
bullet coordinates, as in the source's displayed
$g_w$ construction, rather than solely in the
\emph{projected marked-point} cones. Its
variable roots remain literally the old
$\operatorname{Int}(S)$, and the root-alignment
hypotheses ensure a legal variable word. For
any independently selected corrected-starred
mixed input $x$, packaging this word with the
already-verified tree support $Q(x)$ gives a
starred object with support exactly $Q(x)$.
The whole word $G_F$ depends on the decoded
local maps and the frontier geometry, but
\emph{not} on the local marked points
when the maps are held fixed. Thus changing
a $\Gamma_1$ marked point may change the
$Q$ tree but need not change the word.

The proposed Lean module
\lean{ForwardQFullFrontierWord} proves the
frontier selector's root-avoidance and
single-valuedness, the patched symbol
inside/outside equalities, the exact old
root support, and the conditional legal
starred-word construction. Its thirteen source-facing declarations passed the Lean build,
finite regressions and 956-declaration transitive axiom audit
in merged PR~\#238.

\paragraph{What the new word interface still requires.}
This conditional all-frontier assembly is
\emph{closer to the printed} Lemma~27
word-construction domain but still does
not identify the particular $L_i$ with
its local variable words, enlarged
auxiliary alphabet, $R_i$ decoders,
and canonical cone-root transport.
Each must be instantiated from the
actual corrected mixed-product word
coordinate $w_i$, with explicit proof
of root-prefix alignment. Furthermore,
the simple-colouring $B_1/B_2$ criterion
must be transported from the source
canonical signature $A$ to the literal
corrected marker set $R$ and hence to
a smoothness split on $D_2$.
Without those steps neither smoothness
of $c\circ Q$, the required
mixed-word span/refinement, nor the
numerical Ramsey induction follows.


\paragraph{Three-constructor semantics on the \emph{full} corrected frontier family.} \ok
The earlier checked \lean{ForwardQThreeWayEvaluation}
translated original letters, auxiliary letters and
genuine cone variables, but only over the
\emph{marked-point cones} at selected $D_2$ indices.
This is not the full domain of the displayed
$g_w$ in Lemma~27. Following the corrected
all-frontier patching interface above, the proposed
\lean{ForwardQFullFrontierThreeWay.ConeData}
now supplies for \emph{every} sorted frontier
coordinate $i\in D_0\cup D_2$:
\[
 \operatorname{raw}_i:
 b^{<n}\longrightarrow
    (\Lambda\sqcup\mathrm{Aux})\sqcup\kappa,
 \quad
 R_i:\mathrm{Aux}\longrightarrow
   \Lambda\sqcup\operatorname{Vars}(g),
 \quad
 v_i:\kappa\longrightarrow\operatorname{Vars}(g).
\]
The two local hypotheses distinguish occurrences
of auxiliary letters that decode to old variables
from genuine cone variables: both emitted variables
must be ambient prefixes of their occurrences.
These conditions are explicitly recorded; they are
\emph{not} derived from the paper's still-unidentified
specific $\operatorname{raw}_i$, $R_i$ and $v_i$.

The global word is patched in the unique
\emph{complete-support frontier cone} containing
the ambient position. At such a position $t$
the local decoded symbol and its value under any
assignment $\sigma:\operatorname{Vars}(g)\to\Lambda$ are
\[
 G(t)=
 \begin{cases}
  a,&\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inl}a),\\
  R_i(u),&\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inr}u),\\
  \operatorname{inr}(v_i(z)),
    &\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}z,
 \end{cases}
\]
\[
 \operatorname{eval}_{\sigma}(G)(t)=
 \begin{cases}
  a,&\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inl}a),\\
  \operatorname{eval}_{\sigma}(R_i(u)),
    &\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(\operatorname{inr}u),\\
  \sigma(v_i(z)),&\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}z.
 \end{cases}
\]
Outside \emph{all} complete-support frontier cones,
the output is the original source word and
its corresponding evaluation. The old support-root
set is preserved, repeated occurrences of each
genuine cone variable are mapped uniformly, and
for every actual corrected-starred input the
decoded word is packaged on \emph{exactly}
the corrected $Q$ tree (under the already
verified geometric input certificate).
The ten proposed source-facing endpoints
\lean{ForwardQFullFrontierThreeWay.ConeData.*}
include local root alignment, support-tree
equality, inside/outside symbols, all three
constructor equations, fibre uniformity and
the two evaluation equations.
All ten new source-facing declarations passed the Lean build,
finite regressions and 966-declaration transitive axiom audit
in merged PR~\#239.

\paragraph{Remaining author-facing proof gap.}
This is a \emph{conditional typed assembly}, not
yet the paper's particular $g_w$. It neither
establishes the prescription of $R_i$ by visible
old ancestors nor constructs $v_i$ from the
canonical local root addresses with all required
source-domain bounds. The printed word-restriction
and signature transport must also be checked
for positions not lying in outside-cut frontier cones.
Finally, the Definition~24 simplicity witness
$A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$ must be transported by

No axiom-free statement yet derives smoothness

\paragraph{Concrete forward-frontier visible-ancestor auxiliary decoder $R_i$.} \ok
The full-frontier three-way interface now isolates the
remaining local auxiliary-decoder obligation in Lemma~27.
For a genuine \emph{forward-sorted} minimal outside-cut
frontier $f_i$ of $T$, put
\[
 V_i=\{v\in\operatorname{Vars}(g):
                 \operatorname{root}(v)\sqsubset f_i\}.
\]
Here $V_i$ is taken as the actual finite list of
interior roots that are \emph{support predecessors}
of $f_i$; it is not an unrestricted collection of
all source variables. The corrected forward-skew
rank lemma supplies
\[
 h_T(f_i)\le h_T(t_0)+1=m+1,\qquad
 |V_i|\le h_T(f_i)\le m+1.
\]
Each member of $V_i$ is lifted from its node to
the \emph{actual bounded source-variable subtype},
preserving its exact root. The decoder
\[
 R_i:\{0,\ldots,m\}\longrightarrow
       \Lambda\sqcup\operatorname{Vars}(g)
\]
is chosen to cover $V_i$ when nonempty; if $V_i$
is empty, all auxiliary symbols decode to one
prescribed fallback letter $a\in\Lambda$.
Its variable outputs always satisfy
\[
 R_i(j)=v\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
       \operatorname{root}(v)\sqsubset f_i.
\]
Thus for any position $t$ in the full cone of
$f_i$ one obtains $\operatorname{root}(v)\sqsubseteq t$,
the exact auxiliary root-prefix condition
needed by the corrected three-way word constructor.

The proposed source-facing Lean module
\lean{ForwardQVisibleAncestorDecoder}
formalizes (i) the forward frontier height bound,
(ii) exact source-variable witnesses for all visible
ancestor nodes, (iii) a typed decoder covering the
visible roots, and (iv) auxiliary root-prefix alignment.
It also masks raw symbols \emph{outside their own}
full frontier cones to a fallback original letter.
Those symbols are ignored by the global selector,
so this change preserves \emph{all} original raw
symbols inside the actual cones, while making
the generic decoder's all-position alignment
hypotheses well typed. Given an explicit further
root-alignment certificate only for the
\emph{genuine transported cone variables},
the source-facing constructor
\lean{ForwardQVisibleAncestorDecoder.coneData}
supplies a valid
\lean{ForwardQFullFrontierThreeWay.ConeData}
with no independent auxiliary-alignment hypothesis.
The evaluated output of the resulting starred
$Q$-word agrees with the original three-case
raw-word formula on every selected frontier cone.

This is a stronger local decoder repair than simply
postulating an arbitrary $R_i$: the target, numeric
bound, root type and surjectivity onto visible roots
are all derived from the \emph{actual corrected frame}.
All fourteen new source-facing theorem endpoints passed the
Lean build, finite regressions and 980-declaration
transitive no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#240.

\paragraph{Not yet repaired.}
The genuine cone variables require a
\emph{source-facing} transport from the local
variable-root addresses to old source roots;
the cone-variable alignment certificate remains
an explicit hypothesis. The printed source
word restriction, the canonical
$A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$ to $R$ transport, the
corrected starred-domain Ramsey step and
all mixed-product span/refinement and
colouring-induction claims remain \todo.
The constructed $R_i$ cannot by itself
establish Lemma~27 or the main theorem.


\paragraph{A necessary-and-sufficient \emph{visibility} condition for genuine cone-variable transport.} \ok
The corrected $Q$ support has variable roots exactly
$\operatorname{Int}(S)$, whereas every outside-cut
frontier $f_i$ lies outside the inclusive forward cut.
No old source variable root is a descendant of $f_i$.
Suppose a genuine local cone-variable symbol
$z$ occurs at a position $t$ extending $f_i$,
and is translated to an actual old source
variable $v_i(z)$. Root alignment forces
\[
   \operatorname{root}(v_i(z))\sqsubseteq t.
\]
Two prefixes of $t$ are comparable. Since the
reverse comparison
$f_i\sqsubseteq\operatorname{root}(v_i(z))$
is impossible, the output root must be a
\emph{strict} predecessor:
\[
  \operatorname{root}(v_i(z))\sqsubset f_i.
\]
Because every source variable root belongs to the
ambient complete support $T$, it necessarily
lies in the already verified list $V_i$
of visible source-variable predecessors of
$f_i$. Conversely every root in $V_i$
prefixes every position in the full cone of
$f_i$. Thus, for arbitrary raw local cone words,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\forall i,t,z\
 [f_i\sqsubseteq t\ \land\
   \operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}(z)
  \Longrightarrow
   \operatorname{root}(v_i(z))\sqsubseteq t]\\
{}\iff\;&
\forall i,t,z\
 [f_i\sqsubseteq t\ \land\
   \operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}(z)
  \Longrightarrow
   \operatorname{root}(v_i(z))\in V_i].
\end{aligned}
\]
In particular $V_i=\varnothing$ forces the
absence of genuine cone-variable occurrences
in the $f_i$ cone whenever the decoded output
is a valid variable word on the existing $Q$
support. This is a source-facing obstruction,
not merely a convenient sufficient hypothesis.
The proposed module
\lean{ForwardQConeVariableVisibility}
formalizes the old source-root membership in
$T$ and its support, strict comparability
of co-cone prefixes, both directions of
the visibility equivalence, and the
empty-visible-cone impossibility. It also
specializes the verified $R_i$ constructor to
an explicitly chosen visible-root certificate,
replacing the arbitrary geometric alignment
premise by an exact finite condition at the
occurrences. All eight source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 988-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#241.

\paragraph{Correction to the genuine cone-root prescription.}
The canonical forward complete-support cone
projection $P_i$ takes local tail nodes into
the \emph{descendant} cone of $f_i$.
In general the image $P_i(r)$ of a local
cone-variable root $r$ is \emph{not} an allowable
variable root of $g_w$, whose support is
$\operatorname{Int}(S)$ and hence entirely
outside every frontier descendant cone.
Therefore an argument that simply defines
$v_i(r):=P_i(r)$ is ill typed as a translation
into the old source-variable subtype.
The repair must explicitly select an old
variable root in $V_i$ for every genuine
variable \emph{occurring} in the cone, and
separately check any required injectivity,
independence and mixed-word span properties.
The visibility bound $|V_i|\le m+1$
may force identifications of distinct local
variables; arbitrary collapsing cannot be
used to infer the unmodified Ramsey span.
This observation does not refute Lemma~27's
existence conclusion, but identifies a
substantive remaining condition on any
faithful $g_w$ construction.


\paragraph{Exact visible-root capacity and obstruction to injective transport of too many cone variables.} \ok
The finite visible-root chain $V_i$ in the previous
paragraph is a filtered sublist of the genuinely
duplicate-free ambient complete support $T$;
hence it is itself duplicate-free and
$|V_i|\le h_T(f_i)\le m+1$.
The map from positions in $V_i$ to the actual
source-variable subtype is injective:
two different positions yield different
source variable roots, and each actual old
source variable whose root lies in $V_i$
is represented by exactly one position.
In particular the already checked decoder
$R_i:\{0,\ldots,m\}\to
\Lambda\sqcup\operatorname{Vars}(g)$
is \emph{surjective onto the actual visible old
source variables} (not just onto indices of
an abstract ancestor list).

There is also a quantitative obstruction.
Suppose a single complete-support frontier cone
contains $m+2$ distinct local cone-variable
symbols $z_0,\ldots,z_{m+1}$, all of which
actually occur at some position of the cone.
If every occurrence is translated to an old
source-variable root with valid prefix alignment,
the previous visibility equivalence forces
$v_i(z_j)$ to have its root in $V_i$ for
every $j$. An injective variable-name
transport would therefore embed a set of
$m+2$ names into at most $m+1$ possible
old variable roots, an impossibility:
\[
 \Bigl(|\kappa|=m+2,\quad
       \forall z\in\kappa\ \exists t\in
       \mathrm{Cone}(f_i)\
       \operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}z\Bigr)
 \Longrightarrow
 \neg\operatorname{Injective}(v_i)
\]
whenever all occurrences obey the legal
old-source-root alignment. This is a
conditional obstruction to \emph{injective}
transport of independent local variable names,
not a refutation of Lemma~27's Ramsey
existence statement.

The module
\lean{ForwardQVisibleRootCapacity}
makes both results source-facing: it proves
the actual finite list has no duplicates,
gives faithful position-to-source-variable
indices, shows that every genuinely visible
source variable occurs in the image of the
concrete $R_i$, and derives the strict
$m+2$ versus $m+1$ non-injectivity by
finite cardinal arithmetic. It also derives
the obstruction directly from a family of
raw symbols, each occurring within the
actual corrected frontier cone.
All nine source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 997-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#242.

\paragraph{Consequence for the Ramsey span.}
Because the two-letter span-refinement
theorem preserves variable fibres, the
original proof cannot \emph{silently}
assume distinct local cone variables
become distinct old output variable
names when their number exceeds the
visible-root capacity. Identifying names
may be mathematically legitimate, but
one must then calculate the new evaluated
span and show it has enough independent
substitutions for the promised Ramsey
step. A proof with a smaller number of
genuinely occurring local cone variables
or a different source-variable interface
might avoid the obstruction. No such
span-preserving construction has yet
been established.


\paragraph{An explicit (generally collapsing) cone-root assignment: exact existence criterion.} \ok
The visibility characterization can also be
used constructively. Because the final source
cut $t_0$ is interior, it carries a genuine
old source variable that may serve as an
\emph{unused fallback name} in a frontier
with no visible old root.
For every frontier with $V_i\ne\varnothing$,
choose the \emph{first} visible old variable
$v_i^0$. Define the map on local cone-variable
names by
\[
  \operatorname{rootChoice}_i(z)=
  \begin{cases}
     v_i^0,&V_i\ne\varnothing,\\
     v_{t_0},&V_i=\varnothing.
  \end{cases}
\]
This choice is root-aligned at every
\emph{genuine occurrence} inside a frontier
cone exactly if no cone with
$V_i=\varnothing$ contains a genuine local
variable occurrence. More precisely,
for arbitrary raw local cone words and
the actual source-variable subtype,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\exists\operatorname{rootChoice}
 \quad\forall i,t,z\
  [f_i\sqsubseteq t\
  \land\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}(z)
  \Longrightarrow
  \operatorname{root}(\operatorname{rootChoice}_i(z))
       \sqsubseteq t]
\\
&\quad\Longleftrightarrow\
 \forall i,t,z\
 [f_i\sqsubseteq t\
 \land\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}(z)
 \Longrightarrow V_i\ne\varnothing].
\end{aligned}
\]
Sufficiency uses the first visible root; necessity
is the verified source-facing obstruction
from the previous paragraphs. The resulting
concrete $R_i$ and $\operatorname{rootChoice}_i$
can be fed into the full-frontier three-way
decoder to obtain an actual root-valid
starred $Q$ word under exactly that
nonemptiness condition.
This is a positive algebraic construction,
but does \emph{not} by itself solve the
Ramsey step: for fixed $i$,
\[
 \operatorname{rootChoice}_i(z)
 =\operatorname{rootChoice}_i(z')
 \qquad\text{for all }z,z',
\]
so even when many visible roots are available,
the canonical first-root choice collapses all
local variable names in a cone.
A proof of the original mixed-word span must
supply a more faithful transport when possible,
or prove the smaller span suffices.

The source-facing proposed Lean module
\lean{ForwardQCanonicalConeRootChoice}
packages the genuine source-cut fallback,
the first-visible-root selection, the
necessary-and-sufficient existence theorem,
and the conditional actual starred $Q$ support.
All six source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1003-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#243. The paper's specific raw local
variable words, the canonical $A/B_1/B_2$
simplicity transport, the numerical induction,
and the full dual-tree theorem remain \todo.

\paragraph{Reading genuine mixed-product coordinate words at canonical projected positions.} \ok
The conditional full-frontier three-way $Q$ word
was previously assembled from externally supplied
raw local symbol maps $\operatorname{raw}_i$.
To connect it to the actual mixed-product
\emph{element} $x$, a new source-facing
construction reads the real coordinate word
$x_i:b^{<k-(m+1)}\to\Lambda$ using the
corrected canonical forward projection
\[
 P_i(z)=I_T\bigl(I_T^{-1}(f_i)^\frown z\bigr),
 \quad i\in D_0\cup D_2,\quad z\in b^{<k-(m+1)}.
\]
Here $f_i$ is the exact sorted complete-support
frontier at index $i$, including ordinary
$D_0$ coordinates, not just selected
$D_2$ markers. The verified forward inverse
address equation gives
\[
 I_T^{-1}(P_i(z))=I_T^{-1}(f_i)^\frown z.
\]
Removing the inverse frontier prefix
recovers the original local tail:
\[
 \operatorname{tail}_i(P_i(z))
 =\operatorname{drop}_{|I_T^{-1}(f_i)|}
     (I_T^{-1}(P_i(z)))=z.
\]
An interpolated letter map
$L_i:b^{<n}\to\Lambda$ reads
$x_i(\operatorname{tail}_i(t))$ when $t$
lies in $T$ and the recovered tail
has the safe height; otherwise it
uses a fixed fallback letter. Thus
\[
 \boxed{L_i(P_i(z))=x_i(z)}
 \qquad (i\in D_0\cup D_2).
\]
Inserting these concrete sampled
letters into the original-letter
case of the checked full-frontier
decoder produces a starred output
word on $Q(x)$ with unchanged old
source variable roots, whose symbol
at each actual $P_i(z)$ is exactly
the genuine input coordinate letter.

The proposed module
\lean{ForwardQActualCoordinateWord}
formalizes the forward projection,
its inverse, tail extraction,
actual coordinate-letter recovery
and the resulting corrected starred
$Q$ word. All seven new source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1010-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#244.

\paragraph{Scope and remaining gap.}
The actual mixed-product \emph{element}
$x$ carries ordinary alphabet words.
The new raw maps use only the
original-letter constructor; they
do not yet handle the source's
enlarged auxiliary alphabet or a
\emph{variable} mixed-product word
with genuine local cone variables.
The particular $g_w$ substitution,
simplicity-to-smoothness transport,
evaluated span and recursive Ramsey
bounds all remain independent
obligations. The displayed
coordinate identity is an exact
source-facing bridge, not the
full Lemma~27 conclusion.


\paragraph{Exact projected-word observables for an arbitrary smoothness partition.} \ok
A further source-facing bridge connects the now-verified
actual coordinate-letter identity with the full
$\Gamma_1/\Gamma_2$ split of Definition~21,
\emph{without imposing the overly strong special
case $\Gamma_2=D_2$}. Fix a corrected-starred
mixed-product input $x$ and an actual split
$\gamma_1:D_2\to\{\texttt{true},\texttt{false}\}$.
Call $(i,z)$ an \emph{observable coordinate position}
when (i) $i\in D_0$, or (ii) $i\in\Gamma_1$,
or (iii) $i\in\Gamma_2$ and $z$ lies
\emph{outside} the inclusive local cone of
the selected marked point $s_i(x)$.
For any two actual corrected-starred inputs
$x,y$ with $x\sim_{\gamma_1}y$,
the genuine global $Q$-word constructed from
actual input coordinate letters satisfies
\[
    G_x(P_i(z))=G_y(P_i(z))
    \quad\text{at every observable }(i,z),
\]
where $P_i$ uses the fixed corrected
forward-complete support $T$ and is therefore
independent of both mixed-product inputs.
For $i\in\Gamma_2$ the actual projected
marked point is fixed:
\[
    P_i(s_i(x))=P_i(s_i(y)).
\]
Nothing here asserts equality of the
\emph{entire} $Q$ support trees:
$\Gamma_1$ marked points may move
even while their whole coordinate words
remain unchanged.

Conversely these observable $Q$-symbol
equalities and the $\Gamma_2$ projected
marked-point equalities \emph{recover exactly}
the original Definition~21 smoothness
comparison on the corrected $D_0/D_2$
mixed-product space.
The only additional geometric argument is
that a fixed-frontier canonical projection
$P_i$ reflects equality of local marked tails:
the genuine forward inverse gives
\[
 I_T^{-1}(P_i(s))=I_T^{-1}(f_i)^\frown s,
 \qquad
 P_i(s)=P_i(t)\Longrightarrow s=t
\]
by cancelling the common inverse-address
prefix. The constructed observable relation
is therefore neither a weakening nor an
unjustified strengthening of the
published $\Gamma_1/\Gamma_2$ condition.

The proposed module
\lean{ForwardQProjectedSmoothObservables}
contains this fixed-frontier injectivity
lemma, the observable-position cases,
the actual $Q$-letter equalities and the
bidirectional characterization
\[
 x\sim_{\gamma_1}y
 \Longleftrightarrow
 \text{observable projected $Q$ letters
 and $\Gamma_2$ projected points agree}.
\]
All eleven source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1021-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#245. To avoid a
universe-polymorphism ambiguity from the empty
raw cone-variable type, the formal observation
record stores the actual letters $x_i(z)$;
a separate theorem identifies their equality
with equality of the genuine decoded $Q$
symbols at $P_i(z)$. This is a representation
choice only, not a change to the observable
relation.
The remaining \emph{particular}
Lemma~27 colouring pullback must show
that the original simple colouring
depends only on these observables
\emph{after} transporting the canonical
Definition~24 signature $A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$
to the corrected literal markers $R$.
Equality of observables alone does not
supply this colouring-factorization
statement or the mixed-word span.

\paragraph{Every selected $D_2$ frontier has an available old source variable.} \ok
The visibility obstruction from the preceding
cone-root analysis is not equally severe
at all frontier coordinates.
The corrected source support is genuinely
rooted at an old interior variable
$r_0\in\operatorname{Int}(S)$.
For every literal corrected signature
marker $s\in R$, the already verified
root geometry shows $r_0\sqsubseteq s$.
The selected $D_2$ frontier $f_i$
extends exactly this literal marker.
Moreover $r_0$ lies \emph{inside}
the forward auxiliary initial segment
through $t_0$, whereas $f_i$ is
a minimal support vertex outside it.
Therefore $r_0\ne f_i$, giving
\[
    r_0\sqsubset f_i,
    \qquad i\in D_2.
\]
In particular $r_0$ is a genuine
old source-variable root \emph{in}
the per-frontier visible-root list:
\[
  r_0\in V_i,\qquad
  |V_i|\ge 1\quad(i\in D_2).
\]
This identifies a concrete auxiliary
label under the verified $R_i$ decoder
that returns the root variable for every
selected $D_2$ index. It also gives
a uniformly aligned possible
cone-variable root for \emph{any}
local occurrence in a selected $D_2$
frontier cone. No analogous statement
has been established for arbitrary
ordinary $D_0$ frontiers, which need
not be reached by one of the original
source signature markers.

Thus whenever an array of raw
three-constructor local words has
\emph{genuine cone-variable}
occurrences only at $D_2$ coordinates
(the $D_0$ coordinates use only
original and auxiliary letters),
the necessary-and-sufficient
visibility condition for existence
of a legal output root assignment
holds automatically, even if
some $D_0$ visible lists are empty.
The checked first-visible-root
constructor can therefore yield
an actual starred word on $Q(x)$
for \emph{every} corrected-starred
input $x$ under the explicit
ambient height bound.
This is a positive source-facing
conditional repair, but it may
collapse distinct local variable
names and hence proves no unchanged
mixed-word span.

The proposed
\lean{ForwardQBulletRootVisibility}
module verifies the genuine source-root
variable name, the strict $D_2$
frontier-prefix property, its membership
in the true $V_i$, nonemptiness and
decoder reachability, and the bullet-only
variable occurrence constructor.
All eight source-facing endpoints passed the Lean build,
finite regressions and 1029-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#246.
The original proof's particular local
variable words have not yet been
proved to obey the bullet-only
occurrence restriction, and the
canonical $A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$
simplicity transport is still open.

\paragraph{An explicit canonical quotient for all $\Gamma_1/\Gamma_2$ smoothness classes.} \ok
The preceding verified projected-letter criterion
can be presented as an actual finite record,
rather than a binary relation on inputs.
Fix a partition $D_2=\Gamma_1\dot\cup\Gamma_2$
and a fallback letter $a\in\Lambda$.
For any corrected mixed-product point $x$,
record the local masked coordinate letters
\[
 M_{\gamma_1,a}(x)_{i,z}=
 \begin{cases}
   x_i(z),&i\in D_0\cup\Gamma_1,\\
   a,&i\in\Gamma_2,\quad s_i(x)\sqsubseteq z,\\
   x_i(z),&i\in\Gamma_2,\quad
                      s_i(x)\not\sqsubseteq z,
 \end{cases}
\]
and the marked-point record
\[
 B_{\gamma_1}(x)_i=
  \begin{cases}
   \mathrm{none},&i\in\Gamma_1,\\
   \mathrm{some}(s_i(x)),&i\in\Gamma_2.
  \end{cases}
\]
There are no $D_1$ coordinates in the
particular corrected Lemma~27 frame.
The \emph{observable fingerprint} is
the typed pair $F_{\gamma_1,a}(x)=(M,B)$.
It retains \emph{exactly} the visible
local coordinate information, hiding the
letters inside $\Gamma_2$ marked cones
and hiding the movable marked points of
$\Gamma_1$. It is not a canonical
representative of a starred \emph{input}
in the style of the earlier all-$\Gamma_2$
normalization $N_a$; rather, it is a
canonical \emph{quotient record} and
does not require resetting any points.

The exact proposed source-facing equivalence is
\[
 \boxed{
 F_{\gamma_1,a}(x)=F_{\gamma_1,a}(y)
   \iff x\sim_{\gamma_1}y
 }
\]
for arbitrary corrected mixed-product
inputs $x,y$, not just those in the
starred subtype. Its proof handles the
plain word coordinates, all $\Gamma_1$
letters, $\Gamma_2$ local marked points
and equality of letters outside their
common marked cones. In view of the
actual $Q$ coordinate theorem, the
masked words can equivalently be read
as the \emph{observable portions} of
the original-letter $Q$ output at
the certified projected points $P_i(z)$.

A useful application is a complete
factorization criterion for a colouring
$d:W_*\to C$ on the actual corrected
starred subtype:
\[
 \begin{split}
 d\text{ is }\gamma_1\text{-smooth on }W_*
 \quad\iff\quad
 &\exists\widehat d:
       \mathrm{Fingerprint}_{\gamma_1,a}\to C\\
 &\forall x\in W_*,\
       d(x)=\widehat d(F_{\gamma_1,a}(x)).
 \end{split}
\]
The factor map may be chosen on
\emph{all} fingerprint values,
including those not attained by any
starred point: on attained values
choose a representative and evaluate
$d$ there, using smoothness to
show independence of the representative;
on unattained values use one specified
fallback colour. This proves a
total factorization theorem, not merely
a partial map on the image.

The proposed module
\lean{ForwardQObservableFingerprint}
constructs the mask and point record,
proves the equivalence with Definition~21,
and gives this exact smooth-colouring
factorization. All ten source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1031-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in PR~\#247.
The remaining Lemma~27 task is to
show the \emph{particular} colouring
obtained from a Definition~24
$A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$ simplicity witness
factors through \emph{this explicit record},
after the unresolved canonical
$A$-to-literal-$R$ transport.
No assertion of such a factorization,
or of a span-preserving variable-word
construction, is included here.

\paragraph{Actual mixed-product span evaluations at the certified $Q$ projections.} \ok
Having verified the literal original-letter
construction
\[
 G_x(P_i(z))=\operatorname{inl}(x_i(z))
\]
for each genuine corrected-starred
mixed-product input $x$, we can
connect the statement to a \emph{genuine}
variable mixed-product word rather than
an arbitrary array of coordinate letters.
Suppose $f$ is a variable word of
the corrected $D_0/D_2$ product
whose coordinate supports are
vector-$q$-complete and suppose
$x\in W_*\cap\operatorname{Span}(f)$.
For every actual sorted coordinate $i$,
the span premise supplies an actual
assignment
\[
 \sigma_i:\operatorname{Vars}(f_i)\to\Lambda,
 \qquad
 x_i(z)=\operatorname{eval}_{\sigma_i}(f_i)(z).
\]
The proposed source-facing theorem then proves
\[
 G_x(P_i(z))
   =\operatorname{inl}\Bigl(
     \operatorname{eval}_{\sigma_i}(f_i)(z)
     \Bigr).
\]
A dependent-choice argument selects
\emph{all} assignments $\sigma_i$
simultaneously, thereby certifying
the independent coordinate evaluations
in the actual mixed product.
Under \emph{any} assignment $\tau$ to
the old source variables of the
starred output, evaluation at these
projected positions has the same
local value because $G_x(P_i(z))$
is a literal constant there.
Outside all complete-support
frontier cones $G_x$ remains the
old source word.

The new proposed module
\lean{ForwardQMixedSpanEvaluation}
connects the real
\lean{MixedProduct.VariableWord.span}
membership to the actual $Q$
symbol/evaluation identities,
constructs simultaneous
coordinate substitutions, and
proves the corresponding
outside-cone equality.
All five source-facing endpoints passed the Lean build,
finite regressions and the 1044-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#248.

\paragraph{Important span limitation.}
The family of component assignments
$(\sigma_i)_i$ is a dependent product
over \emph{all local mixed-product
variables}. It is \emph{not} a
single assignment to the old
source-variable subtype carried
by the actual $Q$ output word.
The latter still has only the
original roots in $\operatorname{Int}(S)$,
with the previously verified visible
root capacity restrictions.
Consequently equality of
\emph{coordinatewise} evaluations
does not by itself establish
an image embedding of the whole
mixed-product variable-word span,
the required Hales--Jewett
independence, or the numerical
Ramsey recursion of Lemma~27.
Those remain \todo.

\paragraph{A canonical observable fingerprint indexed by literal corrected signature markers $R$.} \ok
The previous quotient theorem takes an
arbitrary Boolean partition predicate
$\gamma_1$ on the selected $D_2$
coordinate indices. The corrected
geometric construction has already
proved a bijection between the
\emph{literal} finite marker set
$R$ and those actual forward-sorted
indices. Consequently a subset
$B_2\subseteq R$ determines the
specific partition
\[
 \Gamma_2=\{\iota(s):s\in B_2\},
 \qquad
 \Gamma_1=\{\iota(s):s\in R\setminus B_2\}.
\]
The proposed source-facing
marker fingerprint $F_{R,B_2,a}$
is the checked $\Gamma$-fingerprint
with this exact marker-induced
predicate. It satisfies the
following \emph{literal indexed}
identities for every marker
$s\in R$ and local tail $z$:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
s\notin B_2:&
 M_{\iota(s),z}=x_{\iota(s)}(z),\quad
 \mathrm{point}_{\iota(s)}=\mathrm{none};\\[1mm]
s\in B_2,\ s_{\iota(s)}(x)\sqsubseteq z:&
 M_{\iota(s),z}=a,\quad
 \mathrm{point}_{\iota(s)}
   =\mathrm{some}(s_{\iota(s)}(x));\\[1mm]
s\in B_2,\ s_{\iota(s)}(x)\not\sqsubseteq z:&
 M_{\iota(s),z}=x_{\iota(s)}(z),\quad
 \mathrm{point}_{\iota(s)}
   =\mathrm{some}(s_{\iota(s)}(x)).
\end{array}
\]
These are the actual source
$R$ markers, not an assumed
identification with Definition~24's
abstract canonical terminal set $A$.
The proposed Lean

proves each of the two

\[
  F_{R,B_2,a}(x)=F_{R,B_2,a}(y)
  \iff x\sim_{\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2}y.
\]
For any colouring of the genuine
corrected-starred subtype it also

to Definition~21 smoothness
for this exact marker partition.

finite regressions and the 1051-declaration transitive

\paragraph{Remaining $A\to R$ transport in Lemma~27.}
This removes the arbitrary

It still does \emph{not}
