\paragraph{Forced identification of occurring local variables strictly shrinks the evaluated span.} \ok
The source-facing visible-root bound
already proves that if one corrected
full frontier cone contains $m+2$
distinct local variable names, each
actually occurring there, then no
root-aligned assignment can map them
\emph{injectively} to the at most
$m+1$ visible old source-variable
roots. This has an additional
evaluated-span consequence over
any alphabet with at least two
distinct letters.

Let $w:\mathcal I\to\Lambda\sqcup\kappa$
be a local variable word, and suppose
$u\ne v$ both actually occur:
$w(s)=\operatorname{inr}(u)$ and
$w(t)=\operatorname{inr}(v)$.
If a substitution
$\rho:\kappa\to\Lambda\sqcup\nu$
identifies their symbols
$\rho(u)=\rho(v)$, then
\[
  \operatorname{Span}(\rho_*w)
       \subseteq\operatorname{Span}(w)
  \quad\text{but}\quad
  \operatorname{Span}(w)
       \not\subseteq\operatorname{Span}(\rho_*w).
\]
Indeed, choose two different letters
$a,b$ and evaluate $u$ as $a$,
$v$ as $b$. That original evaluation
takes different values at $s$ and $t$,
while every evaluation of $\rho_*w$
takes the same value there.
The fact that the local names
\emph{actually occur} is essential;
identifying unused names creates
no such witness.

The proposed Lean lemma
\lean{ForwardQVariableSpanCollapse.original\_span\_not\_subset\_of\_collapsed\_span}
constructs this explicit witness,
while
\lean{collapsed\_span\_properly\_contained}
pairs it with the previously verified
general span-substitution inclusion.
The specialized source-facing theorem
\lean{actual\_frontier\_full\_span\_not\_preserved}
then combines the exact corrected
$Q$ visible-root capacity
with local raw cone words over the
enlarged auxiliary alphabet:
if all $m+2$ distinct local names
occur in the given frontier cone
and the old-source-root transport
is geometrically aligned, the
evaluated span of that full raw
cone word cannot be recovered
from its root-translated word.
The three source-facing declarations passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1054-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#249.

\paragraph{Consequence for Lemma~27.}
It is \emph{not sufficient} to
repair the local $g_w$ construction
by choosing arbitrary old source
roots from the visible chain,
nor even by showing the resulting
word is geometrically valid.
When that choice collapses
two distinct \emph{occurring}
local variable symbols, it
strictly loses some evaluated
assignments in the corresponding
raw local cone word.
The remaining proof must
control which local names
actually occur, their compatible
assignment to old variables, or
replace the full local span
by a justified smaller one.
This does not refute the full
dual-tree Ramsey theorem; it
identifies precisely why a
naive rooted-word repair cannot
by itself justify the claimed
Hales--Jewett / mixed-word
span induction.


\paragraph{An exact full-local-span criterion: injectivity is required only on \emph{occurring} variable names.} \ok
The preceding obstruction to collapsed variable names
has a useful converse. Let
$w:\mathcal I\to\Lambda\sqcup\kappa$ be any local
raw word and let $\rho:\kappa\to\nu$ rename
its variables, without introducing new
constants. Write
\[
 \operatorname{Occ}(w)=
 \{u\in\kappa:\exists t\in\mathcal I,\
                 w(t)=\operatorname{inr}(u)\}.
\]
If $\Lambda$ is nonempty and
$\rho|_{\operatorname{Occ}(w)}$ is injective,
then the \emph{entire evaluated span}
is preserved:
\[
  \operatorname{Span}(\rho_*w)
   =\operatorname{Span}(w).
\]
Indeed, for each evaluation $\sigma:\kappa\to\Lambda$
choose a target evaluation $\tau:\nu\to\Lambda$
on the image of occurring names by
$\tau(\rho(u))=\sigma(u)$.
Injectivity on $\operatorname{Occ}(w)$ makes
this well defined. Assign a fixed fallback
letter on the remaining names. Every
position of $w$ evaluates identically
under these two assignments.
The reverse inclusion follows from
the already verified substitution
lemma.

Conversely, if $\Lambda$ has at least
two distinct letters, equality of the
full spans forces the restriction
$\rho|_{\operatorname{Occ}(w)}$ to be
injective: otherwise the explicit
two-letter counterassignment from
the preceding paragraph witnesses
strict span loss. Thus the precise
algebraic equivalence is
\[
 \boxed{
   \operatorname{Span}(\rho_*w)
      =\operatorname{Span}(w)
   \iff
   \rho|_{\operatorname{Occ}(w)}
      \text{ is injective}
 }
 \qquad (|\Lambda|\ge 2).
\]
This does \emph{not} require
injectivity on unused names.

For each genuine forward-sorted
complete-support frontier $i$,
the actual target names of the
source-facing variable transport
lie in the old source-variable
subtype. The same criterion
applies to its raw local cone
word over the enlarged alphabet
$\Lambda\sqcup\{0,\ldots,m\}$
and any map of genuine cone names
to those old source variables.
The resulting statement expresses
the remaining local span obligation
as \emph{precisely} injectivity
on the set of names that occur,
rather than an unsupported demand
that all declared local-variable
names remain independent.
This is consistent with the
verified $m+2$ versus $m+1$
obstruction: too many occurring
names make the required
injectivity impossible.

The proposed module
\lean{ForwardQSpanExactInjectivity}
provides the positive evaluation
extension construction, the
two-letter converse, their exact
equivalence and the actual
full-frontier specialization.
All four source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1058-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#251.
No claim is made that the paper's
specific raw local words satisfy
the injectivity condition or
that their global mixed-product
Ramsey span is now established.


\paragraph{Exact visible-root capacity for preserving the full evaluated span of one corrected $Q$ frontier cone.} \ok
The preceding exact span criterion
can be combined with the explicit
finite chain of old source-variable
roots visible at a corrected
outside-cut frontier $f_i$.
Write
\[
 V_i=\{v\in\operatorname{Vars}(g):
               \operatorname{root}(v)\sqsubset f_i\},
 \qquad |V_i|\le m+1.
\]
Because the stored $V_i$ list is
duplicate-free and each listed root
lifts to a unique actual old
source-variable subtype, any
local variable set of size
$q\le|V_i|$ admits a \emph{concrete
injective} root assignment:
send the ordered names
$z\in\operatorname{Fin}(q)$
to the first $q$ members of
the visible-root list.
Every selected target is
a strict predecessor of
$f_i$, and hence automatically
root-aligned with all its
occurrences in the full cone.
The map is genuinely injective,
so the preceding full-span
criterion proves equality
of evaluated raw-word spans
for \emph{every} local word
with these $q$ variable names.

Conversely, suppose all $q$
local names genuinely occur
at positions inside that
full frontier cone.
Any assignment to old
source roots that lies in
$V_i$ and preserves the
\emph{full} local two-letter
evaluated span must be
injective on all $q$ names,
by the proved exact span
criterion. Finite cardinality
therefore forces $q\le|V_i|$.
The sharpened positive/negative
statement is
\[
 \boxed{
  \begin{gathered}
   \exists\,\rho_i:
     \operatorname{Fin}(q)\to
       \operatorname{Vars}(g),\\
   \operatorname{root}(\rho_i(z))\in V_i
        \ \text{for all }z,\quad
   \operatorname{Span}((\rho_i)_*w_i)
        =\operatorname{Span}(w_i)
  \end{gathered}
  \iff q\le|V_i|
 }
\]
provided all $q$ symbols
occur inside the cone
and the enlarged local alphabet
has two distinguishable letters.
The \emph{positive} direction
does not require this
occurrence hypothesis.

The proposed source-facing
\lean{ForwardQVisibleSpanCapacityIff}
module builds the literal
first-$q$ root assignment,
proves its visibility,
prefix alignment and
injectivity, obtains the
positive full-span equality,
and establishes the converse
cardinality bound and
existence/capacity equivalence.
All six source-facing theorem endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1064-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit on PR~\#252.

\paragraph{What this does not prove.}
This is a sharp \emph{single
frontier} capacity statement.
The paper's specific raw local
variable words must still
be shown to have at most
$|V_i|$ independent
\emph{occurring} names for
their respective frontiers,
or have a correctly justified
smaller span. Even if each
cone has enough visible roots,
the corresponding assignments
may overlap across different
$D_0/D_2$ cones and need not
combine into one substitution
of the global old source
variables with the independence
claimed in the mixed-product
Ramsey recursion.
The canonical address transport
from Definition~24's
$A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$ into
literal corrected $R$ and
the particular simple-colouring
factorization remain separate
open obligations.


\paragraph{The missing \emph{global} root-allocation condition is a finite matching problem.} \todo
Even after the single-frontier
capacity equivalence, it would
be incorrect to infer preservation
of the \emph{whole independent
mixed-product} coordinate span
from the bounds
$q_i\le|V_i|$ for each
frontier separately. Two
different cones may offer
overlapping old source-variable
roots.

For a fixed finite family of
raw local variable words, let
\[
  E_i=\{(i,z):\text{$z$ actually
                   occurs in the
                   $i$-th cone}\},
  \qquad E=\mathop{\dot\bigcup}_i E_i.
\]
Distinct tags in $E$ represent
\emph{independent} local variable
assignments in the original
mixed product, even if their
un-tagged local names coincide.
An old source-variable root
$v$ is an allowable translated
name for $(i,z)$ exactly when
$\operatorname{root}(v)\in V_i$.
If a root translation is
required to preserve the entire
\emph{independent cone-coordinate
evaluated span} over a two-letter
alphabet, its restriction to
$E$ must be injective,
by the exact occurring-variable
span criterion. Conversely,
an injective allowable assignment
extends all independent local
evaluations by a partial inverse.

Thus the existence of a
root-aligned, cone-coordinate
full-span-preserving \emph{joint}
translation is equivalent to a
system of distinct representatives
for the allowed root sets
$(V_i)_{(i,z)\in E}$.
By the finite marriage theorem
the exact combinatorial criterion is
\[
 \boxed{
   \forall X\subseteq E,\qquad
   |X|\le
   \left|\bigcup_{(i,z)\in X} V_i\right|.
 }
 \tag{Hall--Q}
\]
The single-frontier inequalities
$q_i\le |V_i|$ cover only
the special tests $X\subseteq E_i$.
They do \emph{not} imply the
Hall--Q inequalities for sets
meeting several frontier cones.
For example, at the abstract
root-allocation level, two
frontiers may each admit
exactly one local variable
and have the same singleton
allowed root set. Their
individual inequalities hold,
but there cannot be two
different assigned target names.
This example is a warning
about the matching argument,
\emph{not} a claimed counterexample
under all parameters of
the printed Lemma~27.

\paragraph{Limit of the Hall--Q reduction.}
The equivalence above concerns
the \emph{coordinatewise} span
on the disjoint union of
frontier-cone positions.
A full starred output
$Q$-word also retains the old
source word outside the cones,
and its canonical support
and admissible orders require
independent checks. Hence the
Hall--Q condition, even if
verified for the particular
local words of Lemma~27,
would not alone prove the
exact image/refinement, simplicity
or Ramsey-induction clauses
of that lemma. Conversely, a
Ramsey proof may legitimately
work with a selected smaller
mixed-word span that requires
no such injective translation;
it would then need a separate
formal definition and
justification of that span.

This is a \emph{mathematical
reduction and author-facing
TODO}, not a new Lean-verified
statement. To formalize it,
one should construct the
finite set of tagged
\emph{occurring} cone variables,
the family of allowable
old source-variable root sets,
and apply a finite Hall/matching
lemma. It is essential to
connect the proposed matching
to the \emph{actual}
three-constructor cone words
before using it to repair
Lemma~27.


\paragraph{A sharp \emph{global} Hall criterion for independent tagged cone-variable spans.} \ok
The verified one-frontier bound $q_i\le|V_i|$
is not sufficient to produce a \emph{single}
injective translation of all independent
variables in the mixed product. The
visible-root chains $V_i$ of different
frontiers can overlap: the same old source
variable may be a predecessor of many
distinct outside-cut cones.
The correct finite index set of variable
names for the independent-coordinate
span is the disjoint union
\[
 E=\mathop{\dot\bigcup}_{i\in D_0\cup D_2}
          \operatorname{Fin}(q_i),
\]
whose elements $(i,z)$ remember the
\emph{actual sorted frontier index} as well
as the local name. A variable tagged by
$i$ may be renamed only to an old
source variable in the checked list
$V_i$. Thus define the finite target
family
\[
 N(i,z)=\bigl\{v\in\operatorname{Vars}(g):
                  \operatorname{root}(v)\in V_i\bigr\}.
\]
The necessary-and-sufficient condition
for a globally \emph{injective}
root-aligned assignment
$\rho:E\to\operatorname{Vars}(g)$ is
\emph{Hall's condition}
\[
 \boxed{\quad
  |H|\le\Bigl|\bigcup_{(i,z)\in H}N(i,z)\Bigr|
  \quad\text{for every }H\subseteq E.
 \quad}
\]
The proposed source-facing Lean module
\lean{ForwardQGlobalHallSpan} applies the
existing Mathlib finite Hall marriage
theorem directly to the genuinely typed
old source-variable subtype, rather
than a hypothetical common set of local
variables. Every chosen root is shown to
be a strict ancestor of the corresponding
frontier, so root-prefix alignment follows
for \emph{every} occurrence inside its cone.

For the disjoint-coordinate raw word
\[
 W(i,t)=
 \begin{cases}
  \operatorname{inl}(a),
   &\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inl}(a),\\
  \operatorname{inr}(i,z),
   &\operatorname{raw}_i(t)=\operatorname{inr}(z),
 \end{cases}
\]
an injective Hall matching preserves its
\emph{entire evaluated independent
coordinate span}, by the verified
injective-on-occurrences criterion.
Conversely, if \emph{all tagged names}
actually occur at some position
\emph{inside their own frontier cones},
and the enlarged alphabet has two
distinct letters, any visible-root
translation preserving the full evaluated
span of $W$ must be injective on all
of $E$. Hall's inequalities are therefore
necessary. The intended exact statement
is
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \begin{gathered}
   \exists\rho:E\to\operatorname{Vars}(g)
   \ \bigl[\rho(i,z)\in N(i,z)\ \forall(i,z),\\
   \operatorname{Span}(\rho_*W)
          =\operatorname{Span}(W)\bigr]
 \end{gathered}
 \quad\iff\quad
 \text{Hall's condition on }(N(e))_{e\in E}.
 \quad}
\]
The positive direction does not require
every declared local variable name to
occur; the reverse direction does.
This is an exact finite \emph{algebraic}
bridge for a disjoint union of raw
local coordinate domains, not yet a
theorem asserting that all those positions
form a single legal \emph{paper} $g_w$
with the correct signature and
Hales--Jewett recursion.

All eight source-facing endpoints passed Lean compilation,
finite regressions and the 1072-declaration transitive
no-\texttt{sorryAx} audit in merged PR~\#253.

\paragraph{Remaining hypotheses to discharge from the printed Lemma~27.}
The local word construction must be
shown to have precisely the tagged
\emph{occurring} variable families
whose visible old-root options satisfy
Hall's inequalities; separate
$q_i\le|V_i|$ bounds alone do not
imply this. Even a Hall matching does
not by itself construct the paper's
literal raw cone words or show that
its Definition~24 simplicity
partition $A=B_1\dot\cup B_2$ transports
through the corrected literal
marker set $R$ to make the induced
colouring depend only on the already
verified $\Gamma_1/\Gamma_2$
observable fingerprint.
The printed-to-forward starred-domain
transport and numerical Ramsey
recursion also remain independent
obligations, not consequences of Hall.


\paragraph{Sharper Hall condition on the \emph{actually occurring} independent cone variables.} \ok
The preceding verified global Hall
criterion names \emph{all} declared
local variables and requires all
of them to occur when deriving
necessity from span preservation.
This formulation is useful but
stronger than needed for a
general variable word, since
unused local names do not affect
its evaluated span. Moreover
the paper's full-frontier $Q$
word never reads positions
\emph{outside the corresponding
frontier cone}. A source-facing
criterion should ignore both
kinds of irrelevant data.

Fix the actual corrected frame
$T$, the vector of local name
counts $(q_i)_i$, a fallback
letter $a$ in the enlarged
alphabet, and local raw words
$\operatorname{raw}_i$.
First form the cone-masked word
\[
 \widehat{\operatorname{raw}}_i(t)
 =
 \begin{cases}
   \operatorname{raw}_i(t),&
             f_i\sqsubseteq t,\\
   \operatorname{inl}(a),&
             f_i\not\sqsubseteq t.
 \end{cases}
\]
This agrees with the original
local symbols on \emph{every
position actually read} by the
full-frontier $Q$ constructor.
Define the tagged occurrence set
\[
 E_{\mathrm{occ}}=
  \left\{(i,z):
    \exists t,\,
       f_i\sqsubseteq t
       \ \land\
       \operatorname{raw}_i(t)=
                  \operatorname{inr}(z)
  \right\}.
\]
Thus local names that never
appear \emph{inside} their own
cone do not consume old-root
capacity. For each occurring
tag $(i,z)$, the allowable set
is exactly the old source
variables with roots in $V_i$.
The proposed strengthened Hall
condition is
\[
 \boxed{\quad
  |H|\le
   \left|\bigcup_{(i,z)\in H}V_i\right|
  \quad\text{for every }
    H\subseteq E_{\mathrm{occ}}.
 \quad}
\]

Mathlib's finite Hall theorem
gives a matching on this
\emph{occurring subtype} exactly
when the inequalities hold.
Such a matching can be extended
to a total assignment on all
declared tagged names using
the already verified genuine
source-cut variable as an

\paragraph{An explicit arithmetic model for Definition~24's boundary A.} \todo
The missing model-to-literal boundary correspondence
should not start from an unspecified ordering of
arbitrary support nodes. A natural explicit model is
the zero-based $b$-ary heap indexing of ambient words:
\[
  \operatorname{ind}(\varnothing)=0,\qquad
  \operatorname{ind}(s^\frown i)
    =b\,\operatorname{ind}(s)+1+i.
\]
For $b>0$ this strictly increases with every
ambient prefix extension, so the candidate
first-$l$ model interior
\[
 H_l=\{s:\operatorname{ind}(s)<l\}
\]
is prefix-closed. Its external immediate-child
boundary is
\[
 A_l=\{s^\frown i:s\in H_l,\ s^\frown i\notin H_l\}.
\]
The proposed \lean{ForwardModelBoundaryHeap}
module proves $A_l$ is a prefix antichain and
the exact necessary arithmetic range
\[
       s\in A_l\ \Longrightarrow\
       l\le\operatorname{ind}(s)\le bl.
\]
It also proves the heap child formula, strict
growth along children, and prefix closure of
$H_l$. Seven source-facing Lean endpoints
target 1191 on this independent branch,
pending CI and the transitive no-\texttt{sorryAx}
audit.

The next steps are to verify formally
that this numerical heap enumeration agrees
with the corrected \emph{forward} auxiliary
order on the homogeneous model, prove the
converse boundary characterization and unique
decoding, and then construct the direction-
preserving canonical map $A_l\cong R$.
None of these is justified by an arbitrary
finite-cardinality equivalence; the printed
REVERSE-lex order remains separately refuted.

\paragraph{Exact forward-heap model boundary interval.} \todo
For positive model interior size $l$, the proposed
forward breadth-first heap code gives an \emph{exact}
characterization of the external-child boundary:
\[
  s\in A_l
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  l\le \operatorname{ind}(s)\le bl.
\]
The forward direction follows from the already
verified external-child arithmetic bounds. For
the converse, an address in this interval is
nonempty and has a unique decomposition
$s=p^\frown i$. If $\operatorname{ind}(p)\ge l$,
then
$\operatorname{ind}(s)=b\operatorname{ind}(p)+1+i
 > bl$, a contradiction. Thus $p\in H_l$.

The new \lean{ForwardModelBoundaryInterval}
module formalizes this converse and the exact
equivalence as two Lean declarations. The
independent Python regression
\texttt{scripts/check-model-boundary-heap.py}
exhaustively checks the heap inverse, parent
prefix closure and boundary interval for
$b=1,\dots,4$, $l=1,\dots,29$.
The transitive no-\texttt{sorryAx} target is
1197, pending CI. The degenerate case $l=0$
is deliberately excluded: the numerical
interval contains the root while $A_0$ is empty.

The model arithmetic is not yet the required
canonical $A$-to-$R$ transport: heap indexing

\subsection{Lemma 28 and Theorem 25}
Lemma 28 iterates Lemma 27 and additionally needs explicit transport between a
model signature in $b^{<m}$ and the corresponding signature observable in the
current variable word.

In Theorem 25, after pulling the signature-coloring back along $Q$, the proof
invokes the induction hypothesis without showing that the new coloring is
simple.  Tree-only finite tests are positive: the modeled $Q$ is onto in the
tested $l=1,2$ cases and tested simple colorings remain simple.  This should be
isolated as a lemma.

\subsection{Appendix Remark 3(ii)}
The unrestricted union assertion is false for disjoint $L_1,L_2$.  It becomes
true when $L_1\cap L_2\ne\varnothing$, which is sufficient for the actual
application $L_j=\{a_j,a_{j+1}\}$.

partial induced colouring to a total smooth colouring of

$r$ equals exactly $P_{\iota(r)}(s_{\iota(r)})$:
every terminal belongs to a unique literal marker
$r'\in R$ and both $r,r'$ are first-exit boundaries
of the same corrected forward cut. The already checked
antichain theorem forces $r=r'$.
Thus the source-relative observable has the exact
value
\[
 I^R_{Q(w)}(r)=P_{\iota(r)}(s_{\iota(r)}(w))
\]
without an additional structural assumption.

This is sufficient to instantiate the conditional
simplicity-to-smoothness theorem with
$A=R$, $\Gamma_2=\iota(B_2)$ and the explicit
relative marker selector $I^R_U$.
For a colouring satisfying the \emph{R-relative}
Definition~24-style simplicity predicate on refinements
of the ambient word $f$, the resulting pullback
$c\circ Q$ is smooth and extends to a TOTAL smooth
colouring of the complete enlarged mixed-product
domain. No unproved marker transport remains in
this \emph{R-relative} statement.

The proposed \lean{ForwardQRelativeMarkerSelector}
module formalizes the unique-leaf principle,
the source-specific marker identity,
the resulting R-simple pullback and its
total smooth extension (four new source-facing
axiom endpoints, target 1188, CI pending).

\textbf{Critical distinction.} In the paper
Definition~24 uses the \emph{canonical model boundary}
$A$ of the first $l$ ambient nodes, not the source
signature marker family $R$. The relative terminal
map $I^R$ need not coincide with the paper's
$I_U(a)$ for arbitrary output $U$, and an arbitrary
published-simple colouring is \emph{not yet}
proved R-simple. The remaining task is the actual
canonical direction/address transport
$A\cong R$ and compatibility of their
successor cones. The mixed Ramsey refinement
and induction remain separate obligations.

\paragraph{Stable direction-order blocks in the forward model.} \todo
The explicit forward heap child formula establishes
two further facts needed by the canonical
$A$-to-$R$ transport: if $i<j$ then the
$i$-child precedes the $j$-child of any parent;
and if $\operatorname{ind}(s)<\operatorname{ind}(t)$
then \emph{all} children of $s$ precede
\emph{all} children of $t$ regardless of their
directions. The second follows from
\[
 b(\operatorname{ind}(s)+1)
 \le b\operatorname{ind}(t),
\qquad 1+i\le b.
\]
The proposed
\lean{ForwardModelHeapChildOrder}
module proves the strict direction ordering,
the full parent block inequality and their
combined lexicographic form. These are three
new source-facing declarations (stacked audit
target 1200), pending GitHub Actions.

These statements are arithmetic preliminaries:
the prefix/direction-respecting equivalence
between model $A$ and corrected literal $R$
and its agreement with the canonical
semi-complete-tree embedding remain open.

\paragraph{Finite-height compatibility of the model A boundary.} \todo
The published Definition~24 constructs its

For $b>0$, each heap child strictly raises
the index by at least one. A direct induction
on the direction list yields
\[
 |s|\le \operatorname{ind}(s).
\]
Hence each node of $H_l$ belongs to $b^{<n}$
when $l\le n$, and every external model

             bl<n.
\]
These sufficient bounds make precise when

The canonical model $A$-to-literal-$R$

\paragraph{Equal-level forward lex and heap-index comparison.} \todo
The previously checked stable child blocks
also give a useful model-side comparison
for whole words. If two heap-addressed
parent nodes have strictly ordered indices,
then appending any two direction sequences
of \emph{equal length} preserves that
strict inequality. An induction on the
length of the direction sequences shows

The proposed

\paragraph{Exact heap bands and corrected auxiliary-order monotonicity.} \todo
Define the first and last model heap indices
of length-$n$ homogeneous nodes by
\[
m_0=M_0=0,\qquad
m_{n+1}=bm_n+1,\qquad
M_{n+1}=bM_n+b.
\]
An induction over fold-left heap coding proves
$m_n\le\operatorname{ind}(s)\le M_n$
whenever $|s|=n$, and a second induction
establishes $M_n+1=m_{n+1}$.
For $b>0$, the minima $m_n$ increase strictly,
so
\[
 |s|<|t|\ \Longrightarrow\
 \operatorname{ind}(s)<\operatorname{ind}(t).
\]
Together with equal-level forward lex monotonicity,
this proves the complete \emph{forward implication}
\[
 s\mathrel{\mathrm{ForwardAux}}t
 \Longrightarrow\
 \operatorname{ind}(s)\le\operatorname{ind}(t).
\]
The proposed
\lean{ForwardModelHeapLevels} module exports
six new source-facing statements (stacked
transitive audit target 1213, CI pending).

The converse still requires address-code
injectivity, especially to eliminate

then the model $A$ boundary can be compared
with the actual canonical address system

The original reverse-lex statement is
not used.

\subsection{Lemma 28 and Theorem 25}
Lemma 28 iterates Lemma 27 and additionally needs explicit transport between a
model signature in $b^{<m}$ and the corresponding signature observable in the
current variable word.

In Theorem 25, after pulling the signature-coloring back along $Q$, the proof
invokes the induction hypothesis without showing that the new coloring is
simple.  Tree-only finite tests are positive: the modeled $Q$ is onto in the
tested $l=1,2$ cases and tested simple colorings remain simple.  This should be
isolated as a lemma.

\subsection{Appendix Remark 3(ii)}
The unrestricted union assertion is false for disjoint $L_1,L_2$.  It becomes
true when $L_1\cap L_2\ne\varnothing$, which is sufficient for the actual
application $L_j=\{a_j,a_{j+1}\}$.

\paragraph{Lean status.} \ok
Definition~30's exact assignment relation is formalized as
\lean{DualTree.StarInsensitivity.StarRelated}, and
\lean{DualTree.StarInsensitivity.starRelated_iff} identifies it with the
three printed clauses.  The unrestricted printed claim is refuted by the
kernel-checked
\lean{DualTree.StarInsensitivity.disjoint_union_failure}.
The corrected overlap statement actually used in the proof is
\lean{DualTree.StarInsensitivity.starInsensitive_union_of_overlap}.
Its proof normalizes both assignments through a common bridge letter in
$L_1\cap L_2$, making the suppressed transitivity explicit.

\subsection{Lemma 31}
The rendered PDF removes two OCR false alarms: Lemma~31 assumes $k\ge2$, and
the proof of Theorem~8 later assumes $k\ge3$.  Two genuine one-step index
errors remain in Lemma~31 itself.

First, at construction stage $i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}$ the new wildcard occupies
the coordinate interval $[q_{i-1},q_i)$ inserted after the first $i-1$
variables.  It is therefore the wildcard of $v_{i-1}$, not $v_i$.  The error
is forced already at the final stage: since
\[
d_m=m+n_0-q_m=m,
\]
the word $w_m$ has precisely the variables
$v_0,\ldots,v_{m-1}$, so a wildcard $v_m$ cannot occur.

Second, with
\[
d_{i-1}=(i-1)+n_0-q_{i-1}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
p_i=q_i-q_{i-1},
\]
the set
\[
J=\{0,\ldots,d_{i-1}-1\}
   \setminus [i-1,i-1+p_i)
\]
has cardinality
\[
\begin{aligned}
|J|
 &=d_{i-1}-p_i \\
 &=(i-1)+n_0-q_i \\
 &=(i-1)+f_1(k,\kappa,m-i,m,r),
\end{aligned}
\]
using equation~(8).  Thus the printed $m-i-1$ must be $m-i$.  With this
correction the exponent in (10) matches equation~(9), so the pigeonhole bound
has the intended value $p_i$.

Both corrections are local to the indexing of the Shelah-style construction;
nothing else in the mechanism needs to change at this point.  A source-faithful
Lean formalization of Lemma~31 remains \todo.

\subsection{Theorem 29}
The product theorem is only asserted to follow by modifying the preceding
argument.  It remains unverified.


\paragraph{Independent stretched and arbitrary-source Q regression.} \todo
Two deterministic Python programs now exercise the globally
forward-auxiliary corrected reconstruction against independently
generated complete skew supports. The first,
\texttt{scripts/check-q-stretched-forward.py}, constructs
forward-initial source interiors inside genuinely stretched
complete supports and checks $912$ valid reconstructed
instances across $34$ distinct supports, using the real
minimal frontier, marker injection, sorted $D_2$ order and
canonical support-address projection.

The independent
\texttt{scripts/check-q-arbitrary-source.py} enumerates
\emph{every} source subset of four binary stretched height-$4$
complete supports and one ternary stretched height-$3$
complete support, discarding precisely those that fail
semi-complete skewness, interior skewness or the early-cut
hypotheses. Across $139{,}259$ nonempty candidates, it finds
$538$ admissible sources and checks $4{,}292$ reconstructed
Q supports against the full executable semi-complete skew
predicate. For every exceptional source terminal at rank
$h_S(t_0)+1$, it additionally asserts the first outside-cut
boundary has ambient length strictly greater than $|t_0|$.
Both programs use fixed seeds and assert their expected
coverage counts.

The arbitrary-subtree checker also tests the following
stronger emph{new/new rank transport candidate}, for every
ordered pair of projected markers in each reconstructed Q:
\[
 h_Q(P_i)<h_Q(P_j)
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 h_T(P_i)<h_T(P_j).
\]
It held throughout all $4{,}292$ sampled Q instances,
including unequal local $D_2$ tail lengths. If established
in Lean, this would make new/new skew clause~(iii) a
direct consequence of the enclosing complete support's
already verified strict intrinsic-rank/ambient-length
separation. The rank-transport implication itself is
presently computational evidence only, not a lemma.

Neither exhaustive small tests nor random stretched tests
constitute a proof for arbitrary parameters; they also do
not verify construction of the word $g_w$, smoothness, or
the final colouring pullback. They serve as adversarial
regressions while the corresponding Lean theorems are
reviewed and the repaired Q construction is completed.

\section{Computational evidence retained}
Before Lean formalization, exhaustive finite programs established or tested:
\begin{itemize}
\item failures of the printed auxiliary order and Remark 2, with zero failures
of the corresponding local tests after the forward-lex change;
\item a separate forward-lex counterexample to Remark 1's extension claim;
\item collapse of refinement for $|\Lambda|=1$ and no nonsyntactic inclusion
in the tested two-letter instances;
\item the height-one Corollary 22 counterexample;
\item the small counterexample to the displayed PTGR/TGR inequality;
\item 1244 finite overlap tests supporting the repaired Remark 3(ii);
\item onto behavior of the Theorem 25 signature map and simplicity preservation
in the tested tree-only cases.
\item The executable regression \texttt{scripts/check-skew-meet.py}
exhaustively enumerates all skew subsets (under both the printed
reverse-lex order and the candidate forward-lex order) in the binary
height-$3$ and height-$4$ trees and the ternary height-$2$ tree.
It found, respectively, $25$, $201$, and $7$ skew trees in each order
convention, and all were closed under ambient meets; of these,
$17$, $114$, and $5$ were complete.  These tests corroborate the
proposed meet-closure lemma for complete skew supports but are
\emph{not} a formal proof for arbitrary height or branching number.
\end{itemize}

\section{Formalization order}
\begin{enumerate}
\item finite homogeneous trees, prefix and the two candidate auxiliary orders;
\item skew, complete skew and semi-complete trees, followed by Remarks 1--2;
\item variable words, evaluation, syntactic substitution and span inclusion;
\item the $|\Lambda|\ge2$ substitution/refinement lemma and the unary
counterexample;
\item starred insensitivity and the Appendix argument;
\item mixed products and Theorem 13;
\item signatures and a repaired Lemma 27;
\item Lemma 28 and Theorem 25;
\item Theorem 3 and finally the product theorem.
\end{enumerate}

Qualitative statements take priority over the large recursive bounds until the
underlying constructions are type-correct.
